Two things must be proved rather than assumed: first, that selecting a preimage of $[1/[U:V]] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ along inflation produces an *actual, unique* class $u_{U/V} \in H^2(U/(V \cap U), C^V)$ rather than a fallback value of Hilbert's choice operator; second, that pulling back an arbitrary (not necessarily normal) subgroup $S \le U/(V \cap U)$ to an open intermediate subgroup $W \le U$ converts index scaling in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ into all three Tate hypotheses on $S$, and then kills $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C)$ for every $r \ge 3$ by dividing the inflated fundamental class in the continuous degree-two group.

**Examples to keep in view.** Let $G = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ act trivially on $C = \mathbb Z$, with open subgroups $U_n = n\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ ($n \ge 1$) and invariant isomorphisms $\operatorname{inv}_{U_n} : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Z) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ normalized so that the carry cocycle $c_m(i, j) = \lfloor(i + j)/m\rfloor$ on $\widehat{\mathbb Z}/m\widehat{\mathbb Z} \simeq C_m$ has inflated invariant $[1/m] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. At the quadratic quotient $U_1/U_2 \simeq C_2$, the unique class with invariant $[1/2]$ is $[c_2]$, and the nonzero degree-four cup square $[c_2 \smile c_2] \in H^4(C_2, \mathbb Z)$ becomes an explicit coboundary once inflated to $U_1/U_4 \simeq C_4$, where $[\operatorname{inf} c_2] = 2[c_4]$ halves the period. Along the subgroup chain $1 < C_2 < C_4 < C_{12}$ inside $U_1/U_{12} \simeq C_{12}$, restricting $[c_{12}]$ scales the open-preimage invariant from $[1/12]$ through $[1/4]$ and $[1/2]$ to $0$, matching exact orders $12, 4, 2, 1$; in a non-abelian quotient $U/V \simeq S_3$, a non-normal reflection subgroup $S = \langle(12)\rangle$ still contains $V$ normally inside its preimage $W = \pi^{-1}(S)$. Dropping $H^1(V, C) = 0$ (trivial $\mathbb F_2$ on $C_4 \twoheadrightarrow C_2$), dropping injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}$ ($C = \mathbb Z^2$ projected to the first summand), or dropping prime-factor surjectivity ($C = \mathbb Z[1/3]$ at $C_{12}$) breaks uniqueness, cyclicity, or existence of the fundamental class.

**Definitions.** Throughout this subsection $G$ denotes a compact, totally disconnected topological group (in particular, every profinite group has this form, and every open subgroup $U \le G$ is closed and compact of finite index). For a commutative ring $k$, a representation $A$ of $G$ over $k$ is *discrete* (`IsDiscreteRep A`) if the stabilizer of every $a \in A$ is an open subgroup of $G$; equivalently, every $a \in A$ is fixed by some open normal subgroup of $G$, and the map $G \to A$, $g \mapsto g a$, is locally constant (`DiscreteModules/IsDiscreteRepIffForallExistsOpenNormalSubgroup.lean:105–145,263–325`) [Lean, read]. Let $C_{\mathrm{cts}}^n(G, A)$ (`locallyConstantCochains A n`) be the $k$-submodule of inhomogeneous cochains $f : (\operatorname{Fin} n \to G) \to A$ that are locally constant on $G^n$ (`ContinuousCochains.lean:13–21`). Because $A$ is discrete and group multiplication is continuous, the standard inhomogeneous differential
$$
(df)(g_0, \ldots, g_n)
= g_0\,f(g_1, \ldots, g_n)
+ \sum_{j=0}^{n-1} (-1)^{j+1} f(g_0, \ldots, g_j g_{j+1}, \ldots, g_n)
+ (-1)^{n+1} f(g_0, \ldots, g_{n-1})
$$
preserves local constancy (`isLocallyConstant_d`, `ContinuousCochains.lean:34–82`), forming a cochain subcomplex `cochainsComplex A` of Mathlib's `inhomogeneousCochains A` whose $n$-th cohomology we write as $H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A)$ (`cohomology A n`, `:96–128`) [Lean, read]. Inclusion of locally constant cochains induces a natural comparison map (`cohomologyToGroupCohomology A n`)
$$
\kappa_{G,A}^n : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A) \longrightarrow H^n(G, A),
$$
which is an isomorphism (`cohomologyIsoGroupCohomology A n`) whenever $G$ carries the discrete topology, since every function on $G^n$ is then locally constant (`ContinuousCochains/Functoriality.lean:130–156`) [Lean, read]. Along any continuous group homomorphism $\varphi : H \to G$ and $H$-equivariant linear map $\alpha : \operatorname{res}_\varphi A \to B$ between discrete representations, pullback of cochains `(f ↦ α ∘ f ∘ φⁿ)` defines `cohomologyMapAlong` (`CohomologyFunctoriality.lean:63–171`), specializing to restriction $\operatorname{res}^G_H : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A) \to H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(H, A|_H)$ (`cohomologyRes`, `:178–203`), relative open-subgroup restriction $\operatorname{res}^U_V : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, A|_U) \to H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, A|_V)$ for open $V \le U \le G$ (`cohomologyResLE`, `ClassFormationDefs.lean:24–52`, with relative index written $[U : V] := |(U : \operatorname{Subgroup} G) / (V \cap U)|$, i.e. `V.relIndex U` in Lean), and inflation $\operatorname{inf}^G_{G/N} : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(G/N, A^N) \to H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A)$ for any closed normal subgroup $N \trianglelefteq G$ (`cohomologyInfl`, `CohomologyFunctoriality.lean:210–228`); all of these commute with $\kappa^n$ (`CohomologyFunctoriality/Naturality.lean:50–71`), and topological group isomorphisms paired with bijective module maps induce isomorphisms on $H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}$ (`isIso_cohomologyMapAlong_of_mulEquiv`, `ClassFormations/IsIsoCohomologyMapAlongResSubgroupOfHom.lean:83–91`) [Lean, read].

Define the *rational circle* (`QZ`, `TorsionDualDefs.lean:8–36`) by
$$
\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z := \operatorname{AddCircle}(1 : \mathbb Q),
$$
equipped with the discrete topology and quotient homomorphism $q \mapsto [q]$ (`QZ.mk : ℚ →+ QZ`), so that $[q] = 0$ if and only if $q \in \mathbb Z$ (`mk_eq_zero_iff`, `:16–19`) [Lean, read]. For every integer $n \ge 1$, $[1/n]$ has exact additive order $n$, and an element $u \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ satisfies $n u = 0$ if and only if $u = [m/n]$ for some integer $0 \le m < n$ (`AddCircle.addOrderOf_period_div` and `AddCircle.nsmul_eq_zero_iff` in `Mathlib/Topology/Instances/AddCircle/Defs.lean:576–632`, packaged as `QZ.addOrderOf_mk_one_div` and `QZ.nsmul_eq_zero_iff` in `IsIsoCohomologyMapAlongResSubgroupOfHom.lean:177–188`) [Lean, read].

Let $C$ be a discrete $k$-representation of $G$, and write $H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C) := H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U)$ (`H2Open C U`, `ClassFormationDefs.lean:21`).
1. A *pre-class formation* on $C$ (`PreClassFormation C`, `ClassFormationDefs.lean:54–63`) consists of:
   - an additive *invariant map* $\operatorname{inv}_U : H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ for every open subgroup $U \le G$;
   - first-cohomology vanishing $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U) = 0$ (`isZero_H1_open_subgroups U`) for every open subgroup $U \le G$;
   - the *restriction scaling axiom* (`inv_res`): for all open subgroups $V \le U \le G$ and every $x \in H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C)$,
     $$
     \operatorname{inv}_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x) = [U : V] \cdot \operatorname{inv}_U(x).
     $$
2. A *class formation* on $C$ (`ClassFormation C`, `ClassFormationDefs.lean:65–68`) is a pre-class formation in which $\operatorname{inv}_U : H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is **bijective** for every open subgroup $U \le G$ (`inv_bijective`).
3. Given a set of primes $P$, a *class formation at $P$* (`ClassFormationAt P C`, `ClassFormationDefs.lean:69–76`) is a pre-class formation in which every $\operatorname{inv}_U$ is injective (`inv_injective`) and its image contains the $p$-primary torsion $(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[p^\infty]$ for every prime $p \in P$ (`primaryComponent_le_range`). Every `ClassFormation C` induces `ClassFormationAt Set.univ C` (`ClassFormation.toAt`, `:78–83`) [Lean, read].
4. For open subgroups $U, V \le G$ such that $V_U := V \cap U$ (`(V : Subgroup G).subgroupOf U`) is normal in $U$, the archive defines (`ClassFormationDefs.lean:86–94`, `ClassFormations/Finite.lean:225–230`)
   $$
   u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}
   := \operatorname{invFun}\!\Bigl(y \longmapsto \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}(y)\bigr)\Bigr)\!\left(\left[\frac{1}{[U : V]}\right]\right)
   \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U/V_U, C^{V_U}),
   $$
   called `F.fundamentalClass U V`, and sets (`F.groupFundamentalClass U V`)
   $$
   u_{U/V} := \kappa_{U/V_U, C^{V_U}}^2(u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}) \in H^2(U/V_U, C^{V_U}).
   $$
   Here `Function.invFun f z` chooses an $x$ with $f(x) = z$ when such an $x$ exists. Otherwise it uses `Classical.arbitrary`, a witness selected via `Classical.choice`. The instance `Nonempty` is a proposition, and its zero witness supplies only inhabitance. Lemma 3 proves existence and uniqueness from the formation axioms. See `Mathlib/Logic/Function/Basic.lean:471–481`. [Lean, read] [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-114316-fundamental-classes-and-the-three-subgroup-hypotheses-in-a-class-formation)

**Lemma 1 (continuous inflation–restriction in degrees one and two).** Let $G$ be compact and totally disconnected, $N \trianglelefteq G$ a closed normal subgroup, and $A$ a discrete $k$-representation of $G$.
1. The sequence
   $$
   0 \longrightarrow H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G/N, A^N)
   \xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{inf}_1\;} H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A)
   \xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{res}_1\;} H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(N, A|_N)
   $$
   is exact.
2. If $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(N, A|_N) = 0$, then the degree-two sequence
   $$
   0 \longrightarrow H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(G/N, A^N)
   \xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{inf}_2\;} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A)
   \xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{res}_2\;} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(N, A|_N)
   $$
   is also exact. In particular, for any open subgroups $V \le U \le G$ with $V_U = V \cap U \trianglelefteq U$ and $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, A|_V) = 0$, the inflation map $\operatorname{inf}_2 : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U/V_U, A^{V_U}) \to H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, A|_U)$ is injective and its image is precisely $\ker(\operatorname{res}^U_V : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, A|_U) \to H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, A|_V))$.

*Proof.* In every positive degree $n + 1$, the composite $N \hookrightarrow G \twoheadrightarrow G/N$ factors through the trivial one-element group $P$, whose inhomogeneous $(n+1)$-cocycles are coboundaries of constant $n$-cochains; functoriality therefore gives $\operatorname{res}_{n+1} \circ \operatorname{inf}_{n+1} = 0$ (`cohomologyInfl_comp_cohomologyRes`, `InflationRestriction/DegreeOne.lean:81–104`).

For (1), every $0$-cochain on $\operatorname{Fin} 0 \to G$ is a function on a one-point space and is automatically locally constant (`zero_cochain_mem_locallyConstant`, `DegreeOne.lean:45–48`). Consequently a locally constant $1$-cocycle on $G$ is a coboundary in `cochainsComplex A` if and only if it is a coboundary in `inhomogeneousCochains A`, so $\kappa_{G,A}^1 : H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A) \to H^1(G, A)$ is injective (`cohomologyToGroupCohomology_one_injective`, `:50–64`). On abstract group cohomology, if a $1$-cocycle $\bar f : G/N \to A^N$ inflates to $g \mapsto g y - y$ for some $y \in A$, evaluating on $s \in N$ gives $0 = \bar f(1) = s y - y$, so $y \in A^N$ and $\bar f = d y$ on $G/N$ (`Mono (H1InfRes A N).f`, `Mathlib/RepresentationTheory/Homological/GroupCohomology/Functoriality.lean:379–389`). Since $\kappa^1$ commutes with inflation and is injective on $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G/N, A^N)$ and $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, A)$, $\operatorname{inf}_1$ is injective (`cohomologyInfl_one_injective`, `DegreeOne.lean:124–130`). Next let $[Z] \in \ker(\operatorname{res}_1)$, represented by a locally constant $1$-cocycle $Z : G \to A$ whose restriction to $N$ equals $s \mapsto s w - w$ for some $w \in A$. Set $Z'(g) := Z(g) - (g w - w)$, which is cohomologous to $Z$ in `cochainsComplex A` and vanishes on $N$. For $g \in G$ and $s \in N$, the $1$-cocycle rule $Z'(g s) = g Z'(s) + Z'(g) = Z'(g)$ makes $Z'$ constant on left cosets of $N$, while $Z'(s g) = s Z'(g) + Z'(s) = s Z'(g)$ paired with $s g = g(g^{-1} s g)$ and $g^{-1} s g \in N$ gives $s Z'(g) = Z'(g(g^{-1} s g)) = Z'(g)$, so $Z'$ takes values in $A^N$ (`H1InfRes_exact`, `Functoriality.lean:394–425`). Because the quotient map $\pi : G \to G/N$ of a topological group is open (`QuotientGroup.isOpenMap_coe`), the descended map $\bar Z : G/N \to A^N$ satisfies $\bar Z \circ \pi = Z'$ and is locally constant on $G/N$ (`isLocallyConstant_of_comp_quotient`, `DegreeOne.lean:134–137`), providing $[\bar Z] \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G/N, A^N)$ with $\operatorname{inf}_1([\bar Z]) = [Z]$ (`exists_cohomologyInfl_one_eq`, `:139–197`; `infResShortComplex_zero`, `InflationRestriction/Higher.lean:97–103`).

For (2), embed $A$ into the discrete coinduced module $M := \operatorname{LocallyConstant}(G, A)$ (`discreteCoind k G A`, with action $(g \cdot \varphi)(x) = \varphi(x g)$ and unit $j(a)(x) = x a$) and set $Q := M/j(A)$ (`coindCokernel A`), obtaining a $k$-linearly split short exact sequence $0 \to A \xrightarrow{j} M \to Q \to 0$ of discrete $G$-modules (`CoinducedModules.lean:18–168`). On compact $G$, the cochain contracting homotopy
$$
(h f)(g_0, \ldots, g_{n-1})(x) := f(x, g_0, \ldots, g_{n-1})(1)
$$
preserves local constancy: for fixed $(g_0, \ldots, g_{n-1})$ and $x \in G$, local constancy of $f$ supplies open neighborhoods around each $(x, g_0, \ldots, g_{n-1})$, and compactness of $G$ extracts a finite subcover on which $(g'_0, \ldots, g'_{n-1}) \mapsto (h f)(g'_0, \ldots, g'_{n-1})$ is locally constant (`eventually_forall_cons_eq`, `CoinducedModules/Acyclic.lean:29–70`). Direct expansion of $(d h + h d) f$ cancels all face contractions except the initial term, giving $d h + h d = \mathrm{id}$ and hence $H^{n+1}_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, M) = 0$ for every $n \ge 0$ (`isZero_cohomology_discreteCoind_succ`, `Acyclic.lean:72–157`). To identify $M|_N$ and $M^N$, the archive constructs a continuous coset section $\sigma : G/N \to G$ (`exists_continuous_section`, `InflationRestriction/Section.lean:142–168`): define `cosetSelectors N` (`:11–15`) as the family of closed subsets $T \subseteq G$ admitting a closed normal subgroup $K \trianglelefteq G$ ($K \le N$) such that $T K \subseteq T$, $T$ meets every coset of $N$, and $t, t' \in T$ in the same $N$-coset satisfy $t^{-1} t' \in K$. Nonempty chains ordered by reverse inclusion intersect inside `cosetSelectors N` by compactness of the closed fibres $T \cap \pi^{-1}(c)$ (`sInter_mem_cosetSelectors`, `:24–61`), so `zorn_superset` extracts a minimal closed transversal candidate $T$ with residual subgroup $K$ (`exists_closed_transversal`, `:64–71`). If some $x \in K$ satisfied $x \neq 1$, total disconnectedness would supply an open normal subgroup $U \trianglelefteq G$ with $x \notin U$, and choosing a set-theoretic representative map $r : G/(K U) \to G/U$ on the discrete quotient $G/(K U)$ would cut out the closed subset $T' := \{g \in T : g U = r(g K U)\} \in \operatorname{cosetSelectors}(N)$ with smaller residual group $K \cap U$ (`:80–125`); minimality forces $T \subseteq T'$, which on $t \in T$ and $t x \in T$ implies $t U = t x U$, contradicting $x \notin U$ (`:126–140`). Hence $K = \{1\}$, making $T \to G/N$ a continuous bijection from compact $T$ onto Hausdorff $G/N$, hence a homeomorphism $e : T \xrightarrow{\sim} G/N$ (`homeoOfEquivCompactToT2`, `:146–155`), and $\sigma(c) := e^{-1}(c)\,(e^{-1}(1))^{-1}$ is a continuous section with $\sigma(1) = 1$ (`:156–168`). Using $\sigma$, the map $(n, \bar g) \mapsto \sigma(\bar g)\,n$ is a homeomorphism $N \times (G/N) \xrightarrow{\sim} G$ that identifies $M|_N$ with $\operatorname{discreteCoind}_k^N(\operatorname{LocallyConstant}(G/N, A))$ (`discreteCoindResIso`, `InflationRestriction/Coinduced.lean:110–147`), while $N$-invariant locally constant functions on $G$ identify with $\operatorname{discreteCoind}_k^{G/N}(A)$ (`discreteCoindInvariantsIso`, `:183–242`). Because both $N$ (closed in compact $G$) and $G/N$ are compact and totally disconnected, $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(N, M|_N) = 0$ and $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G/N, M^N) = 0$ for every $r \ge 1$ (`Higher.lean:75–94`). Furthermore, in the long exact cohomology sequence on $N$ (`ContinuousCochains/LongExactSequence.lean:60–110`), the hypothesis $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(N, A|_N) = 0$ forces $0 \to A^N \to M^N \to Q^N \to 0$ to remain short exact (`invariants_shortExact_of_isZero_H1`, `Higher.lean:37–73,110–136`). All three connecting homomorphisms $\delta : H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(-, Q^{(-)}) \to H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(-, A^{(-)})$ are therefore isomorphisms, and naturality of $\delta$ (`CohomologyFunctoriality/QuotientToInvariantsFunctorPreservesZeroMorphism.lean:71–75,116–122`) yields an isomorphism of short complexes from the degree-one inflation–restriction sequence of $Q$ (exact by part (1)) to the degree-two inflation–restriction sequence of $A$ (`infResShortComplexIso`, `Higher.lean:168–199`).

Finally, when $V \le U \le G$ are open with $V_U \trianglelefteq U$, since $V \le U$ we have $V \cap U = V$, so the inclusion $V_U \hookrightarrow V$ is a topological group isomorphism (`subgroupOfEquivOfLe`, `IsIsoCohomologyMapAlongResSubgroupOfHom.lean:105–136`) identifying $H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(V_U, (A|_U)|_{V_U}) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, A|_V)$ compatibly with $\operatorname{res}^U_V$. Thus $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, A|_V) = 0$ implies $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(V_U, (A|_U)|_{V_U}) = 0$, and applying (2) to $(U, V_U, A|_U)$ gives injectivity of $\operatorname{inf}_2$ and $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}_2) = \ker(\operatorname{res}^U_V)$ (`cohomologyInfl_two_injective_of_open` and `mem_range_cohomologyInfl_two_iff`, `ClassFormations/Finite.lean:50–84`). [Lean, read]

**Lemma 2 (restriction kernel test, Shapiro corestriction, and invariant normalization).** Let $F$ be a pre-class formation on a discrete $k$-representation $C$ of $G$, and let $V \le U \le G$ be open subgroups.
1. (*Restriction kernel test.*) If $\operatorname{inv}_V$ is injective, then for every $x \in H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C)$,
   $$
   \operatorname{res}^U_V(x) = 0 \Longleftrightarrow [U : V] \cdot \operatorname{inv}_U(x) = 0 \quad\text{in }\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z.
   $$
2. (*Continuous Shapiro isomorphism and corestriction.*) For every $n \ge 0$, there is a corestriction map $\operatorname{cores}^U_V : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, C|_V) \to H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U)$ satisfying
   $$
   \operatorname{cores}^U_V\bigl(\operatorname{res}^U_V(x)\bigr) = [U : V] \cdot x
   \qquad \bigl(x \in H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U)\bigr).
   $$
3. (*Surjectivity of restriction and corestriction invariant law.*) If $F$ is a `ClassFormation C`, then $\operatorname{res}^U_V : H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C) \to H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(V, C)$ is surjective, and for every $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(V, C)$,
   $$
   \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{cores}^U_V(y)\bigr) = \operatorname{inv}_V(y).
   $$

*Proof.* (1) By the restriction scaling axiom, $\operatorname{inv}_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x) = [U : V] \cdot \operatorname{inv}_U(x)$; since $\operatorname{inv}_V$ is an injective group homomorphism, the left-hand side vanishes if and only if $\operatorname{res}^U_V(x) = 0$ (`PreClassFormation.resLE_eq_zero_iff`, `IsIsoCohomologyMapAlongResSubgroupOfHom.lean:195–201`).

(2) Fix the open subgroup $H := V_U \le U$ inside the compact group $U$; because $H$ is open in compact $U$, the coset space $U/H$ is finite and $[U : V] = |U/H| > 0$ (`relIndex_ne_zero`, `Finite.lean:87–93`). For a discrete $H$-module $B$, let $\operatorname{Coind}_H^U(B)$ (`shapiroCoind U H B`, `ShapiroDefs.lean:18–45`) be the discrete $U$-submodule of $\operatorname{LocallyConstant}(U, B)$ consisting of functions $\varphi$ satisfying $\varphi(h u) = h\,\varphi(u)$ for all $h \in H, u \in U$, with right-translation $U$-action $(u' \cdot \varphi)(u) = \varphi(u u')$. Evaluation at $1_U$ along $H \hookrightarrow U$ defines a cochain map from $C_{\mathrm{cts}}^\bullet(U, \operatorname{Coind}_H^U(B))$ to $C_{\mathrm{cts}}^\bullet(H, B)$ that is an isomorphism on $H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}$, transforms short exact sequences of discrete $H$-modules into short exact sequences of discrete $U$-modules (using a continuous coset section of $U \to H \backslash U$), and carries `discreteCoind` on $H$ to `discreteCoind` on $U$; dimension shifting from degree $0$ therefore makes the Shapiro comparison $S_{H,B}^n : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \operatorname{Coind}_H^U(B)) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(H, B)$ (`shapiroMap H B n`) an isomorphism in every degree $n \ge 0$ (`isIso_shapiroMap`, `Shapiro.lean:208–277`). When $B = (C|_U)|_H$, the unit $\eta : C|_U \to \operatorname{Coind}_H^U((C|_U)|_H)$ sending $c \mapsto (u \mapsto u c)$ factors restriction as $\operatorname{res}^U_H = S_{H,(C|_U)|_H}^n \circ H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \eta)$ (`cohomologyMap_coindUnit_comp_shapiroMap`, `Shapiro.lean:290–296`), while summing over left cosets $x H \in U/H$ via $\varphi \mapsto \sum_{x H} x\,\varphi(x^{-1})$ (independent of the coset representative because $(x h)\,\varphi((x h)^{-1}) = x h\,\varphi(h^{-1} x^{-1}) = x\,\varphi(x^{-1})$) defines a $U$-equivariant trace $\operatorname{tr} : \operatorname{Coind}_H^U((C|_U)|_H) \to C|_U$ (`coindTrace`, `ShapiroDefs.lean:57–95`) satisfying $(\operatorname{tr} \circ \eta)(c) = \sum_{x H} x(x^{-1} c) = [U : H]\,c$ (`coindUnit_comp_coindTrace`, `:97–106`). Defining $\operatorname{cores}^U_H := H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \operatorname{tr}) \circ (S_{H,(C|_U)|_H}^n)^{-1}$ (`cohomologyCores`, `ShapiroDefs.lean:158–161`) and transporting along $V_U \xrightarrow{\sim} V$ gives $\operatorname{cores}^U_V$ (`cohomologyCoresLE`, `CorestrictionDefs.lean:42–50`) with $\operatorname{cores}^U_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x) = [U : V]\,x$ (`cohomologyResLE_comp_cohomologyCoresLE`, `:63–69`).

(3) Because $[U : V] > 0$, every element $[a/b] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ equals $[U : V] \cdot [a/([U : V] b)]$, so multiplication by $[U : V]$ is surjective on $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ (`QZ.exists_nsmul_eq`, `Corestriction.lean:16–23`). Given $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(V, C)$, choose $q \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ with $[U : V] q = \operatorname{inv}_V(y)$, then by surjectivity of $\operatorname{inv}_U$ choose $x \in H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C)$ with $\operatorname{inv}_U(x) = q$. By scaling,
$$
\operatorname{inv}_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x) = [U : V]\,\operatorname{inv}_U(x) = [U : V] q = \operatorname{inv}_V(y),
$$
and injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}_V$ forces $\operatorname{res}^U_V(x) = y$, proving surjectivity of $\operatorname{res}^U_V$ (`ClassFormation.cohomologyResLE_surjective`, `Corestriction.lean:60–67`). Substituting $y = \operatorname{res}^U_V(x)$ and applying part (2) gives
$$
\operatorname{inv}_U(\operatorname{cores}^U_V y)
= \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{cores}^U_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x)\bigr)
= \operatorname{inv}_U([U : V] x)
= [U : V]\,\operatorname{inv}_U(x)
= \operatorname{inv}_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x)
= \operatorname{inv}_V(y),
$$
which is `ClassFormation.inv_cohomologyCoresLE` (`Corestriction.lean:68–72`). [Lean, read]

**Lemma 3 (existence, uniqueness, and prime-set synthesis of the finite-quotient fundamental class).** Let $V \le U \le G$ be open subgroups with $V_U = V \cap U \trianglelefteq U$.
1. Suppose $F$ is a `PreClassFormation C` such that $\operatorname{inv}_U$ and $\operatorname{inv}_V$ are injective and $[1/[U : V]] \in \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U)$. Then there exists a **unique** continuous cohomology class $u \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U/V_U, C^{V_U})$ satisfying
   $$
   \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}(u)\bigr) = \left[\frac{1}{[U : V]}\right].
   $$
   Consequently `F.fundamentalClass U V` equals this unique $u$, and `F.groupFundamentalClass U V` is the unique class in ordinary $H^2(U/V_U, C^{V_U})$ whose continuous inverse image inflates to invariant $[1/[U : V]]$.
2. In any `ClassFormation C`, the condition $[1/[U : V]] \in \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U)$ holds unconditionally for every open pair $V \le U$. More generally, in `ClassFormationAt P C`, $[1/[U : V]] \in \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U)$ holds whenever every prime divisor of $[U : V]$ belongs to $P$.
3. (*Tower compatibility.*) In a `ClassFormation C`, if $V \le W \le U$ are open subgroups with both $V_U$ and $W_U$ normal in $U$, then under inflation $U/V_U \twoheadrightarrow U/W_U$ and restriction $W/V_W \hookrightarrow U/V_U$,
   $$
   \operatorname{inf}^{U/V_U}_{U/W_U}(u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/W}) = [W : V] \cdot u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V},
   \qquad
   \operatorname{res}^{U/V_U}_{W/V_W}(u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}) = u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{W/V}
   $$
   (the latter after identifying $(C|_U)^{V_U}|_{W/V_W}$ with $(C|_W)^{V_W}$ along $W_U/V_U \simeq W/V_W$).

*Proof.* (1) Choose $x \in H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(U, C)$ with $\operatorname{inv}_U(x) = [1/[U : V]]$. In $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ (`QZ.nsmul_mk_one_div`) we have
$$
[U : V] \cdot \operatorname{inv}_U(x) = [U : V] \cdot \left[\frac{1}{[U : V]}\right] = [1] = 0.
$$
Because $\operatorname{inv}_V$ is injective, Lemma 2(1) gives $\operatorname{res}^U_V(x) = 0$. Since $F$ supplies $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, C|_V) = 0$, Lemma 1(2) yields $u \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U/V_U, C^{V_U})$ with $\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}(u) = x$, whence $\operatorname{inv}_U(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}(u)) = [1/[U : V]]$. For uniqueness, both $\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}$ (by Lemma 1(2)) and $\operatorname{inv}_U$ (by hypothesis) are injective, so their composite $u \mapsto \operatorname{inv}_U(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}(u))$ is injective! Because $[1/[U : V]]$ lies in the range of that injective map, `Function.invFun` returns its unique preimage $u$ (`inv_cohomologyInfl_fundamentalClass`, `ClassFormations/Finite.lean:121–134`). Moreover, since $V_U$ is open in $U$, the quotient $U/V_U$ carries the discrete topology (`discreteTopology_quot`, `ClassFormations/Quotient.lean:177–180`), so $\kappa_{U/V_U, C^{V_U}}^2$ is an isomorphism sending $u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}$ bijectively to `F.groupFundamentalClass U V` (`Finite.lean:225–243`).

(2) For `ClassFormation C`, $\operatorname{inv}_U$ is surjective (`exists_inv_eq`, `ClassFormations.lean:24–26`). For `ClassFormationAt P C`, let $A := \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U) \le \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$; we show by strong induction on $n \ge 1$ that if every prime divisor of $n$ lies in $P$, then every $q \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ with $n q = 0$ belongs to $A$ (`QZ.mem_of_nsmul_eq_zero`, `Quotient.lean:13–75`). For $n = 1$, $q = 0 \in A$. For $n > 1$, let $p := \operatorname{minFac}(n) \in P$. If $n$ is a power $p^k$, then $p^k q = 0$ puts $q$ in the $p$-primary component of $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, which is contained in $A$ by `primaryComponent_le_range`. Otherwise write $n = p^k m$ with $k \ge 1$, $m > 1$, and $\gcd(p^k, m) = 1$. Bézout's identity supplies $a, b \in \mathbb Z$ with $a m + b p^k = 1$, hence
$$
q = (a m + b p^k) q = a(m q) + b(p^k q).
$$
Here $p^k(m q) = n q = 0$ puts $m q \in (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[p^\infty] \subseteq A$, while $m(p^k q) = n q = 0$ and $m < n$ (with all prime factors of $m$ dividing $n$, hence in $P$) put $p^k q \in A$ by the induction hypothesis; additive closure of $A$ gives $q \in A$, and applying this to $q = [1/[U : V]]$ yields $[1/[U : V]] \in \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U)$ (`ClassFormationAt.exists_inv_eq_one_div`, `ClassFormations.lean:97–102`).

(3) Functoriality of inflation along $U \twoheadrightarrow U/V_U \twoheadrightarrow U/W_U$ gives $\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}(\operatorname{inf}^{U/V_U}_{U/W_U} u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/W}) = \operatorname{inf}^U_{U/W_U}(u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/W})$, whose $\operatorname{inv}_U$-image is
$$
\left[\frac{1}{[U : W]}\right]
= \left[\frac{[W : V]}{[U : W][W : V]}\right]
= [W : V] \cdot \left[\frac{1}{[U : V]}\right]
= \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}([W : V]\,u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V})\bigr),
$$
so injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}_U \circ \operatorname{inf}^U_{U/V_U}$ gives the first formula; the second is the normal-subgroup case $S = W_U/V_U$ of the Theorem below. [Lean, read]

**Lemma 4 (cyclic order and generation from an injective $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$-invariant).** Let $X, Y$ be additive commutative groups, $\Phi : X \to Y$ and $\operatorname{inv} : Y \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ injective group homomorphisms, $n \ge 1$ a positive integer, and $u \in X$. Suppose that
$$
\operatorname{inv}(\Phi(u)) = \left[\frac{1}{n}\right]
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\forall y \in X,\quad n \cdot \operatorname{inv}(\Phi(y)) = 0.
$$
Then $\operatorname{ord}(u) = n$ and every $y \in X$ equals $m u$ for some integer $m \in \mathbb Z$ (in fact some $0 \le m < n$).

*Proof.* Because $\operatorname{inv} \circ \Phi : X \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is an injective additive homomorphism, it preserves exact additive orders (`addOrderOf_injective`), so $\operatorname{ord}(u) = \operatorname{ord}([1/n]) = n$. Next let $y \in X$. Since $n \cdot \operatorname{inv}(\Phi(y)) = 0$ and $n > 0$, the torsion characterization on $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ (`QZ.nsmul_eq_zero_iff`) gives $0 \le m < n$ such that
$$
\operatorname{inv}(\Phi(y)) = \left[\frac{m}{n}\right] = m \cdot \left[\frac{1}{n}\right] = \operatorname{inv}\bigl(\Phi(m u)\bigr).
$$
Injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}$ and then of $\Phi$ forces $y = m u$ (`QZ.cyclic_of_injective`, `ClassFormations/Finite.lean:15–26`). [Lean, read]

**Theorem (all-subgroup Tate hypotheses and profinite vanishing in degrees $r \ge 3$).** Let $G$ be a compact, totally disconnected topological group and $C$ a discrete $k$-representation of $G$.
1. (*Production of the three subgroup hypotheses.*) Let $F$ be a `ClassFormation C` (or a `ClassFormationAt P C` and let $V \le U \le G$ be open subgroups with $V_U = V \cap U \trianglelefteq U$ such that every prime divisor of $[U : V]$ lies in $P$). Put $\Gamma := U/V_U$, $M := C^{V_U}$ as a $\Gamma$-representation, and $u := u_{U/V} \in H^2(\Gamma, M)$ (`F.groupFundamentalClass U V`). Then for **every** subgroup $S \le \Gamma$ (not assumed normal in $\Gamma$),
   $$
   H^1(S, M|_S) = 0,\qquad
   \operatorname{ord}(\operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S u) = |S|,\qquad
   \forall v \in H^2(S, M|_S)\ \exists m \in \mathbb Z\quad v = m\,\operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S u.
   $$
2. (*Handoff to the high-degree consumer.*) Specialize to $k = \mathbb Z$ (or `ULift ℤ`) and let $F$ be a full `ClassFormation C`. Then for every open subgroup $U \le G$ and every integer $r \ge 3$,
   $$
   H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U) = 0,
   \qquad\text{and in particular}\qquad
   H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, C) = 0.
   $$

*Proof.* **Part (1).** Fix any subgroup $S \le \Gamma = U/V_U$, let $\pi : U \twoheadrightarrow U/V_U$ be the quotient homomorphism, and pull $S$ back to a subgroup of $G$ via $U$ (`quotPreimageOpen hVU S`):
$$
W_U := \pi^{-1}(S) \le U,\qquad
W := (W_U \text{ pushed along } U \hookrightarrow G) \le G.
$$
Because $V_U = \ker(\pi) \le \pi^{-1}(S) = W_U$ is open in $U$, $W_U$ is a union of open cosets of $V_U$ and is therefore open in $U$; since $U$ is open in $G$, $W$ is an open subgroup of $G$ satisfying $V \le W \le U$ (`isOpen_quotPreimage` and `le_quotPreimageOpen`, `Quotient.lean:84–105`, `Finite.lean:94–112`). Notice that $S$ need *not* be normal in $\Gamma$, so $W_U$ need not be normal in $U$; nevertheless, restricting $\pi$ to $W$ produces a surjective homomorphism $\pi_S : W \twoheadrightarrow S$ (`quotPreimageHom S`, `Quotient.lean:113–122`) whose kernel is $V \cap W = V_W$ (`mem_ker_quotPreimageHom_iff`, `:124–131`). Being the kernel of a group homomorphism, $V_W$ **is** normal in $W$ (`normal_subgroupOf_quotPreimage`, `:134–138`), and the first isomorphism theorem gives a group isomorphism (`quotPreimageEquiv S`, `Quotient.lean:141–148`)
$$
e_S : W/V_W \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} S.
$$
Because $V$ is open in $G$, both $W/V_W$ and $\Gamma = U/V_U$ (hence its subspace $S$) carry the discrete topology (`discreteTopology_quot`, `:177–186`), so $e_S$ is a topological group isomorphism. Counting cosets along $V \le W \le U$ gives
$$
[W : V] = |W/V_W| = |S| > 0,\qquad
[U : V] = [U : W]\cdot [W : V] = [U : W]\cdot |S|
$$
(`relIndex_quotPreimage`, `Quotient.lean:150–154`; `relIndex_mul_relIndex`, `Finite.lean:194–198`). Note also that if every prime divisor of $[U : V]$ lies in $P$, the same holds for $[W : V] = |S|$.

Define the *quotient-subgroup inflation* map (`cohomologyInflQuot C S n`, `Quotient.lean:204–207`)
$$
\iota_S^n : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S) \longrightarrow H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W)
$$
as cochain pullback along $\pi_S : W \twoheadrightarrow S$ composed with the inclusion $M = C^{V_U} = C^V \hookrightarrow C|_W$. Factoring $\pi_S = e_S \circ q_W$, where $q_W : W \twoheadrightarrow W/V_W$ is the canonical quotient, decomposes $\iota_S^n$ as the isomorphism along $e_S : W/V_W \xrightarrow{\sim} S$ (identifying $(C|_W)^{V_W}$ with $M|_S$) followed by continuous inflation $\operatorname{inf}^W_{W/V_W} : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(W/V_W, (C|_W)^{V_W}) \to H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W)$ (`cohomologyInflQuot_eq_comp`, `Quotient.lean:242–256`). Moreover, because the square of continuous group homomorphisms
$$
\begin{array}{ccc}
W & \xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{incl}\;} & U \\
\downarrow{\scriptstyle \pi_S} & & \downarrow{\scriptstyle \pi} \\
S & \xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{incl}\;} & U/V_U
\end{array}
$$
commutes identically (`rfl` on underlying elements of $G$), functoriality of `cohomologyMapAlong` yields the commutative restriction–inflation square (`cohomologyRes_comp_cohomologyInflQuot`, `Quotient.lean:210–218`)
$$
\iota_S^n \circ \operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S
= \operatorname{res}^U_W \circ \operatorname{inf}^U_\Gamma
: H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \longrightarrow H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W).
$$

We now verify the three subgroup assertions on $S$:
- *First cohomology vanishes ($H^1(S, M|_S) = 0$).* By Lemma 1(1) applied to $(W, V_W, C|_W)$, $\operatorname{inf}^W_{W/V_W}$ is injective in degree $1$; precomposing with the isomorphism along $e_S$ makes $\iota_S^1 : H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S) \to H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W)$ injective (`cohomologyInflQuot_one_injective`, `Finite.lean:136–140`). Since $W$ is an open subgroup of $G$, the pre-class formation axiom gives $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W) = 0$, forcing $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S) = 0$ (`isZero_H1_quotSubgroup`, `:142–147`). Because $S$ has the discrete topology, $\kappa_{S, M|_S}^1 : H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^1(S, M|_S)$ is an isomorphism, hence $H^1(S, M|_S) = 0$ (`isZero_groupCohomology_one_sub`, `:256–262`).
- *Exact invariant of the restricted fundamental class.* Because $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, C|_V) = 0$, Lemma 1(2) applied to $(W, V_W, C|_W)$ makes $\operatorname{inf}^W_{W/V_W}$ injective in degree $2$, so $\iota_S^2 : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S) \to H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W)$ is injective (`cohomologyInflQuot_two_injective`, `Finite.lean:150–158`). Evaluating $\operatorname{inv}_W \circ \iota_S^2$ on $\operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S(u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V})$ via the commutative square, the restriction scaling axiom on $W \le U$, Lemma 3(1)–(2), and $[U : V] = [U : W]\cdot |S|$ gives
  $$
  \begin{aligned}
  \operatorname{inv}_W\bigl(\iota_S^2(\operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V})\bigr)
  &= \operatorname{inv}_W\bigl(\operatorname{res}^U_W(\operatorname{inf}^U_\Gamma u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V})\bigr) \\
  &= [U : W] \cdot \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^U_\Gamma u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}\bigr) \\
  &= [U : W] \cdot \left[\frac{1}{[U : V]}\right]
  = [U : W] \cdot \left[\frac{1}{[U : W]\cdot |S|}\right]
  = \left[\frac{1}{|S|}\right]
  \end{aligned}
  $$
  (`QZ.nsmul_mk_one_div_mul`, `inv_cohomologyInflQuot_cohomologyRes_fundamentalClass`, `Finite.lean:171–202`).
- *Exact order $|S|$ and $\mathbb Z$-generation on $H^2(S, M|_S)$.* For any $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S)$, restricting $\iota_S^2(y) \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C|_W)$ further to $V \le W$ composites with $\operatorname{res}^W_V \circ \operatorname{inf}^W_{W/V_W} = 0$ (Lemma 1(2)), so $\operatorname{res}^W_V(\iota_S^2(y)) = 0$ (`resLE_cohomologyInflQuot`, `Finite.lean:159–168`). Lemma 2(1) (using injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}_V$) therefore gives
  $$
  |S| \cdot \operatorname{inv}_W(\iota_S^2(y)) = [W : V] \cdot \operatorname{inv}_W(\iota_S^2(y)) = 0.
  $$
  All hypotheses of Lemma 4 (`QZ.cyclic_of_injective`) now hold for $X = H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S)$, $Y = H^2_{\mathrm{open}}(W, C)$, $\Phi = \iota_S^2$, $\operatorname{inv} = \operatorname{inv}_W$, $n = |S| > 0$, and $u^{\mathrm{cts}}_S = \operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}$! Lemma 4 concludes that $\operatorname{ord}(u^{\mathrm{cts}}_S) = |S|$ (`cyclic_cohomologyRes_fundamentalClass`, first conclusion, `Finite.lean:205–222`) and every $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(S, M|_S)$ equals $m\,u^{\mathrm{cts}}_S$ for some $m \in \mathbb Z$ (the second conclusion of the same declaration). Finally, naturality of $\kappa^2$ along $S \hookrightarrow \Gamma$ identifies $\kappa_{S, M|_S}^2(\operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}) = \operatorname{res}^\Gamma_S(u_{U/V})$ (`groupCohomology_map_groupFundamentalClass`, `:232–243`), and because $\kappa_{S, M|_S}^2$ is an additive isomorphism on discrete $S$, both exact order $|S|$ and $\mathbb Z$-generation transfer immediately to ordinary $H^2(S, M|_S)$ (`PreClassFormation.tate_hypotheses`, `Finite.lean:245–278`; `ClassFormation.tate_hypotheses` and `ClassFormationAt.tate_hypotheses`, `ClassFormations.lean:76–84,104–111`). [Lean, read]

**Part (2).** Let $k = \mathbb Z$, let $F$ be a `ClassFormation C`, let $U \le G$ be open, and let $r \ge 3$; write $r = n + 2$ with $n = r - 2 \ge 1$. Take any continuous cohomology class $z \in H^{n+2}_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U)$, represented by a locally constant $(n+2)$-cocycle $f : U^{n+2} \to C$. Because $U^{n+2}$ is compact and $f$ is locally constant with values in the discrete module $C$, $f$ takes only finitely many values (stabilized by open normal subgroups of $U$) and is constant on cosets of a finite intersection $N \trianglelefteq U$ of open normal subgroups; thus $z = \operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(w)$ for some ordinary class $w \in H^{n+2}(U/N, C^N)$ (`exists_inflation_eq`, `Colimit.lean:93–99`). Since $U \le G$ carries the subspace topology, openness of $N$ in $U$ produces an open subgroup $V \le G$ with $V \le U$ and $V \cap U = N$ (`exists_openSubgroup_subgroupOf_eq`, `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/FormationThree.lean:19–25`, by intersecting $U$ with an open subset of $G$ whose trace on $U$ is $N$ and shrinking to an open subgroup of $G$). Compactness of $U$ makes the discrete quotient $\Gamma := U/N = U/V_U$ a finite group (`SubsingletonCohomologyTopOfThreeLeInt.lean:44–48`).

At `SubsingletonCohomologyTopOfThreeLeInt.lean:49–51`, `ClassFormation.exists_cupH_quot` passes the fundamental class $a := u_{U/V} \in H^2(\Gamma, C^N)$ (`F.groupFundamentalClass U V`) and the three all-subgroup projections of Part (1) (`(F.tate_hypotheses hVU S).1`, `.2.1`, `.2.2`) into the **inherited** finite-group cup-factorization theorem `GroundTheory.GroupCoh.exists_cupH_of_tate` (`ClassFormations/HighFinite.lean:94–117`, proved across §§11.4.3.13–11.4.3.15 from the degree-zero Tate unit, the normalized double connector, and all-subgroup cohomological-triviality detection of the splitting module $B_a$, cited here without re-proof). Because $n \ge 1$, that theorem returns an ordinary cohomology class $y \in H^n(\Gamma, \mathbb Z)$ (for the trivial $\Gamma$-action on $\mathbb Z$) and a nonzero integer $m \ge 1$ ($m = |\Gamma|$) such that
$$
m \cdot y = 0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
w = (\rho_{C^N})_*(a \smile y) \in H^{n+2}(\Gamma, C^N),
$$
where $\rho_{C^N} : C^N \otimes_{\mathbb Z} \mathbb Z \xrightarrow{\sim} C^N$ is the right unitor. Inflating $y$ from $\Gamma = U/N$ to $U$ yields $t := \operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(y) \in H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \mathbb Z)$ still satisfying $m \cdot t = 0$ (`SubsingletonCohomologyTopOfThreeLeInt.lean:83–87`), while compatibility of inflation with the inhomogeneous cup product (`cup_inflation`) and with the right unitor (`rightUnitor_quotientToInvariants`, `:59–66,88–115`) gives
$$
z = \operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(w) = (\rho_{C|_U})_*\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(a) \smile t\bigr) \in H^{n+2}_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U).
$$
Now comes the decisive profinite step (`:116–120`): because $m \neq 0$, divisibility of $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ (`QZ.exists_nsmul_eq`) furnishes $q \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ with $m q = \operatorname{inv}_U(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(a))$, and **surjectivity** of $\operatorname{inv}_U$ (`(F.inv_bijective U).2`) furnishes $\beta \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U)$ with $\operatorname{inv}_U(\beta) = q$. By additivity and **injectivity** of $\operatorname{inv}_U$ (`(F.inv_bijective U).1`),
$$
\operatorname{inv}_U(m \cdot \beta) = m\,\operatorname{inv}_U(\beta) = m q = \operatorname{inv}_U\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(a)\bigr)
\implies m \cdot \beta = \operatorname{inf}^U_{U/N}(a).
$$
Bilinearity of the cup product then shifts the factor $m$ from the left slot to the torsion right slot:
$$
z = (\rho_{C|_U})_*\bigl((m \cdot \beta) \smile t\bigr)
= m \cdot (\rho_{C|_U})_*(\beta \smile t)
= (\rho_{C|_U})_*\bigl(\beta \smile (m \cdot t)\bigr)
= (\rho_{C|_U})_*(\beta \smile 0)
= 0.
$$
Hence $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U) = 0$ for every open $U \le G$ and every $r \ge 3$ (`subsingleton_cohomology_of_three_le_int`, `:69–121`). Specializing to $U = \top$ and transporting along the topological isomorphism $\top \xrightarrow{\sim} G$ (`subsingleton_cohomology_of_top`, `:20–29,124–126`) — and across `ULift ℤ ≃ ℤ` when coefficients live over `ULift ℤ` (`:136–146`) — gives $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, C) = 0$ for all $r \ge 3$. [Lean, read]

**Hand calculation 1 (cyclic quadratic quotient $U/V \simeq C_2$ and explicit degree-four annihilation on $C_4$).** Take the unconditional profinite model $G = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ acting trivially on $C = \mathbb Z$, whose open subgroups are $U_n = n\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ ($n \ge 1$) with topological generators $\gamma_n = n \cdot 1$ and relative indices $[U_n : U_{d n}] = d$. Because $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ is torsion-free and $\mathbb Z$ is discrete, $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Z) = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Z) = 0$ for every $n \ge 1$. From the short exact sequence $0 \to \mathbb Z \to \mathbb Q \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z \to 0$ of trivial discrete $U_n$-modules (in which $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Q) = H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Q) = 0$ because every finite-quotient group cohomology of $\mathbb Q$ is killed by multiplication by the quotient order), the connecting isomorphism $\delta_{U_n} : \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Z)$ composed with evaluation at $\gamma_n$ defines a bijection $\operatorname{inv}_{U_n}(\delta_{U_n}(\chi)) := \chi(\gamma_n) \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. For $U_{d n} \le U_n$, $\gamma_{d n} = d\,\gamma_n$, so $\operatorname{inv}_{U_{d n}}(\operatorname{res}^{U_n}_{U_{d n}} \delta_{U_n}(\chi)) = \chi(d\gamma_n) = d\,\chi(\gamma_n) = [U_n : U_{d n}]\,\operatorname{inv}_{U_n}(\delta_{U_n}(\chi))$; thus $(G, C, \operatorname{inv})$ satisfies all axioms of `ClassFormation C`. Moreover, for $\chi_m : \widehat{\mathbb Z} \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ sending $1 \mapsto [1/m]$ (factoring through $\widehat{\mathbb Z}/m\widehat{\mathbb Z} \simeq C_m = \{0, 1, \ldots, m-1\}$), lifting $[i/m]$ to $i/m \in \mathbb Q$ produces the connecting $2$-cocycle
$$
\frac{j}{m} - \frac{(i + j) \bmod m}{m} + \frac{i}{m}
= \left\lfloor\frac{i + j}{m}\right\rfloor
= c_m(i, j),
$$
so $\operatorname{inv}_{U_1}(\operatorname{inf}^{U_1}_{C_m}[c_m]) = [1/m] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$.

Now set $U = U_1 = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ and $V = U_2 = 2\widehat{\mathbb Z}$, with quotient $\Gamma = U/V \simeq C_2 = \{0, 1\}$. On $C_2$, $H^2(C_2, \mathbb Z) = \{0, [c_2]\} \simeq \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$, where the carry matrix is
$$
\bigl(c_2(i, j)\bigr)_{i,j \in \{0,1\}} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix},
\qquad 2\,c_2 = d^1 u_1 \quad\text{for } u_1 = (0, 1).
$$
Under $y \mapsto \operatorname{inv}_U(\operatorname{inf}^U_{C_2} y)$, the two classes $0$ and $[c_2]$ map to $[0]$ and $[1/2]$ in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$; hence `Function.invFun` at $[1/[U : V]] = [1/2]$ uniquely selects $u_{U/V} = [c_2]$. Restricting $\operatorname{inf}^U_{C_2}[c_2]$ to $V = 2\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ scales the invariant to $2 \cdot [1/2] = [1] = 0$, matching $\operatorname{res}^U_V(\operatorname{inf}[c_2]) = 0$. Conversely, any $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(V, \mathbb Z)$ with $\operatorname{inv}_V(y) = [a/b]$ lifts to $x \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \mathbb Z)$ with $\operatorname{inv}_U(x) = [a/(2b)]$, and Shapiro's trace $\operatorname{coindTrace}$ sums over the two cosets $0 + 2\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ and $1 + 2\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ to give $\operatorname{cores}^U_V(y) = \operatorname{cores}^U_V(\operatorname{res}^U_V x) = 2 x$, whose $U$-invariant is $2 \cdot [a/(2b)] = [a/b] = \operatorname{inv}_V(y)$. On the two subgroups of $C_2$:
- For $S = \{0\}$: the preimage is $W = 2\widehat{\mathbb Z} = V$, $[U : W] = 2$, $[W : V] = 1$, $\operatorname{inv}_W(\iota_{\{0\}}^2(\operatorname{res}[c_2])) = 2 \cdot [1/2] = 0 = [1/1]$, $\operatorname{ord}(0) = 1 = |S|$, and $H^1(\{0\}, \mathbb Z) = H^2(\{0\}, \mathbb Z) = 0$.
- For $S = C_2$: the preimage is $W = \widehat{\mathbb Z} = U$, $[U : W] = 1$, $[W : V] = 2$, $\operatorname{inv}_W(\iota_{C_2}^2([c_2])) = [1/2]$, $\operatorname{ord}([c_2]) = 2 = |S|$, $H^1(C_2, \mathbb Z) = 0$, and $H^2(C_2, \mathbb Z) = \mathbb Z[c_2]$.

To watch the degree-$r \ge 3$ consumer in action where finite-quotient cohomology alone does **not** vanish, take $r = 4$ ($n = 2$) and the cup square $w_4 := c_2 \smile c_2 : C_2^4 \to \mathbb Z$, given by $w_4(i_0, i_1, i_2, i_3) = c_2(i_0, i_1)\,c_2(i_2, i_3)$ (equal to $1$ solely at $(1, 1, 1, 1)$). On $C_2$ itself, $[w_4] \neq 0$ in $H^4(C_2, \mathbb Z) \simeq \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$: for *every* $3$-cochain $h : C_2^3 \to \mathbb Z$, summing $(d^3 h)(a, 1, b, 1)$ over $a, b \in \{0, 1\}$ cancels the middle two face sums identically and cancels the last two face sums identically, leaving
$$
\sum_{a,b \in \{0,1\}} (d^3 h)(a, 1, b, 1)
= 2 \sum_{b \in \{0,1\}} h(1, b, 1) \equiv 0 \pmod 2,
\quad\text{whereas}\quad
\sum_{a,b \in \{0,1\}} w_4(a, 1, b, 1) = 1.
$$
In Theorem Part (2), $w = [w_4]$ already has the factored form $[c_2] \smile y$ with $y = [c_2] \in H^2(C_2, \mathbb Z)$ killed by $m = 2$ ($2 c_2 = d^1 u_1$). To divide $\operatorname{inv}_U(\operatorname{inf}^U_{C_2}[c_2]) = [1/2]$ by $m = 2$ inside $H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \mathbb Z)$, pass one level deeper to $U_4 = 4\widehat{\mathbb Z}$, where $U/U_4 \simeq C_4 = \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$ carries the fundamental carry $c_4(k, l) = \lfloor(k + l)/4\rfloor$ of invariant $[1/4]$ ($2 \cdot [1/4] = [1/2]$). Writing any $k \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$ in binary as $k = 2\,b(k) + (k \bmod 2)$ with $b(k) = \lfloor k/2\rfloor$, i.e. $b = (0, 0, 1, 1)$, equating quotients by $2$ in $k + l = 4\,c_4(k, l) + ((k + l) \bmod 4)$ gives the exact $2$-cochain identity on $C_4^2$:
$$
(\operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2} c_2)(k, l) = 2\,c_4(k, l) - (d^1 b)(k, l)
\qquad (0 \le k, l < 4),
$$
witnessing $[\operatorname{inf} c_2] = 2[c_4]$ (`m • β = inf a` at finite level $C_4$). Cup-multiplying on the right by $\operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2} c_2$ and applying the graded Leibniz rule $d(f \smile g) = (df) \smile g + (-1)^{\deg f} f \smile (dg)$ (`CupCochains.lean:113–116`), where $d^2 c_4 = 0$, $(-1)^2 = +1$, $2\,\operatorname{inf} c_2 = d^1(\operatorname{inf} u_1)$, and $d^2(\operatorname{inf} c_2) = 0$, gives an explicit $3$-cochain primitive on all $4^4 = 256$ quadruples in $C_4^4$:
$$
\operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2}(c_2 \smile c_2)
= (2\,c_4 - d^1 b) \smile \operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2} c_2
= d^3\!\left(c_4 \smile \operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2} u_1 - b \smile \operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2} c_2\right).
$$
Inflating further from $C_4$ to $U = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ kills $z = \operatorname{inf}^U_{C_2}[w_4]$ in $H^4_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \mathbb Z)$, exactly as proved abstractly in `SubsingletonCohomologyTopOfThreeLeInt.lean:116–120`.

**Hand calculation 2 (subgroup chains in $U/V \simeq C_{12}$, Bézout prime-set synthesis, and a non-normal preimage).** Retain $(G = \widehat{\mathbb Z}, C = \mathbb Z)$ and take $U = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$, $V = 12\widehat{\mathbb Z}$, so $\Gamma = U/V \simeq C_{12} = \{0, 1, \ldots, 11\}$ has $[U : V] = 12$. The unique class in $H^2(C_{12}, \mathbb Z) \simeq \mathbb Z/12\mathbb Z$ whose inflation has $\operatorname{inv}_U$-value $[1/12]$ is represented by $c_{12}(i, j) = \lfloor(i + j)/12\rfloor$: indeed $\sum_{i=0}^{11} c_{12}(i, 1) = c_{12}(11, 1) = 1$, whereas for any $1$-cochain $h : C_{12} \to \mathbb Z$ telescoping gives $\sum_{i=0}^{11} (d^1 h)(i, 1) = 12\,h(1) \equiv 0 \pmod{12}$, while $12\,c_{12} = d^1 u_{12}$ for $u_{12}(i) = i$.

Every subgroup of $C_{12}$ has the form $S_d := \langle d \bmod 12\rangle$ of order $|S_d| = 12/d$ for a unique divisor $d \mid 12$ ($d \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 12\}$). Its open preimage in $U = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ is $W_d := \pi^{-1}(S_d) = d\widehat{\mathbb Z} = U_d$, with index factorization $[U : W_d] = d$, $[W_d : V] = 12/d = |S_d|$, and $[U : W_d][W_d : V] = 12 = [U : V]$. Under the topological isomorphism $e_{S_d} : W_d/V = d\widehat{\mathbb Z}/12\widehat{\mathbb Z} \xrightarrow{\sim} S_d \simeq C_{12/d}$ sending $d a + 12\widehat{\mathbb Z} \mapsto d a \bmod 12$ ($0 \le a < 12/d$), restricting $c_{12}$ to $S_d$ satisfies the exact pointwise carry identity
$$
(\operatorname{res}^{C_{12}}_{S_d} c_{12})(d a, d b)
= \left\lfloor\frac{d a + d b}{12}\right\rfloor
= \left\lfloor\frac{a + b}{12/d}\right\rfloor
= c_{12/d}(a, b)
\qquad \bigl(0 \le a, b < 12/d\bigr),
$$
because $d$ cancels identically between numerator and denominator inside the floor! Meanwhile the commutative square $\iota_{S_d}^2 \circ \operatorname{res}^\Gamma_{S_d} = \operatorname{res}^U_{W_d} \circ \operatorname{inf}^U_\Gamma$ sends $u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V}$ to a class on $W_d = d\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ of invariant
$$
\operatorname{inv}_{W_d}\bigl(\iota_{S_d}^2(\operatorname{res}^\Gamma_{S_d} u^{\mathrm{cts}}_{U/V})\bigr)
= [U : W_d] \cdot \left[\frac{1}{12}\right]
= d \cdot \left[\frac{1}{12}\right]
= \left[\frac{1}{12/d}\right]
= \left[\frac{1}{|S_d|}\right].
$$
Tracing the primary chain $1 = S_{12} < S_6 \simeq C_2 < S_3 \simeq C_4 < S_1 = C_{12}$ (and the complementary chain $1 = S_{12} < S_4 \simeq C_3 < S_2 \simeq C_6 < S_1 = C_{12}$) across all six subgroups gives:

| Divisor $d$ | Subgroup $S_d \le C_{12}$ | Open preimage $W_d$ | $[U : W_d]$ | $[W_d : V] = |S_d|$ | Restricted cocycle | $\operatorname{inv}_{W_d}(\iota_{S_d}^2 \operatorname{res} u)$ | $H^1(S_d, \mathbb Z)$ | Generator of $H^2(S_d, \mathbb Z)$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $1$ | $S_1 \simeq C_{12}$ | $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ | $1$ | $12$ | $c_{12}$ | $[1/12]$ | $0$ | $[c_{12}]$ (order $12$) |
| $2$ | $S_2 \simeq C_6$ | $2\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ | $2$ | $6$ | $c_6$ | $[1/6]$ | $0$ | $[c_6]$ (order $6$) |
| $3$ | $S_3 \simeq C_4$ | $3\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ | $3$ | $4$ | $c_4$ | $[1/4]$ | $0$ | $[c_4]$ (order $4$) |
| $4$ | $S_4 \simeq C_3$ | $4\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ | $4$ | $3$ | $c_3$ | $[1/3]$ | $0$ | $[c_3]$ (order $3$) |
| $6$ | $S_6 \simeq C_2$ | $6\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ | $6$ | $2$ | $c_2$ | $[1/2]$ | $0$ | $[c_2]$ (order $2$) |
| $12$ | $S_{12} = \{0\}$ | $12\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ | $12$ | $1$ | $c_1 = 0$ | $[0]$ | $0$ | $0$ (order $1$) |

Along each inclusion $S_{d'} \le S_d$ ($d \mid d'$), restricting $[c_{12/d}]$ further to $S_{d'}$ multiplies the open-preimage invariant by $[W_d : W_{d'}] = d'/d$ (for instance $[1/12] \xrightarrow{\times 3} [1/4] \xrightarrow{\times 2} [1/2] \xrightarrow{\times 2} [0]$ along $C_{12} \supset C_4 \supset C_2 \supset 1$).

Two auxiliary checks illuminate the remaining branches of Lemma 3(2) and Theorem Part (1):
- *Bézout synthesis in `ClassFormationAt P`.* Suppose only the prime-primary components for $P = \{\text{primes } p \neq 3\}$ are assumed in $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U)$, and take $[U : U_{10}] = 10 = 2 \cdot 5$ (all prime factors in $P$). With $p = \operatorname{minFac}(10) = 2$ and $m = 5$, Bézout's coefficients $(-1) \cdot 5 + 3 \cdot 2 = 1$ decompose
  $$
  \left[\frac{1}{10}\right] = (-1)\cdot\left[\frac{1}{2}\right] + 3\cdot\left[\frac{1}{5}\right] \in (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[2^\infty] + (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[5^\infty] \subseteq \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}_U),
  $$
  producing $u_{C_{10}} = -5\,[c_{10}] + 6\,[c_{10}] = [c_{10}]$ without assuming $3$-primary surjectivity.
- *Non-normal subgroup preimage (`normal_subgroupOf_quotPreimage`).* In the finite discrete group $U = C_2 \times S_3$ with normal subgroup $V = C_2 \times \{\mathrm{id}\} \trianglelefteq U$, the quotient is $U/V \simeq S_3$. The transposition subgroup $S = \{\mathrm{id}, (12)\} \le S_3$ is **not** normal in $S_3$ (since $(13)(12)(13)^{-1} = (23) \notin S$), and its preimage $W = \pi^{-1}(S) = C_2 \times S$ of order $4$ is **not** normal in $U$. Nevertheless $V = \ker(\pi|_W : W \twoheadrightarrow S)$ **is** normal in $W$ with $W/V \simeq S$, which is why $\iota_S^n : H^n(S, C^V) \to H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, C)$ is defined on non-normal subgroups $S \le U/V$. The injectivity statements used above are in degree one and, under the stated vanishing hypothesis, in degree two.

**Failure boundaries (deleting any of the three axioms).** Each structural hypothesis in Lemma 3 and the Theorem is strictly necessary:
1. *Dropping $H^1(V, C) = 0$ breaks degree-two inflation injectivity.* Take the finite discrete group $U = C_4 = \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$, normal subgroup $V = \{0, 2\} \simeq C_2$, and trivial coefficients $C = \mathbb F_2$. Here $H^1(V, \mathbb F_2) = \operatorname{Hom}(C_2, \mathbb F_2) \simeq \mathbb F_2 \neq 0$. On $U/V \simeq C_2$, the reduced carry $\bar c_2(i, j) = \lfloor(i + j)/2\rfloor \bmod 2$ represents a **nonzero** class in $H^2(C_2, \mathbb F_2) \simeq \mathbb F_2$, because every (even unnormalized) $1$-coboundary $d^1 h$ on $C_2$ with trivial $\mathbb F_2$-action satisfies $(d^1 h)(1, 1) - (d^1 h)(0, 0) = (2 h(1) - h(0)) - h(0) \equiv 0 \pmod 2$, whereas $\bar c_2(1, 1) - \bar c_2(0, 0) = 1 - 0 = 1 \neq 0$. Yet inflating $\bar c_2$ to $C_4$ reduces $2 c_4 - d^1 b$ modulo $2$ to
   $$
   (\operatorname{inf}^{C_4}_{C_2} \bar c_2)(k, l) \equiv -(d^1 \bar b)(k, l) \equiv (d^1 \bar b)(k, l) \pmod 2
   \qquad \bigl(\bar b = (0, 0, 1, 1)\bigr),
   $$
   so $\operatorname{inf}_2([\bar c_2]) = 0$ in $H^2(C_4, \mathbb F_2)$: the connecting map $H^1(V, \mathbb F_2) \to H^2(U/V, \mathbb F_2)$ has nontrivial image, and Lemma 1(2) fails.
2. *Dropping injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}$ breaks fundamental-class uniqueness and $H^2$-cyclicity.* Keep $G = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$, replace $C = \mathbb Z$ by the trivial module $C' = \mathbb Z \oplus \mathbb Z$, and define $\operatorname{inv}'_{U_n}(x_1, x_2) := \operatorname{inv}_{U_n}(x_1)$ by projecting onto the first summand. Every `PreClassFormation` axiom and surjectivity of $\operatorname{inv}'_{U_n}$ hold: $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Z^2) = 0$ and $\operatorname{inv}'_V \circ \operatorname{res}^U_V = [U : V]\,\operatorname{inv}'_U$. Yet at $U/U_2 \simeq C_2$, **two** distinct classes $([c_2], 0)$ and $([c_2], [c_2])$ in $H^2(C_2, \mathbb Z^2) \simeq (\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z)^2$ inflate to invariant $[1/2]$, and $H^2(C_2, \mathbb Z^2)$ has four elements while every cyclic $\mathbb Z$-span has size at most $2$, violating both uniqueness in Lemma 3(1) and generation in Theorem Part (1).
3. *Dropping prime-factor surjectivity in `ClassFormationAt P` breaks existence of the fundamental class.* Take $G = \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ acting trivially on $C'' = \mathbb Z[1/3] \subset \mathbb Q$. From $0 \to \mathbb Z[1/3] \to \mathbb Q \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z[1/3] \to 0$ and the canonical embedding $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z[1/3] \hookrightarrow \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ onto the prime-to-$3$ torsion $\bigoplus_{p \neq 3} (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[p^\infty]$, $(G, C'', \operatorname{inv}'')$ is a genuine `ClassFormationAt P` for $P = \{\text{primes } p \neq 3\}$: $\operatorname{inv}''_{U_n}$ is injective on every $U_n$, $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U_n, \mathbb Z[1/3]) = 0$, and every quotient of order coprime to $3$ (such as $C_2, C_4, C_5, C_{10}$) acquires its fundamental class and Tate hypotheses. At $[U_1 : U_{12}] = 12$, however, $3 \mid 12$ and $3 \notin P$: the image of $\operatorname{inv}''_{U_1} \circ \operatorname{inf}^{U_1}_{C_{12}}$ on $H^2(C_{12}, \mathbb Z[1/3]) \simeq \mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$ is $\{[0], [1/4], [1/2], [3/4]\}$, which misses $[1/12]$. Because $H^2(C_{12}, \mathbb Z[1/3]) \simeq \mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z$ has exponent $4$, **no element of order $12$ exists** in $H^2(C_{12}, \mathbb Z[1/3])$, and `Function.invFun` at $[1/12]$ falls back to `Classical.arbitrary` without satisfying the fundamental-class invariant identity.

**Remarks: abstract class formations, arithmetic realizations, and classical comparison.**
1. *Exact boundary between abstract class formations and arithmetic class field theory.* Every proof in this subsection belongs to **pure profinite group cohomology**: `ClassFormation C` and `ClassFormationAt P C` axiomatize $\operatorname{inv}_U$, $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C) = 0$, and $\operatorname{inv}_V \circ \operatorname{res}^U_V = [U : V]\,\operatorname{inv}_U$ without mentioning valuations, Brauer groups, adeles, or ideles. In the archive, two separate arithmetic trees inhabit `ClassFormation` and then immediately inherit `groupFundamentalClass`, `tate_hypotheses`, and $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}} = 0$ ($r \ge 3$) through the one-line consumers in `ClassFormations/HighFormations.lean:14–27` [Lean, read]:
   - **Local class field theory (`localClassFormation` and `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure`, `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:184–207` — proved at local R3 in [§11.4.3.17](#114317-arithmetic-realization-of-the-non-archimedean-local-class-formation-localclassformation)):** for a non-archimedean local field $K$ with separable closure $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, $G = \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ acts on the discrete multiplicative group `galUnits K Ksep` $= K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times$; §11.4.3.17 reads in full the open-subgroup–fixed-field correspondence, profinite Hilbert 90 (`isZero_cohomology_one_galUnits`), the division-algebra-free Hasse–Brauer invariant bijection (`brauerInv_bijective`), local degree scaling (`brauerInv_brauerRes`), and the spectral-norm valuative loader `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep`, discharging `HighFormations.subsingleton_cohomology_galUnits_of_three_le` (`HighFormations.lean:14–18`) and `FormationThree.subsingleton_cohomology_three` (`FormationThree.lean:30–53`, `Vanishing.lean:44–46`).
   - **Global class field theory (`globalClassFormation` and `globalClassFormationOf`, `GlobalClassFormation.lean:19–20`, `GlobalClassFormation/ClassFormationGlobal.lean:152–164` `[Lean]`):** for a number field $K$, $G = \operatorname{Gal}(\overline K/K)$ acts on the colimit idele-class module `classLimitRep K` $= \varinjlim_L C_L$; the archive supplies idele-class $H^1$ vanishing (`isZero_cohomology_one_classLimitRep`), global invariant bijectivity (`classInv_bijective`, built from the sum of local invariants and global reciprocity), and degree scaling (`classInv_classResField` from `SumInvRestriction`), whence `HighFormations.subsingleton_cohomology_classLimitRep_of_three_le` (`HighFormations.lean:23–26`) gives $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(\overline K/K), C_{\overline K}) = 0$ for all $r \ge 3$.
   All internal global arithmetic proofs behind `globalClassFormation` remain **named unread producers** (`[Lean]`) outside this chapter's current R3 scope.
2. *Comparison with Milne, Serre, and Artin–Tate.* Classical treatments of class formations originate in E. Artin and J. Tate, *Class Field Theory*, Ch. XIV, and J.-P. Serre, *Local Fields*, Springer GTM 67, Ch. XI §§1–3, pp. 164–171 [Paper]:
   - Serre defines a *formation* in Ch. XI §1 (pp. 164–165) and in §2 (pp. 166–167) defines a *class formation* by adjoining finite-layer invariant embeddings $\operatorname{inv}_{F/E} : H^2(F/E) \xrightarrow{\sim} \frac{1}{[F:E]}\mathbb Z/\mathbb Z \subset \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ satisfying Axiom I ($H^1(F/E) = 0$), Axiom II(a), and the restriction scaling Axiom II(b) ($\operatorname{inv}_{F'/E'} \circ \operatorname{Res} = [E' : E]\,\operatorname{inv}_{F/E}$).
   - The Lean archive instead places the bijective invariant maps $\operatorname{inv}_U : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ and scaling law $\operatorname{inv}_V \circ \operatorname{res}^U_V = [U : V]\,\operatorname{inv}_U$ directly on open-subgroup continuous cohomology (matching J. S. Milne, *Class Field Theory*, v4.03, II §4, pp. 86–88 for continuous profinite colimits, III Theorem 2.1 & Lemma 2.7, pp. 103–106 locally, and VIII Lemma 4.6 & Theorem 4.7, p. 242 for global scaling and finite-layer fundamental classes), and *deduces* existence and uniqueness of $u_{U/V}$ alongside all three subgroup hypotheses in Milne II Theorem 3.11 (pp. 84–85) uniformly via degree-two inflation–restriction (Milne II Proposition 1.34, pp. 71–72) and `QZ.cyclic_of_injective`, before killing $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U)$ ($r \ge 3$) by dividing inflated fundamental classes in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, v4.03](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf) · [Source ledger and consumer contract](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-114316-fundamental-classes-and-the-three-subgroup-hypotheses-in-a-class-formation)

#### 11.4.3.17 Arithmetic realization of the non-archimedean local class formation (`localClassFormation`)

**Motivation.** [Section 11.4.3.16](#114316-fundamental-classes-and-the-three-subgroup-hypotheses-in-a-class-formation) proved that once a discrete representation $C$ of a compact, totally disconnected group $G$ carries a `ClassFormation C` — first-cohomology vanishing $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U) = 0$, bijective invariants $\operatorname{inv}_U : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, C|_U) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, and index scaling $\operatorname{inv}_V \circ \operatorname{res}^U_V = [U : V]\,\operatorname{inv}_U$ for open subgroups $V \le U \le G$ — every finite Galois quotient acquires a unique fundamental class, every subgroup (normal or not) satisfies Tate's three hypotheses, and $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G, C) = 0$ for all $r \ge 3$. For a non-archimedean local field $K$ with separable closure $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, $G = \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ acts on the discrete multiplicative group $K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times$ (`galUnits K Ksep`), and inhabiting `ClassFormation (galUnits K Ksep)` requires four distinct arithmetic bridges: (1) identifying open subgroups $U \le \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ with finite separable extensions $K_U = K_{\mathrm{sep}}^U$ so that relative group indices $[U : V]$ equal field degrees $[K_V : K_U]$ and continuous subgroup cohomology transports to $\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U)$; (2) lifting finite-extension Hilbert's Theorem 90 (§11.4.3.10) across the Krull colimit to $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) = 0$; (3) constructing a bijective Hasse–Brauer invariant $\operatorname{inv}_F : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/F), K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ **purely cohomologically** (without central simple division algebras) by splitting arbitrary finite Galois classes over unramified cyclotomic extensions and combining valuation ramification $e$ with Frobenius power $f$ to obtain $[F' : F] = e \cdot f$ scaling; and (4) loading the unique ultrametric spectral-norm valuation and topology onto an untopologized separable closure $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ (`localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure`) so downstream consumers receive $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}} = 0$ ($r \ge 3$) from `[IsSepClosure K Ksep]` alone.

**Examples to keep in view.** Over $K = \mathbb Q_p$ ($p$ odd, say $p = 5$ with non-square unit $u = 2$, or $p = 3$ with $u = -1$), the unramified quadratic extension $L_2 = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{u})$ has Galois group $\Gamma = \{1, \sigma\} \simeq C_2$ generated by the arithmetic Frobenius $\sigma(\sqrt{u}) = -\sqrt{u}$, and the normalized $2$-cocycle $c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(\sigma, \sigma) = p$ pushes under $\operatorname{ord}_{L_2}(p) = 1$ to the integer carry cocycle of invariant $[1/2] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. Restricting $[c_{\mathrm{unr},2}]$ to the totally ramified quadratic extension $E_2 = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p})$ ($e = 2, f = 1$) doubles the valuation $\operatorname{ord}_{L_2(\sqrt{p})}(p) = 2$ and writes $c_{\mathrm{unr},2} = d^1(1, \sqrt{p})$, scaling $[1/2] \mapsto 2 \cdot [1/2] = [0]$; restricting the quartic unramified fundamental class on $L_4 = \mathbb Q_5(2^{1/4})$ ($\operatorname{inv} = [1/4]$) to $E_2$ or $L_2$ scales $[1/4] \mapsto 2 \cdot [1/4] = [1/2] \neq 0$. Conversely, on the totally ramified quadratic extension $E_2 = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p})$ ($\Delta = \{1, \tau\} \simeq C_2$, $\tau(\sqrt{p}) = -\sqrt{p}$), the unit cocycle $c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(\tau, \tau) = u$ has $\operatorname{ord}_{E_2}(u) = 0$ and cannot be evaluated directly by valuation on $E_2$; passing to the biquadratic compositum $M_2 = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p}, \sqrt{u})$ ($\operatorname{Gal}(M_2/\mathbb Q_p) \simeq C_2 \times C_2$), the explicit $1$-cochain $\eta_2 = (1, u^{-1}\sqrt{u}, \sqrt{p}, -u^{-1}\sqrt{p}\sqrt{u})$ satisfies $\operatorname{inf}^{M_2}_{L_2} c_{\mathrm{unr},2} = (\operatorname{inf}^{M_2}_{E_2} c_{\mathrm{ram},2}) \cdot d^1 \eta_2$, revealing $\operatorname{inv}_{\mathbb Q_p}(\operatorname{inf}[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}]) = [1/2]$. Deleting non-archimedean infinite unramified divisibility ($K = \mathbb R$, where $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb R) \simeq \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$ and $H^4(\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R), \mathbb C^\times) \neq 0$), deleting local completeness ($K = \mathbb Q$, where $|\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q)[2]| = \infty$), or replacing the finite residue field of a complete discrete valuation ring by $\mathbb Q$ ($K = \mathbb Q((t))$, where $H^3(\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb Q(i,\sqrt{2})((t))/\mathbb Q((t))), \mathbb Q(i,\sqrt{2})((t))^\times) \neq 0$) breaks `brauerInv_bijective` or degree-$3$/$4$ vanishing.

**Definitions.** In pinned Mathlib (`Mathlib/NumberTheory/LocalField/Basic.lean:45–50,102–113` at commit `0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474`), a topological field $K$ equipped with a valuation equivalence class (`[ValuativeRel K]`) is a *non-archimedean local field* (`IsNonarchimedeanLocalField K`) if its topology is induced by the valuation (`IsValuativeTopology K`), $K$ is locally compact (`LocallyCompactSpace K`), and the valuation is nontrivial (`ValuativeRel.IsNontrivial K`); these typeclasses automatically make the valuation ring $\mathcal O_K$ (`𝒪[K]`) a compact discrete valuation ring, the residue field $\kappa(K)$ (`𝓀[K]`) finite of cardinality $q_K := |\kappa(K)| > 1$, and $K$ complete (`normedField_completeSpace`, `GroundTheory/LocalFields/FiniteExtension.lean:15–54`) [Lean, read].

For any field extension $L/K$, the archive packages the multiplicative group $L^\times$ (written additively via `Additive Lˣ`) with Galois action $\sigma \cdot \operatorname{ofMul}(u) := \operatorname{ofMul}(\sigma(u))$ as a representation `galUnits K L` over `ULift ℤ` and `galUnitsZ K L` over $\mathbb Z$ (`ProfiniteCohomology/GaloisModules/CoeGalRootsOfUnityHomApply.lean:58–126`), related in degree $2$ by the definitional cochain identification `h2GalUnitsAddEquiv K L : H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \operatorname{galUnits} K L) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \operatorname{galUnitsZ} K L)` (`LocalClassFormation/OrderScalars.lean:22–95`) [Lean, read]. When $L/K$ is algebraic (`[Algebra.IsIntegral K L]`), every $u \in L^\times$ generates a finite subextension $K(u)/K$ whose fixing subgroup is open in the Krull topology on $\operatorname{Gal}(L/K)$, making `galUnits K L` and `galUnitsZ K L` discrete representations (`isDiscreteRep_galUnits`, `GaloisModules/Discrete.lean:35–71`) [Lean, read].

For a Galois extension $\Omega/F$, write $\operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F) := H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F), \Omega^\times)$ (`galoisBrauerGroup F Ω`, specialized to `cohomologicalBrauerGroup F Ksep` when $\Omega = K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ is a separable closure of $F$, `GaloisCohomology/UnitsInflationCochainsFHomApply.lean:64–70`). For any finite normal intermediate field $E \subseteq \Omega$ over $F$, pulling locally constant $2$-cochains back along the continuous quotient $\operatorname{AlgEquiv.restrictNormalHom} E : \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F) \twoheadrightarrow \operatorname{Gal}(E/F)$ composed with $E^\times \hookrightarrow \Omega^\times$ defines *Brauer inflation* (`brauerInflation E`, `UnitsInflationCochainsFHomApply.lean:92–136`, `GaloisCohomology/Brauer.lean:15–22`):
$$
\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{E/F} : H^2\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times\bigr) \longrightarrow \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F).
$$
When $F$ is a non-archimedean local field and $\Omega/F$ is a separable closure (`[IsSepClosure F Ω]`) equipped with a compatible valuative extension topology (`[ValuativeRel Ω] [TopologicalSpace Ω] [IsValuativeTopology Ω] [ValuativeExtension F Ω]`), the archive constructs:
1. For each $n \in \mathbb N_+$, a chosen primitive $(q_F^n - 1)$-th root of unity $\zeta_{F,n} \in \Omega$ (`unramifiedRoot F Ω n`) and the *degree-$n$ unramified level* $F_n := F(\zeta_{F,n}) \subseteq \Omega$ (`unramifiedLevel F Ω n`, `LocalClassFormation/InvariantDefs.lean:17–56`), which is cyclic Galois and unramified over $F$ of degree $[F_n : F] = n$.
2. For any finite unramified extension $L/F$ of local fields with arithmetic Frobenius generator $\operatorname{Frob}_{L/F} \in \operatorname{Gal}(L/F)$ (`frob F L`, lifting $y \mapsto y^{q_F}$ on $\kappa(L)/\kappa(F)$, `LocalFields/UnramifiedGalois.lean:183–212`), the *unramified invariant* (`unramifiedInv F L`, `LocalClassFormation/Unramified.lean:163–203`)
   $$
   \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/F} : H^2\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(L/F), L^\times\bigr)
   \xrightarrow{\;(\operatorname{ord}_L)_*\;} H^2\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(L/F), \mathbb Z\bigr)
   \xrightarrow{\;\delta^{-1}\;} \operatorname{Hom}\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(L/F), \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\bigr)
   \xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{ev}_{\operatorname{Frob}_{L/F}}\;} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z,
   $$
   where $\delta : H^1(\operatorname{Gal}(L/F), \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/F), \mathbb Z)$ is the connecting isomorphism of $0 \to \mathbb Z \to \mathbb Q \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z \to 0$ (`h2IntAddEquiv`, `TateCohomology/Characters.lean:27–103`), and sets `unramifiedLevelInv F Ω n := unramifiedInv F F_n ∘ h2GalUnitsAddEquiv F F_n` (`InvariantDefs.lean:66–74`).
3. On $\operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F)$, `brauerInvFun F Ω x` (`InvariantDefs.lean:76–82`) chooses $(n, y)$ with $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{F_n/F}(y) = x$ when one exists and evaluates `unramifiedLevelInv F Ω n y` (returning $0$ in the `else` branch, which Lemma 3 shows is vacuous), promoted to the additive homomorphism $\operatorname{inv}_F := \operatorname{brauerInv}(F, \Omega) : \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ (`LocalClassFormation/InvariantSplit.lean:130–145`).
4. For a non-archimedean local field $K$, separable closure $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, and open subgroup $U \le \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ with fixed field $K_U := K_{\mathrm{sep}}^U$, the *open-subgroup local invariant* (`localInv K Ksep U`, `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:145–153`) is
   $$
   \operatorname{localInv}_U := \operatorname{brauerInv}(K_U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}) \circ \theta_U^2
   : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}\bigl(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U\bigr) \longrightarrow \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z,
   $$
   where $\theta_U^n$ (`openCohomologyIso U n`, `:106–118`) transports continuous cohomology along $\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U) \xrightarrow{\sim} U$; these assemble into `localClassFormation K Ksep` (`:184–189`) and, after loading the spectral-norm valuation and topology on $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, into `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep` (`:201–207`). [Lean, read] [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-114317-arithmetic-realization-of-the-non-archimedean-local-class-formation-localclassformation)

**Inherited inputs cited without re-proof.** Four upstream results enter at their established scope and assurance levels:
- From §11.4.3.5 (author-level R3 + independent audit passed): unramified local unit-norm surjectivity $\widehat H^0(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$ (`subsingleton_tateZero_integerUnits`, `LocalFields/UnramifiedNorms.lean`).
- From §§11.4.3.9–11.4.3.10 (author-level R3 + §11.4.3.9 same-source audit passed + §11.4.3.10 independent audit `11251045` mathematical items C1–C4 passed + Level-1 calculation PASS): finite-extension multiplicative Hilbert's Theorem 90 (`isMulCoboundary₁_of_isMulCocycle₁_of_aut_to_units`), unit Hilbert 90 $\widehat H^{-1}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$ (`subsingleton_tateNegOne_integerUnits`), and the cyclic local norm index $|\widehat H^0(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times)| = [L : K]$ (`natCard_tateZero_units`, `LocalFields/HerbrandLocal.lean:65–79`).
- From §11.4.3.11 (author-level R3 + same-source audit passed): cyclic Tate $2$-periodicity `tateCohomologyPeriodicIso` (`TateCohomology/Herbrand.lean`).
- From §11.4.3.16 (author-level R3 + independent audit mathematical items 1–4 passed, with the `Section.lean` Zorn transversal and `invFun`/literature fixes incorporated upon merge + Level-1 calculation PASS): `ClassFormation` fundamental classes and all-subgroup Tate hypotheses, `isIso_cohomologyMapAlong_of_mulEquiv`, degree-two inflation–restriction, and the high-degree consumers `subsingleton_cohomology_top_of_three_le` (`SubsingletonCohomologyTopOfThreeLeInt.lean:69–146`) and `subsingleton_cohomology_three` (`FormationThree.lean:30–53`).

**Lemma 1 (open-subgroup–fixed-field dictionary, index–degree identity, and profinite $H^1$ transport).** Let $K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K$ be a Galois extension (`[IsGalois K Ksep]`) with profinite Galois group $G := \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$, and for each open subgroup $U \le G$ put $K_U := \operatorname{fixedField}(U) = K_{\mathrm{sep}}^U$.
1. (*Index–degree dictionary.*) For every open subgroup $U \le G$, $\operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(K_U) = U$, $K_U/K$ is finite-dimensional, and $[G : U] = [K_U : K]$. For any open subgroups $V \le U \le G$, $K_U \subseteq K_V$ and the relative subgroup index (`(V : Subgroup G).relIndex U`, written $[U : V]$ in §11.4.3.16 and sometimes $[V : U]$ in subgroup-lattice notation) equals the field degree:
   $$
   [U : V] = |(U : \operatorname{Subgroup} G) / (V \cap U)| = [K_V : K_U].
   $$
2. (*Topological Galois isomorphism and restriction square.*) Scalar restriction $\sigma \mapsto \sigma|_K$ defines a topological group isomorphism $\phi_U : \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U) \xrightarrow{\sim} U$ (`galFixedFieldEquivOpen U`), inducing natural continuous-cohomology isomorphisms (`openCohomologyIso U n`)
   $$
   \theta_U^n : H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}\bigl(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U\bigr)
   \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} H^n_{\mathrm{cts}}\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U), K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times\bigr)
   \qquad (n \ge 0).
   $$
   For open $V \le U \le G$, $\theta_V^n \circ \operatorname{res}^U_V = \operatorname{res}_{K_V/K_U}^n \circ \theta_U^n$, where $\operatorname{res}_{K_V/K_U}^2 = \operatorname{brauerRes}(K_U, K_V, K_{\mathrm{sep}}) : \operatorname{Br}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U) \to \operatorname{Br}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_V)$ is cochain pullback along $\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_V) \hookrightarrow \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U)$.
3. (*Profinite Hilbert 90 and open-subgroup $H^1$ vanishing.*) For every Galois extension $L/K$, $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times) = 0$ (`isZero_cohomology_one_galUnits`); consequently $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) = 0$ (`isZero_H1_open U`) for every open subgroup $U \le \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$, without any valuation or local-field hypothesis on $K$.

*Proof.* (1) Every open subgroup $U \le G$ is closed, so infinite Galois correspondence (`InfiniteGalois.fixingSubgroup_fixedField` and `InfiniteGalois.isOpen_iff_finite` in `Mathlib/FieldTheory/Galois/Infinite.lean:146–178,241–252`) gives $\operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(K_U) = U$ (`fixingSubgroup_fixedField_openSubgroup`, `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:14–16`), $[K_U : K] < \infty$ (`finiteDimensional_fixedField_openSubgroup`, `:18–22`), and $[G : U] = [G : \operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(K_U)] = [K_U : K]$ (`index_openSubgroup`, `:24–26`). If $V \le U$, any element fixed by $U$ is fixed by $V$, giving $K_U \subseteq K_V$ (`fixedField_le_fixedField`, `:30–32`) and a scalar tower $K \subseteq K_U \subseteq K_V$. Multiplying coset indices $[U : V]\cdot [G : U] = [G : V]$ (`Subgroup.relIndex_mul_index`) and substituting $[G : U] = [K_U : K]$, $[G : V] = [K_V : K] = [K_U : K]\,[K_V : K_U]$ yields $[K_U : K]\,[U : V] = [K_U : K]\,[K_V : K_U]$; cancelling $[K_U : K] > 0$ (`Module.finrank_pos`) gives $[U : V] = [K_V : K_U]$ (`relIndex_eq_finrank`, `:34–44`).

(2) First consider any field tower $F \subseteq F' \subseteq \Omega$: we show that $\operatorname{AlgEquiv.restrictScalarsHom} F : \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F') \to \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F)$ is continuous (`continuous_restrictScalarsHom_of_tower`, `LocalClassFormation/InvariantRes.lean:54–80`). Since it is a group homomorphism between topological groups, continuity at $1$ suffices. A basic Krull neighborhood of $1 \in \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F)$ contains $\operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(S)$ for a finite-dimensional intermediate field $S/F$ inside $\Omega$. Pick a finite $F$-basis $b_1, \ldots, b_d$ of $S$ and adjoin $T := \{b_1, \ldots, b_d\}$ over $F'$ to form $S' := F'(T) \subseteq \Omega$. Each $b_i$ is algebraic over $F$, hence over $F'$, so $[S' : F'] < \infty$ and $\operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(S')$ is an open neighborhood of $1 \in \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/F')$. Any $\sigma \in \operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(S')$ fixes every $b_i$ and is $F$-linear, so $\sigma$ fixes every $\sum_i c_i b_i \in S$ ($c_i \in F$), putting $\sigma|_F \in \operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(S)$!

Specializing to $K \subseteq K_U \subseteq K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, every $\sigma \in \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U)$ fixes $K_U$ pointwise and hence lands in $\operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(K_U) = U$, defining a continuous homomorphism $\phi_U : \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U) \to U$ (`galFixedFieldToOpen U`, `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:46–61`). It is injective by extensionality on $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ and surjective because any $\tau \in U = \operatorname{fixingSubgroup}(K_U)$ is $K_U$-linear (`galFixedFieldToOpen_bijective`, `:62–69`). Since $K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U$ is Galois (`IsGalois.tower_top_of_isGalois`), $\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U)$ is compact (`galois_compactSpace`) while $U \subseteq G$ is Hausdorff (`galois_t2Space`), so the continuous bijection $\phi_U$ has a continuous inverse (`Continuous.homeoOfEquivCompactToT2`, `continuous_galFixedFieldEquivOpen_symm`, `:70–87`). Pairing $\phi_U$ with the identity map on `Additive Ksepˣ` (`galUnitsOpenHom U`, `:93–104`) and invoking `isIso_cohomologyMapAlong_of_mulEquiv` (§11.4.3.16) gives the isomorphism $\theta_U^n$ (`openCohomologyIso U n`, `:106–118`). For $V \le U$, both composites $U \hookleftarrow V \xleftarrow{\phi_V} \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_V)$ and $U \xleftarrow{\phi_U} \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U) \hookleftarrow \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_V)$ act on $x \in K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ by $\sigma \mapsto \sigma(x)$, so functoriality of `cohomologyMapAlong` yields $\theta_V^n \circ \operatorname{res}^U_V = \operatorname{res}_{K_V/K_U}^n \circ \theta_U^n$ (`cohomologyResLE_comp_openCohomologyIso`, `:120–135`; `brauerRes_eq`, `InvariantRes.lean:96–102`).
