(3) For any Galois extension $L/K$ and open normal subgroup $N \trianglelefteq \operatorname{Gal}(L/K)$ with finite Galois fixed field $E = L^N$ (`GaloisModules/FixedField.lean:48–80`), $(L^\times)^N = E^\times$ (`invariantsGalUnitsAddEquiv`, `GaloisCohomology/IsZeroGroupCohomologyQuotientToInvariantsGalAdd.lean:79–103`) and $\operatorname{Gal}(L/K)/N \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Gal}(E/K)$ induce an isomorphism $H^n(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K)/N, (L^\times)^N) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^n(\operatorname{Gal}(E/K), E^\times)$ (`groupCohomologyQuotientGalUnitsIso`, `:120–126`). In degree $1$, the target vanishes by the **inherited** finite-dimensional Hilbert's Theorem 90 (`isZero_groupCohomology_one_galUnits`, `:131–150`). Because every continuous class $z \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times)$ inflates from some open normal quotient (`exists_inflation_eq`, §11.4.3.16), every such $z$ is zero (`isZero_cohomology_of_forall_isZero`, `isZero_cohomology_one_galUnits`, `:34–44,152–157`). Transporting $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U), K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times) = 0$ along $\theta_U^1$ gives $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) = 0$ (`isZero_H1_open`, `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:161–164`). [Lean, read]

**Lemma 2 (unramified local invariant, $[K' : K] = e \cdot f$ scaling, and inflation invariance).** Let $L/K$ be a finite unramified extension of non-archimedean local fields (`[IsUnramified K L]`, meaning $e(L/K) = 1$).
1. (*All-positive-degree unit acyclicity and valuation isomorphism.*) For every integer $q \ge 1$, $H^q(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$. Consequently the normalized valuation sequence $1 \to \mathcal O_L^\times \to L^\times \xrightarrow{\operatorname{ord}_L} \mathbb Z \to 0$ induces an isomorphism $(\operatorname{ord}_L)_* : H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathbb Z)$ (`h2UnitsIsoInt K L`).
2. (*Injectivity and exact range.*) The unramified invariant $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/K} : H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ (`unramifiedInv K L`) is injective with exact image
   $$
   \operatorname{im}\bigl(\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/K}\bigr)
   = \left\{x \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z : [L : K] \cdot x = 0\right\}
   = \mathbb Z \cdot \left[\frac{1}{[L : K]}\right].
   $$
3. (*Base-change scaling and inflation invariance.*) Let $K'/K$ and $L'/K'$ be finite extensions of non-archimedean local fields fitting into commutative field towers $K \subseteq K' \subseteq L'$ and $K \subseteq L \subseteq L'$ with both $L/K$ and $L'/K'$ unramified. Along the restriction homomorphism $\operatorname{galRestrict} : \operatorname{Gal}(L'/K') \to \operatorname{Gal}(L/K)$ ($\sigma \mapsto (\sigma|_K)|_L$) and $L^\times \to (L')^\times$,
   $$
   \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L'/K'}\bigl(\operatorname{res}(x)\bigr)
   = [K' : K] \cdot \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/K}(x)
   \qquad \bigl(x \in H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times)\bigr).
   $$
   In particular, taking $K' = K$ ($[K : K] = 1$) gives inflation invariance $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L'/K}(\operatorname{inf} x) = \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/K}(x)$ along any tower $K \subseteq L \subseteq L'$ of finite unramified extensions.

*Proof.* (1) Because $e(L/K) = 1$, $L/K$ is Galois with cyclic Galois group $\operatorname{Gal}(L/K) \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Gal}(\kappa(L)/\kappa(K))$ generated by the arithmetic Frobenius $\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K}$ of exact order $f(L/K) = [L : K]$ (`isGalois_of_unramified`, `isCyclic_of_unramified`, `orderOf_frobenius`, `zpowers_frobenius`, `LocalFields/UnramifiedGalois.lean:145–212`). On $\mathcal O_L^\times$, **inherited** unramified unit-norm surjectivity (§11.4.3.5) and unit Hilbert 90 (§11.4.3.10) supply $\widehat H^0(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$ and $\widehat H^{-1}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$, which translate to `IsZero` on additive Tate cohomology (`isZero_tateCohomology_zero_of_subsingleton` and `..._neg_one_of_subsingleton`, `LocalClassFormation/Unramified.lean:18–46`). Induction on $n \ge 0$ along **inherited** cyclic $2$-periodicity $\widehat H^{(n-1)+2} \simeq \widehat H^{n-1}$ (§11.4.3.11, `tateCohomologyPeriodicIso`) shows $\widehat H^n(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$ for all $n \ge 0$, hence $H^q(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times) = 0$ for all $q \ge 1$ (`isZero_groupCohomology_of_subsingleton_tate` and `isZero_groupCohomology_integerUnits`, `:47–80`). Since $\sigma \in \operatorname{Gal}(L/K)$ preserves $\operatorname{ord}_L$, $0 \to \mathcal O_L^\times \to L^\times \xrightarrow{\operatorname{ord}_L} \mathbb Z \to 0$ is short exact in $\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb Z}\operatorname{Gal}(L/K)$ (`unitsValuationSES_shortExact`, `LocalClassFormation/IntegerUnitsToUnitsHomApply.lean:39–50`). Vanishing of $H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times)$ and $H^3(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathcal O_L^\times)$ in its long exact sequence makes $(\operatorname{ord}_L)_*$ (`h2Valuation K L`) both monic and epic, hence an isomorphism (`isIso_h2Valuation`, `Unramified.lean:163–188`).

(2) On any finite group $\Gamma$, $H^1(\Gamma, \mathbb Q) = H^2(\Gamma, \mathbb Q) = 0$ (`isZero_groupCohomology_trivial_rat`, by $|\Gamma|$-torsion and unique divisibility of $\mathbb Q$), so the connecting map $\delta : \operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma, \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z) \simeq H^1(\Gamma, \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2(\Gamma, \mathbb Z)$ is an isomorphism (`h2IntAddEquiv`, `TateCohomology/Characters.lean:55–85`). Because $\operatorname{Gal}(L/K) = \langle \operatorname{Frob}_{L/K} \rangle$ is generated by $\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K}$, evaluating a character $\chi : \operatorname{Gal}(L/K) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ at $\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K}$ (`evalCharQZ (frob K L)`) is injective (`evalCharQZ_injective`, `Unramified.lean:88–106`); composing three injective maps proves injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/K}$ (`unramifiedInv_injective`, `:209–211`). Moreover, since $(\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K})^{[L:K]} = 1$, every $x = \chi(\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K})$ in the range satisfies $[L : K] \cdot x = \chi((\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K})^{[L:K]}) = \chi(1) = 0$. Conversely, if $[L : K] \cdot x = 0$ in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, then $x = m \cdot [1/[L : K]]$ for some $m \in \mathbb N$; sending $\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K} \mapsto [1/[L : K]]$ defines a generator `cyclicChar` of $\operatorname{Hom}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)$ (`Characters.lean:128–136`), and lifting $m \cdot \operatorname{cyclicChar}$ across the surjection $\delta^{-1} \circ (\operatorname{ord}_L)_*$ realizes $x$ (`mem_range_unramifiedInv_iff` and `range_unramifiedInv`, `Unramified.lean:213–241`).

(3) Write $e := e(K'/K)$ and $f := f(K'/K)$. Multiplicativity of ramification indices along both sides of the square ($e(L'/K) = e(L/K)\,e(L'/L) = 1 \cdot e(L'/L)$ and $e(L'/K) = e(K'/K)\,e(L'/K') = e \cdot 1$, using the named local-field input `ramificationIndex_tower`) gives $e(L'/L) = e$ (`ramificationIndex_right_eq`, `LocalClassFormation/UnramifiedRes.lean:56–66`). Therefore $\operatorname{ord}_{L'}(a) = e\,\operatorname{ord}_L(a)$ for every $a \in L^\times$ (`ord_algebraMap`), so $(\operatorname{ord}_{L'})_* \circ \operatorname{res} = (m_e)_* \circ (\operatorname{ord}_L)_*$ where $m_e : \mathbb Z \to \mathbb Z$ is multiplication by $e$ along $\rho := \operatorname{galRestrict} : \operatorname{Gal}(L'/K') \to \operatorname{Gal}(L/K)$ (`unitsResH2_comp_h2Valuation`, `:95–107`). Tracing the explicit rational lift $y : \operatorname{Gal}(L/K) \to \mathbb Q$ of $\chi = \delta^{-1}(z)$ (`h2IntAddEquiv_symm_apply_eq_H2_class`, `Characters.lean:87–102`) shows that multiplying integer $2$-cocycles by $e$ and pulling back along $\rho$ sends $\chi \mapsto e \cdot (\chi \circ \rho)$ (`h2IntAddEquiv_map_intMulRes`, `Unramified.lean:123–154`). Meanwhile, $|\kappa(K')| = |\kappa(K)|^f$ (`card_residueField_eq_pow`, `UnramifiedRes.lean:67–72`). For every $\bar y \in \kappa(L)$, mapped injectively into $\kappa(L')$, both $\rho(\operatorname{Frob}_{L'/K'})$ and $(\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K})^f$ raise $\bar y$ to the $|\kappa(K')| = |\kappa(K)|^f$-th power; injectivity of $\operatorname{Gal}(L/K) \to \operatorname{Aut}(\kappa(L))$ (`residueAlgEquiv_injective_of_unramified`, `UnramifiedGalois.lean:151–158`) forces
$$
\rho\bigl(\operatorname{Frob}_{L'/K'}\bigr) = \bigl(\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K}\bigr)^f
$$
(`galRestrict_frob`, `UnramifiedRes.lean:73–94`). Evaluating $e \cdot (\chi \circ \rho)$ at $\operatorname{Frob}_{L'/K'}$ and invoking $e \cdot f = [K' : K]$ (`ramificationIndex_mul_residueDegree`) gives
$$
\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L'/K'}\bigl(\operatorname{res}(x)\bigr)
= e \cdot \chi\bigl((\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K})^f\bigr)
= e \cdot f \cdot \chi(\operatorname{Frob}_{L/K})
= [K' : K] \cdot \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L/K}(x)
$$
(`unramifiedInv_unitsResH2`, `:108–117`), and setting $K' = K$ gives `unramifiedInv_inflation` (`:130–133`). [Lean, read]

**Lemma 3 (finite Galois $H^2$ cardinality, unramified splitting, and bijectivity and scaling of `brauerInv`).** Let $F$ be a non-archimedean local field and $\Omega/F$ a separable closure equipped with a compatible valuative extension topology.
1. (*Exact finite-layer cardinality.*) For every finite Galois extension $E/F$ of non-archimedean local fields, $|H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)| = [E : F]$ (`natCard_groupCohomology_two_galUnits`).
2. (*Unramified splitting of the local Brauer group.*) For every finite Galois intermediate field $E \subseteq \Omega$ of degree $n = [E : F]$ and every $y \in H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)$, there exists $z \in H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(F_n/F), F_n^\times)$ on the degree-$n$ unramified level $F_n = \operatorname{unramifiedLevel}(F, \Omega, n)$ such that $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{F_n/F}(z) = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{E/F}(y)$. Consequently every $x \in \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F)$ inflates from $F_n$ for some $n \ge 1$ (`exists_brauerInflation_unramifiedLevel`).
3. (*Bijectivity and restriction scaling of `brauerInv`.*) The map $\operatorname{brauerInv}(F, \Omega) : \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is a well-defined **bijective** additive homomorphism (`brauerInv_bijective`) satisfying $\operatorname{brauerInv}(\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{F_n/F} y) = \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_n/F}(y)$, and for every finite extension $F'/F$ inside $\Omega$,
   $$
   \operatorname{brauerInv}(F', \Omega)\bigl(\operatorname{brauerRes}(F, F', \Omega)(x)\bigr)
   = [F' : F] \cdot \operatorname{brauerInv}(F, \Omega)(x)
   \qquad \bigl(x \in \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F)\bigr).
   $$

*Proof.* (1) & (2) First prove the **upper divisibility** $|H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)| \mid [E : F]$ by strong induction on $n = [E : F]$ (`natCard_groupCohomology_two_galUnits_dvd_of_finrank`, `LocalClassFormation/OrderBound.lean:99–140`). If $\operatorname{Gal}(E/F)$ is cyclic, **inherited** cyclic $2$-periodicity (§11.4.3.11) and the **inherited** cyclic local norm index (§11.4.3.10) identify $|H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)| = |\widehat H^0(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)| = [E : F]$ (`natCard_groupCohomology_two_galUnits_of_isCyclic`, `OrderCyclic.lean:68–80`). If $\operatorname{Gal}(E/F)$ is not cyclic, finite local Galois groups are solvable (`LocalField.isSolvable_algEquiv`, named `[Lean]` ramification-filtration input), and any non-cyclic solvable group contains a normal subgroup $N \trianglelefteq \operatorname{Gal}(E/F)$ with $N \neq 1$ and $N \neq \operatorname{Gal}(E/F)$ (`exists_normal_ne_bot_ne_top_of_not_isCyclic`, `OrderBound.lean:78–94`: otherwise $G$ would be simple solvable, hence abelian simple, hence cyclic). Because $H^1(N, E^\times) = 0$ by Hilbert 90 (`isZero_groupCohomology_one_res_galUnits`, `:29–33`), degree-two inflation–restriction gives exactness of $0 \to H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E^N/F), (E^N)^\times) \xrightarrow{\operatorname{inf}} H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times) \xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}} H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/E^N), E^\times)$, so $|H^2(E/F)| = |\ker\operatorname{res}|\cdot |\operatorname{im}\operatorname{res}|$ divides $|H^2(E^N/F)|\cdot |H^2(E/E^N)|$, which by the induction hypothesis on $[E^N : F] < n$ and $[E : E^N] = |N| < n$ divides $[E^N : F]\,[E : E^N] = [E : F]$ (`natCard_groupCohomology_two_galUnits_dvd_of_normal`, `:45–73`). In particular $H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)$ is finite (`finite_groupCohomology_two_galUnits`, `:141–145`).

Next establish the **lower bound** $n \le |H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)|$ and **unramified splitting** (`FinrankLeNatCardGroupCohomologyTwoGalUnits.lean:42–168`, `InvariantSplit.lean:24–121`). Let $q := |\kappa(F)| > 1$ and $f := f(E/F) > 0$, so $|\kappa(E)| = q^f$. Inside a separable closure $\Omega$ (using $\overline E$ in `FinrankLeNatCard...lean:82` and $\Omega$ itself in `InvariantSplit.lean:45`), Hensel root lifting (`exists_isPrimitiveRoot_card_pow_sub_one`, `isUnramifiedSubextension_adjoin`, named `[Lean]` inputs) supplies a primitive $(q^{f n} - 1)$-th root of unity $\zeta \in \Omega$ generating an unramified Galois extension $F(\zeta)/F$ of degree $f n$. Form the compositum $M := E \cdot F(\zeta)$ (`E ⊔ F⟮ζ⟯`). Over $E$, $M = E(\zeta)$ is generated by a primitive $(|\kappa(E)|^n - 1)$-th root of unity, so $M/E$ is **unramified** (`ramificationIndex_eq_one_of_adjoin_eq_top`, `UnramifiedGalois.lean:95–106`; `InvariantSplit.lean:58–75`). Meanwhile, since $q^n - 1 \mid q^{f n} - 1$, the degree-$n$ unramified level $L_0 := F_n$ (or $F(\zeta^{(q^{f n}-1)/(q^n-1)})$) sits inside $F(\zeta) \subseteq M$ with $[L_0 : F] = n = [E : F]$ (`InvariantSplit.lean:77–84`). Consider the composite
$$
H^2\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(L_0/F), L_0^\times\bigr)
\xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{inf}^M_{L_0}\;} H^2\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(M/F), M^\times\bigr)
\xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{res}^M_E\;} H^2\bigl(\operatorname{Gal}(M/E), M^\times\bigr).
$$
Because both $L_0/F$ and $M/E$ are unramified, Lemma 2(2)–(3) gives $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{M/E}(\operatorname{res}^M_E(\operatorname{inf}^M_{L_0} w)) = [E : F]\,\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L_0/F}(w) = n\,\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L_0/F}(w) = 0$ (since $[L_0 : F] = n$ annihilates $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L_0/F})$), and injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{M/E}$ forces $\operatorname{res}^M_E \circ \operatorname{inf}^M_{L_0} = 0$ (`unitsInfl_comp_unitsRes_eq_zero`, `FinrankLeNatCard...lean:42–53`). By tower exactness $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_E) = \ker(\operatorname{res}^M_E)$ and injectivity of $\operatorname{inf}^M_{L_0}$ and $\operatorname{inf}^M_E$ (from $H^1 = 0$, `unitsInfl_two_injective` and `range_unitsInfl_two_eq_ker_unitsRes`, `OrderInfRes.lean:194–233`), we obtain the subgroup inclusion inside $H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(M/F), M^\times)$:
$$
\operatorname{im}\!\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^M_{L_0}\bigr) \subseteq \operatorname{im}\!\bigl(\operatorname{inf}^M_E\bigr).
$$
Since $L_0/F$ is cyclic of degree $n$, $|\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_{L_0})| = |H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(L_0/F), L_0^\times)| = n$, giving $n \le |\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_E)| = |H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)|$; combined with $|H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)| \mid n$, this proves $|H^2(\operatorname{Gal}(E/F), E^\times)| = n = [E : F]$, completing (1)! Returning to the inclusion above (`InvariantSplit.lean:89–120`), both $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_{F_n})$ and $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_E)$ are finite subgroups of **equal** cardinality $n$, so $\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_{F_n}) = \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inf}^M_E)$ (`AddSubgroup.eq_of_le_of_card_ge`)! Inflating further to $\Omega$ via $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_M \circ \operatorname{inf}^M_{(-)} = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{(-)}$ (`brauerInflation_unitsInflationOfLE`, `:17–23`) expresses $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_E(y) = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{F_n}(z)$. Since every continuous class $x \in \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F)$ inflates from some finite Galois $E/F$ (`exists_brauerInflation_eq`, `GaloisCohomology/Brauer.lean:65–70`), (2) follows (`exists_brauerInflation_unramifiedLevel`, `InvariantSplit.lean:122–129`).

(3) First note that for any finite normal $E/F$ inside $\Omega$, $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{E/F}$ is injective (`brauerInflation_injective`, `GaloisCohomology/BrauerInflation.lean:197–231`): if $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_E(y) = 0$, colimit exactness (`exists_transition_eq_zero`) kills $y$ upon inflation to some larger finite Galois $F' \supseteq E$, contradicting injectivity of finite-tower degree-two inflation (`unitsInflationOfLE_two_injective`, `:126–130`). Now if $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{F_m}(y) = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{F_n}(z)$, both $F_m$ and $F_n$ lie inside $F_{mn}$ (`unramifiedLevel_le`, `InvariantDefs.lean:51–56`), injectivity of $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{F_{mn}}$ identifies their inflations to $F_{mn}$, and inflation invariance of $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}$ (Lemma 2(3)) gives $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_m/F}(y) = \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_{mn}/F}(\operatorname{inf} y) = \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_{mn}/F}(\operatorname{inf} z) = \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_n/F}(z)$ (`unramifiedLevelInv_eq_of_brauerInflation_eq` and `brauerInvFun_brauerInflation`, `InvariantLevels.lean:23–72`). Additivity of `brauerInv` follows by inflating any two classes from $F_m$ and $F_n$ to the common level $F_{mn}$ (`InvariantSplit.lean:130–145`). Injectivity holds because $\operatorname{brauerInv}(\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{F_n} y) = 0$ implies $\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_n/F}(y) = 0$, hence $y = 0$ by Lemma 2(2) (`brauerInv_injective`, `:151–160`). Surjectivity holds because every $a \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ has finite additive order (`isAddTorsion_QZ`, `CohomologyTwoIntAddEquivQZSymmApply.lean:10–18`), so $n \cdot a = 0$ for some $n \in \mathbb N_+$, putting $a \in \operatorname{im}(\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_n/F})$ by Lemma 2(2) (`brauerInv_surjective`, `InvariantSplit.lean:162–176`).

Finally, let $F'/F$ be a finite extension inside $\Omega$ and $x = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{F_n/F}(y) \in \operatorname{Br}(\Omega/F)$. Because $q_{F'} = q_F^{f(F'/F)}$ (`card_residueField_eq_pow_residueDegree`, `LocalFields/MaximalUnramified.lean:596–602`), $q_F^n - 1$ divides $q_{F'}^n - 1$, so the $(q_F^n - 1)$-th root $\zeta_{F,n}$ is a power of $\zeta_{F',n}$, giving $F_n \subseteq F'_n$ (`unramifiedLevel_le_unramifiedLevel`, `InvariantRes.lean:173–190`). Cochain naturality on the square of field towers $F \subseteq F' \subseteq F'_n \subseteq \Omega$ and $F \subseteq F_n \subseteq F'_n \subseteq \Omega$ yields $\operatorname{brauerRes}_{F'/F}(\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F}_{F_n/F} y) = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega/F'}_{F'_n/F'}(\operatorname{res} y)$ (`brauerRes_brauerInflation`, `:118–169`), and applying `brauerInv` on both sides reduces to Lemma 2(3) (`brauerInv_brauerRes`, `:192–220`):
$$
\operatorname{brauerInv}(F', \Omega)\bigl(\operatorname{brauerRes}_{F'/F}(x)\bigr)
= \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F'_n/F'}\bigl(\operatorname{res}(y)\bigr)
= [F' : F]\,\operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{F_n/F}(y)
= [F' : F]\,\operatorname{brauerInv}(F, \Omega)(x).
$$
[Lean, read]

**Theorem (realization of `localClassFormation`, spectral-norm valuative loading `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure`, and $H^r$ vanishing supply).** Let $K$ be a non-archimedean local field and $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ a separable closure of $K$ (`[IsSepClosure K Ksep]`).
1. (*With a given valuative extension topology on $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$.*) Suppose $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ carries `[ValuativeRel Ksep] [TopologicalSpace Ksep] [IsValuativeTopology Ksep] [ValuativeExtension K Ksep]`. Then every open subgroup $U \le G := \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ has a non-archimedean local fixed field $K_U = K_{\mathrm{sep}}^U$, and the family $\operatorname{localInv}_U = \operatorname{brauerInv}(K_U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}) \circ \theta_U^2$ makes $K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times$ (`galUnits K Ksep`, and over $\mathbb Z$, `galUnitsZ K Ksep`) a full **class formation** (`localClassFormation K Ksep`, `localClassFormationZ K Ksep`):
   - $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) = 0$ for every open $U \le G$;
   - $\operatorname{localInv}_U : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is bijective for every open $U \le G$;
   - for all open subgroups $V \le U \le G$ and $x \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U)$,
     $$
     \operatorname{localInv}_V\bigl(\operatorname{res}^U_V(x)\bigr)
     = [U : V] \cdot \operatorname{localInv}_U(x)
     = [K_V : K_U] \cdot \operatorname{localInv}_U(x).
     $$
2. (*Canonical valuative loading on a bare separable closure.*) Without assuming any prior valuation or topology on $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, the unique ultrametric spectral norm on algebraic $K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K$ endows $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ with `algebraicValuativeRel K Ksep` and `algebraicTopology K Ksep` satisfying `IsValuativeTopology Ksep` and `ValuativeExtension K Ksep`, defining `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep : ClassFormation (galUnits K Ksep)`.
3. (*Supply to the degree-$r \ge 3$ vanishing consumers.*) Feeding `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep` into the abstract class-formation consumers of §11.4.3.16 proves:
   - for every integer $r \ge 3$, $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K), K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times) = 0$ (`HighFormations.subsingleton_cohomology_galUnits_of_three_le`) and $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) = 0$ for every open $U \le \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$;
   - directly on finite layers, $H^3(\operatorname{Gal}(K_V/K_U), K_V^\times) = 0$ for every finite Galois extension $K_V/K_U$ inside $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ and $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U) = 0$ (`FormationThree.subsingleton_cohomology_three` and `Vanishing.subsingleton_cohomology_three_galUnits_open`), which in turn supplies $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \mu_n) = 0$ (`(n : K) ≠ 0`), $\operatorname{cd}_\ell(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)) \le 2$ ($\ell \neq \operatorname{char}(K)$), and $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, M) = 0$ ($r \ge 3$) for discrete $n$-torsion modules $M$ (`Vanishing.lean:48–196`, `FiniteVanishing.lean:16–77`).

*Proof.* **Part (1).** Because $K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K$ is separable algebraic, for any open $U \le G$ Lemma 1(1) gives $[K_U : K] < \infty$, and `intermediateField_isNonarchimedeanLocalField_of_finiteDimensional` (`LocalFields/SeparableClosure.lean:330–351`) identifies the induced valuation and subspace valuative topology (`intermediateField_isValuativeTopology`, `:276–306`) on $K_U$ with the complete local-field structure `finiteExtensionStructure K K_U` (`valuativeRel_eq_of_isAlgebraic`, `:150–156`, and `topologicalSpace_eq_of_isValuativeTopology`, `:245–250`), while `isSepClosure_intermediateField` (`Brauer/BaseChangeRestriction.lean:104–105`) gives `IsSepClosure K_U Ksep`. Thus `brauerInv K_U Ksep` is defined and bijective by Lemma 3(3); composing with the isomorphism $\theta_U^2$ of Lemma 1(2) proves `localInv_bijective` (`LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:154–156`). Open-subgroup $H^1$ vanishing is Lemma 1(3) (`isZero_H1_open`, `:161–164`). For open $V \le U \le G$, inclusion $K_U \subseteq K_V \subseteq K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ is a finite valuative extension (`IntermediateField.valuativeExtension_inclusion`, `SeparableClosure.lean:313–320`), and chaining Lemma 1(2), Lemma 3(3), and Lemma 1(1) gives (`localInv_res`, `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:166–182`):
$$
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{localInv}_V\bigl(\operatorname{res}^U_V(x)\bigr)
&= \operatorname{brauerInv}(K_V, K_{\mathrm{sep}})\bigl(\theta_V^2(\operatorname{res}^U_V x)\bigr) \\
&= \operatorname{brauerInv}(K_V, K_{\mathrm{sep}})\bigl(\operatorname{brauerRes}_{K_V/K_U}(\theta_U^2 x)\bigr) \\
&= [K_V : K_U] \cdot \operatorname{brauerInv}(K_U, K_{\mathrm{sep}})\bigl(\theta_U^2(x)\bigr)
= [U : V] \cdot \operatorname{localInv}_U(x).
\end{aligned}
$$
This inhabits `localClassFormation K Ksep : ClassFormation (galUnits K Ksep)` (`:184–193`), and transporting across `cohomologyULiftIntAddEquiv` (`LocalClassFormation/CupSymbolDefs.lean:15–65`) inhabits `localClassFormationZ K Ksep : ClassFormation (galUnitsZ K Ksep)`.

**Part (2).** Downstream theorems take only `[Field K] [ValuativeRel K] [TopologicalSpace K] [IsNonarchimedeanLocalField K]` and `[Field Ksep] [Algebra K Ksep] [IsSepClosure K Ksep]`, with **no** valuation or topology assumed on $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$. Because $K$ is complete and ultrametric (`normedField_isUltrametricDist`, `normedField_completeSpace`, `FiniteExtension.lean:39–54`) and $K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K$ is algebraic, Mathlib's ultrametric spectral norm (`spectralNorm.nontriviallyNormedField K Ksep`, named `[Lean]` input) equips $K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ with an ultrametric norm extending $\|\cdot\|_K$ (`algebraicNormedField`, `SeparableClosure.lean:49–72`). Setting `algebraicValuativeRel K Ksep := ValuativeRel.ofValuation NormedField.valuation` and `algebraicTopology K Ksep` to its uniform topology (`:173–180`) immediately supplies `ValuativeExtension K Ksep` (from $\|a\|_{K_{\mathrm{sep}}} = \|a\|_K$ on $a \in K$, `:187–197`) and `IsValuativeTopology Ksep` (`:209–219`). Installing those four local instances inside `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure` (`LocalClassFormationAlt.lean:201–207`) invokes `localClassFormation K Ksep`.

**Part (3).** At `ClassFormations/HighFormations.lean:14–18`, `subsingleton_cohomology_galUnits_of_three_le` is the one-line call `(localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep).subsingleton_cohomology_top_of_three_le hr`,
which applies Theorem Part (2) of §11.4.3.16 (`SubsingletonCohomologyTopOfThreeLeInt.lean:136–146`, dividing inflated fundamental classes in $\operatorname{Br}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K_U) \simeq \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ to kill torsion right-hand cup factors) to prove $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K), K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times) = 0$ for all $r \ge 3$ (and `subsingleton_cohomology_of_three_le_int` gives the same on every open $U$). In parallel, at `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/Vanishing.lean:44–46`, `subsingleton_cohomology_three_galUnits_open` calls `(localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep).subsingleton_cohomology_three U` (`FormationThree.lean:30–53`), which uses $H^1(U/N, \mathbb Z) = \operatorname{Hom}(U/N, \mathbb Z) = 0$ (`TateThree.lean:14–70`) to obtain $H^3(U/N, (K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times)^N) = 0$ on every finite Galois quotient without needing deeper division, and then passes along the Kummer exact sequence $1 \to \mu_n \to K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times \xrightarrow{(\cdot)^n} K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times \to 1$ (`(n : K) ≠ 0`, surjective on $K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times$ with $H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, K_{\mathrm{sep}}^\times|_U)[n]$ matched by Kummer surjectivity in `KummerOpen.lean`, named `[Lean]` bridge) to $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, \mu_n) = 0$ (`subsingleton_cohomology_three_rootsOfUnity`, `Vanishing.lean:48–53`). Restricting to a pro-$\ell$ Sylow subgroup $P \le \operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)$ ($\ell \neq \operatorname{char}(K)$), every pro-$\ell$ automorphism fixes $\mu_\ell$ pointwise because $\sigma^{\ell^k}(\zeta) = \zeta$ and $m^{\ell^k} \equiv m \pmod \ell$ (`apply_eq_of_isProPGroup`, `:77–110`), identifying $\mu_\ell|_P \simeq \mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z$ and yielding $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(P, \mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z) = 0$, hence $\operatorname{cd}_\ell(\operatorname{Gal}(K_{\mathrm{sep}}/K)) = \operatorname{cd}_\ell(P) \le 2$ (`cdp_galois_le_two`, `:132–177`) and $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(U, M) = 0$ ($r \ge 3$) for every discrete $n$-torsion $U$-module $M$ with $(n : K) \neq 0$ (`subsingleton_cohomology_of_three_le`, `:179–196`; `FiniteVanishing.lean:16–77`). [Lean, read]

**Hand calculation 1 (unramified quadratic and quartic extensions of $\mathbb Q_p$: explicit $2$-cocycles and $e \cdot f$ scaling).** Let $p$ be an odd prime and $u \in \mathbb Z$ a quadratic non-residue modulo $p$ (concretely $(p, u) = (5, 2)$ or $(3, -1)$). Then $X^2 - u$ is irreducible over $\mathbb F_p$, so $L_2 := \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{u})$ is the unique degree-$2$ unramified extension of $K = \mathbb Q_p$ ($e(L_2/\mathbb Q_p) = 1$, $f(L_2/\mathbb Q_p) = 2$), corresponding to the index-$2$ open normal subgroup $V_{\mathrm{unr},2} := \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/L_2) \trianglelefteq U_0 := \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/\mathbb Q_p)$ ($\Omega = \mathbb Q_{p,\mathrm{sep}}$). In $\kappa(L_2) = \mathbb F_p(\sqrt{\bar u})$, Euler's criterion $u^{(p-1)/2} \equiv -1 \pmod p$ gives $(\bar a + \bar b\sqrt{\bar u})^p = \bar a + \bar b\,\bar u^{(p-1)/2}\sqrt{\bar u} = \bar a - \bar b\sqrt{\bar u}$, so the nontrivial automorphism $\sigma(\sqrt{u}) = -\sqrt{u}$ of $\Gamma := \operatorname{Gal}(L_2/\mathbb Q_p) = \{1, \sigma\} \simeq C_2$ is the arithmetic Frobenius $\operatorname{Frob}_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p}$!

Define the normalized inhomogeneous $2$-cochain $c_{\mathrm{unr},2} : \Gamma \times \Gamma \to L_2^\times$ by
$$
c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(1, 1) = c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(1, \sigma) = c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(\sigma, 1) = 1,
\qquad
c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(\sigma, \sigma) = p.
$$
On all $8$ triples in $\Gamma^3$, $(d^2 c_{\mathrm{unr},2})(g_0, g_1, g_2) = 1$ (at $(\sigma, \sigma, \sigma)$, $\sigma(p)\cdot 1^{-1}\cdot 1\cdot p^{-1} = p\,p^{-1} = 1$). Its square $c_{\mathrm{unr},2}^2$ equals $d^1 h$ for $h(1) = 1, h(\sigma) = p$ since $(d^1 h)(\sigma, \sigma) = \sigma(p)\,1^{-1}\,p = p^2$, whereas $c_{\mathrm{unr},2} = d^1 b$ would require $N_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p}(b(\sigma)) = p$, impossible because $\operatorname{ord}_p(N_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p}(z)) = 2\,\operatorname{ord}_{L_2}(z) \in 2\mathbb Z$ while $\operatorname{ord}_p(p) = 1$. Evaluating `unramifiedInv` step by step as in Lemma 2:
1. Since $e(L_2/\mathbb Q_p) = 1$, $\operatorname{ord}_{L_2}(p) = 1$, so $(\operatorname{ord}_{L_2} \circ c_{\mathrm{unr},2})(g_0, g_1)$ is the integer carry cocycle $v_2(1,1) = v_2(1,\sigma) = v_2(\sigma,1) = 0,\; v_2(\sigma,\sigma) = 1$.
2. Lifting $\chi : \Gamma \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, $\chi(1) = [0], \chi(\sigma) = [1/2]$, to $y : \Gamma \to \mathbb Q$ with $y(1) = 0, y(\sigma) = 1/2$ gives $y(g_0) + y(g_1) - y(g_0 g_1) = v_2(g_0, g_1)$ on all $4$ pairs, so `h2IntAddEquiv` maps $[v_2] \mapsto \chi$.
3. Evaluating at $\operatorname{Frob}_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p} = \sigma$ yields
   $$
   \operatorname{localInv}_{U_0}\!\left(\operatorname{inf}^{U_0}_{\Gamma}[c_{\mathrm{unr},2}]\right)
   = \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p}([c_{\mathrm{unr},2}])
   = \chi(\sigma)
   = \left[\frac{1}{2}\right] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z,
   $$
   identifying $[c_{\mathrm{unr},2}] = u_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p}$.

Now verify the scaling law $\operatorname{localInv}_V(\operatorname{res}^{U_0}_V x) = [U_0 : V]\,\operatorname{localInv}_{U_0}(x)$ across **both** factors of $[K' : \mathbb Q_p] = e \cdot f$, including non-vanishing outputs:
- *Unramified quadratic restriction ($V = V_{\mathrm{unr},2}$, $K' = L_2$, $e = 1, f = 2$, $[U_0 : V] = 2$):* $\operatorname{Frob}_{L_2/L_2} = \mathrm{id}$ restricts to $\sigma^2 = 1$, pulling $c_{\mathrm{unr},2}$ back to $1$ and scaling $[1/2] \mapsto 1 \cdot 2 \cdot [1/2] = [0]$.
- *Totally ramified quadratic restriction ($V = V_{\mathrm{ram},2} := \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/\mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p}))$, $K' = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p})$, $e = 2, f = 1$, $[U_0 : V] = 2$):* $M_2 := L_2 \cdot K' = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p}, \sqrt{u})$ is unramified of degree $2$ over $K'$ with Frobenius $\sigma'(\sqrt{p}) = \sqrt{p},\; \sigma'(\sqrt{u}) = -\sqrt{u}$ restricting to $\sigma^1$ on $L_2$. Pulling $c_{\mathrm{unr},2}$ back to $\operatorname{Gal}(M_2/K') = \{1, \sigma'\}$ gives $c'_{\mathrm{unr}}(\sigma', \sigma') = p = (\sqrt{p})^2$: its valuation $\operatorname{ord}_{M_2}(p) = 2$ doubles the character to $2 \cdot [1/2] = [0]$, and at the $2$-cochain level $b'(1) = 1, b'(\sigma') = \sqrt{p} \in M_2^\times$ satisfies $(d^1 b')(\sigma', \sigma') = \sigma'(\sqrt{p})\cdot 1^{-1}\cdot \sqrt{p} = p = c'_{\mathrm{unr}}(\sigma', \sigma')$, splitting $c'_{\mathrm{unr}}$ identically!
- *Unramified cubic restriction ($K_3/\mathbb Q_p$ unramified of degree $3$, $e = 1, f = 3$, $[U_0 : V_3] = 3$):* in $L_2 \cdot K_3 / K_3$, $\operatorname{Frob}$ restricts on $L_2$ to $\sigma^3 = \sigma$, while $e = 1$ keeps $\operatorname{ord}(p) = 1$, yielding the **nonzero** scaled invariant $3 \cdot [1/2] = [3/2] = [1/2] \neq 0$.
- *Nonzero $[1/4] \xrightarrow{\times 2} [1/2]$ and $[1/4] \xrightarrow{\times 3} [3/4]$ scaling at $p = 5$:* over $\mathbb Q_5$, $2 \in \mathbb F_5^\times$ has multiplicative order $4$, and in $\mathbb F_5[X]/(X^4 - 2)$ we have $X^5 = 2 X$ and $X^{25} = 4 X \neq X$, so $X^4 - 2$ is irreducible over $\mathbb F_5$ and $L_4 := \mathbb Q_5(2^{1/4})$ is the degree-$4$ unramified extension of $\mathbb Q_5$. Letting $i \in \mathbb Z_5^\times$ be the Hensel square root of $-1$ with $i \equiv 2 \pmod 5$, $\sigma_4(2^{1/4}) = i\,2^{1/4}$ lifts $X \mapsto X^5$ and equals $\operatorname{Frob}_{L_4/\mathbb Q_5}$. The fundamental cocycle $c_{\mathrm{unr},4}(\sigma_4^{b_1}, \sigma_4^{b_2}) := 5^{\lfloor(b_1+b_2)/4\rfloor}$ ($0 \le b_1, b_2 < 4$) has invariant $[1/4] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$: restricting to $L_2 = \mathbb Q_5(\sqrt{2})$ ($e=1, f=2$, $\operatorname{Frob}_{L_4/L_2} = \sigma_4^2$) gives $c_{\mathrm{unr},4}(\sigma_4^{2r}, \sigma_4^{2s}) = 5^{\lfloor(r+s)/2\rfloor} = c_{\mathrm{unr},2}$ of invariant $2 \cdot [1/4] = [1/2] \neq 0$; restricting to $E_2 = \mathbb Q_5(\sqrt{5})$ ($e=2, f=1$) doubles $\operatorname{ord}(5) = 2$ to give $2 \cdot [1/4] = [1/2] \neq 0$; and restricting to unramified $K_3/\mathbb Q_5$ ($[K_3 : \mathbb Q_5] = 3$) gives $3 \cdot [1/4] = [3/4] \neq 0$.

**Hand calculation 2 (totally ramified quadratic and quartic extensions of $\mathbb Q_p$: explicit unramified-splitting cochain transfer and scaling).** Retain odd $p$ and non-square unit $u \in \mathbb Z$ modulo $p$ ($(p, u) = (5, 2)$ or $(3, -1)$), and take the Eisenstein totally ramified quadratic extension $E_2 := \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p})$ ($e(E_2/\mathbb Q_p) = 2$, $f(E_2/\mathbb Q_p) = 1$, open subgroup $V_{\mathrm{ram},2} = \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/E_2)$ of index $2$, quotient $\Delta := \operatorname{Gal}(E_2/\mathbb Q_p) = \{1, \tau\} \simeq C_2$ with $\tau(\sqrt{p}) = -\sqrt{p}$). Here $u \notin N_{E_2/\mathbb Q_p}(E_2^\times)$: for any $x + y\sqrt{p} \in E_2^\times$, $\operatorname{ord}_p(x^2) = 2\operatorname{ord}_p(x)$ is even while $\operatorname{ord}_p(p y^2) = 2\operatorname{ord}_p(y) + 1$ is odd, so $\operatorname{ord}_p(x^2 - p y^2) = 0$ forces $x, y \in \mathbb Z_p$, where $x^2 - p y^2 \equiv x^2 \not\equiv u \pmod p$. Consequently the normalized unit $2$-cocycle
$$
c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(1, 1) = c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(1, \tau) = c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(\tau, 1) = 1,
\qquad
c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(\tau, \tau) = u
$$
satisfies $d^2 c_{\mathrm{ram},2} = 1$ and represents the unique nontrivial class $[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}] \in H^2(\Delta, E_2^\times) \simeq \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$.

Notice that $\operatorname{ord}_{E_2}(c_{\mathrm{ram},2}) \equiv 0$, so `brauerInv` **cannot** read the invariant of $[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}]$ directly on $E_2$! Instead, following the proof of Lemma 3(2) (`InvariantSplit.lean:51–120`), pass to the biquadratic compositum $M_2 := E_2 \cdot L_2 = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{p}, \sqrt{u})$, which is unramified over $E_2$ and has Galois group $\operatorname{Gal}(M_2/\mathbb Q_p) = \Delta \times \Gamma = \{1, \tau\} \times \{1, \sigma\} \simeq C_2 \times C_2$. Define the explicit $1$-cochain $\eta_2 : \Delta \times \Gamma \to \mathbb Q(\sqrt{p}, \sqrt{u})^\times \subset M_2^\times$ by
$$
\eta_2(1, 1) = 1,\qquad
\eta_2(\tau, 1) = \frac{1}{\sqrt{u}} = u^{-1}\sqrt{u},\qquad
\eta_2(1, \sigma) = \sqrt{p},\qquad
\eta_2(\tau, \sigma) = -\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{u}} = -u^{-1}\sqrt{p}\sqrt{u}
$$
(at $p = 3, u = -1$, $\eta_2 = (1, -i, \sqrt{3}, i\sqrt{3})$; at $p = 5, u = 2$, $\eta_2 = (1, \frac{1}{2}\sqrt{2}, \sqrt{5}, -\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{10})$). Evaluating $(d^1 \eta_2)(g_1, g_2) = g_1(\eta_2(g_2))\,\eta_2(g_1 g_2)^{-1}\,\eta_2(g_1)$ on the key nontrivial pairs gives:
- at $((\tau, 1), (\tau, 1))$: $\tau(1/\sqrt{u}) \cdot 1^{-1} \cdot (1/\sqrt{u}) = (1/\sqrt{u})^2 = u^{-1} = c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(1,1)\,c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(\tau,\tau)^{-1}$;
- at $((1, \sigma), (1, \sigma))$: $\sigma(\sqrt{p}) \cdot 1^{-1} \cdot \sqrt{p} = (\sqrt{p})^2 = p = c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(\sigma,\sigma)\,c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(1,1)^{-1}$;
- at $((\tau, 1), (1, \sigma))$: $\tau(\sqrt{p}) \cdot (-\sqrt{p}/\sqrt{u})^{-1} \cdot (1/\sqrt{u}) = (-\sqrt{p})(-\sqrt{u}/\sqrt{p})(1/\sqrt{u}) = 1$;
- at $((1, \sigma), (\tau, 1))$: $\sigma(1/\sqrt{u}) \cdot (-\sqrt{p}/\sqrt{u})^{-1} \cdot \sqrt{p} = (-1/\sqrt{u})(-\sqrt{u}/\sqrt{p})\sqrt{p} = 1$;
- at $((\tau, \sigma), (\tau, \sigma))$: $(\tau\sigma)(-\sqrt{p}/\sqrt{u}) \cdot 1^{-1} \cdot (-\sqrt{p}/\sqrt{u}) = (-\sqrt{p}/\sqrt{u})^2 = p\,u^{-1} = c_{\mathrm{unr},2}(\sigma,\sigma)\,c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(\tau,\tau)^{-1}$.

Across all $16$ pairs in $(\Delta \times \Gamma)^2$, we obtain the exact multiplicative cochain identity in $\mathbb Q(\sqrt{p}, \sqrt{u})^\times$:
$$
\operatorname{inf}^{M_2}_{L_2} c_{\mathrm{unr},2}
= \bigl(\operatorname{inf}^{M_2}_{E_2} c_{\mathrm{ram},2}\bigr) \cdot d^1 \eta_2.
$$
Therefore $\operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{E_2}[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}] = \operatorname{inf}^{\Omega}_{L_2}[c_{\mathrm{unr},2}]$ in $\operatorname{Br}(\Omega/\mathbb Q_p)$, so `brauerInv` assigns
$$
\operatorname{localInv}_{U_0}\!\left(\operatorname{inf}^{U_0}_{\Delta}[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}]\right)
= \operatorname{inv}^{\mathrm{unr}}_{L_2/\mathbb Q_p}([c_{\mathrm{unr},2}])
= \left[\frac{1}{2}\right] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z!
$$
Restricting $[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}]$ to $E_2$ kills it by $\operatorname{res}\circ\operatorname{inf} = 0$, while restricting $[c_{\mathrm{ram},2}]$ to $L_2 = \mathbb Q_p(\sqrt{u})$ kills it on $\operatorname{Gal}(M_2/L_2) = \{1, \tau\}$ via $b_L(1) = 1, b_L(\tau) = \sqrt{u} \in L_2^\times$ since $(d^1 b_L)(\tau, \tau) = \tau(\sqrt{u})\sqrt{u} = u = c_{\mathrm{ram},2}(\tau, \tau)$, both matching $2 \cdot [1/2] = [0]$.

To see **nonzero** scaling along a totally ramified tower, set $p = 5$ ($i \in \mathbb Z_5^\times$, $i^2 = -1$, $i \equiv 2 \pmod 5$) and form the cyclic totally ramified degree-$4$ extension $E_4 := \mathbb Q_5(\varpi)$ ($\varpi := 5^{1/4}$, $e(E_4/\mathbb Q_5) = 4, f = 1$), with $\operatorname{Gal}(E_4/\mathbb Q_5) = \langle \tau_4 \rangle \simeq C_4$ acting by $\tau_4(\varpi) = i\,\varpi$. On $\langle \tau_4 \rangle$, define the unit $2$-cocycle $c_{\mathrm{ram},4}(\tau_4^{a_1}, \tau_4^{a_2}) := 2^{-\lfloor(a_1 + a_2)/4\rfloor}$ ($0 \le a_1, a_2 < 4$). In the degree-$16$ compositum $M_4 := E_4 \cdot L_4 = \mathbb Q_5(5^{1/4}, 2^{1/4})$ ($\alpha := 2^{1/4}$, $\sigma_4(\alpha) = i\,\alpha$, $\operatorname{Gal}(M_4/\mathbb Q_5) = \langle \tau_4 \rangle \times \langle \sigma_4 \rangle \simeq C_4 \times C_4$), define the monomial $1$-cochain
$$
\eta_4(\tau_4^a \sigma_4^b) := i^{a b}\,\varpi^b\,\alpha^a \in \mathbb Q(i, 5^{1/4}, 2^{1/4})^\times
\qquad (0 \le a, b < 4).
$$
Because $(\tau_4^{a_1}\sigma_4^{b_1})(\eta_4(\tau_4^{a_2}\sigma_4^{b_2})) = i^{a_2 b_2 + a_1 b_2 + b_1 a_2}\,\varpi^{b_2}\,\alpha^{a_2}$ and $(a_1 + a_2)(b_1 + b_2) \equiv (a_1 + a_2 \bmod 4)(b_1 + b_2 \bmod 4) \pmod 4$, the $i$-powers cancel identically in $(d^1 \eta_4)(\tau_4^{a_1}\sigma_4^{b_1}, \tau_4^{a_2}\sigma_4^{b_2})$, leaving $\varpi^{b_1 + b_2 - ((b_1+b_2)\bmod 4)}\,\alpha^{a_1 + a_2 - ((a_1+a_2)\bmod 4)} = 5^{\lfloor(b_1+b_2)/4\rfloor}\,2^{\lfloor(a_1+a_2)/4\rfloor}$ on all $256$ pairs of $(C_4 \times C_4)^2$! Thus $\operatorname{inf}^{M_4}_{L_4} c_{\mathrm{unr},4} = (\operatorname{inf}^{M_4}_{E_4} c_{\mathrm{ram},4}) \cdot d^1 \eta_4$, proving $\operatorname{localInv}_{U_0}(\operatorname{inf}[c_{\mathrm{ram},4}]) = [1/4]$. Restricting $\operatorname{inf}[c_{\mathrm{ram},4}]$ to the index-$2$ ramified subgroup $V_{\mathrm{ram},2} = \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/\mathbb Q_5(\sqrt{5}))$ ($\operatorname{Gal}(E_4/E_2) = \langle \tau_4^2 \rangle$, $\tau_4^2(\varpi) = -\varpi$) yields $(\operatorname{res} c_{\mathrm{ram},4})(\tau_4^{2r}, \tau_4^{2s}) = 2^{-\lfloor(r+s)/2\rfloor}$ on $E_4/E_2$, whose $V_{\mathrm{ram},2}$-invariant is $[1/2] = 2 \cdot [1/4] \neq 0$.

**Failure boundaries (deleting non-archimedean unramified divisibility, local completeness, or finite residue cyclicity).** Each ingredient of `IsNonarchimedeanLocalField K` is strictly required for `brauerInv_bijective` and $H^r$ ($r \ge 3$) vanishing:
1. *Real closed field $K = \mathbb R$ (archimedean complete, finite separable closure $[\mathbb C : \mathbb R] = 2$ — `brauerInv` surjectivity and $H^4$ vanishing fail).* Over $K = \mathbb R$ with $K_{\mathrm{sep}} = \mathbb C$ and $G = \operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R) = \{1, \tau\} \simeq C_2$ ($\tau(z) = \bar z$), there are no extensions of degree $n \ge 3$. Cyclic $2$-periodicity gives $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R) = H^2(C_2, \mathbb C^\times) \simeq \widehat H^0(C_2, \mathbb C^\times) = \mathbb R^\times / N_{\mathbb C/\mathbb R}(\mathbb C^\times) = \mathbb R^\times / \mathbb R_{>0} \simeq \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$, generated by the quaternion $2$-cocycle $c_{\mathbb R}(\tau, \tau) = -1$. Because $|\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R)| = 2$ whereas $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is infinite, **no map $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ can be surjective** (`brauerInv_bijective` fails). Moreover, because $[1/2] \in \frac{1}{2}\mathbb Z/\mathbb Z$ cannot be divided by $2$ inside $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R)$, the division step $m \cdot \beta = \operatorname{inf} a$ in `HighFormations.subsingleton_cohomology_galUnits_of_three_le` fails at $r = 4$: the $4$-cocycle $c_{\mathbb R, 4}(\tau^{i_0}, \tau^{i_1}, \tau^{i_2}, \tau^{i_3}) := (-1)^{i_0 i_1 i_2 i_3}$ ($i_k \in \{0, 1\}$) defines a **nonzero** class in $H^4(C_2, \mathbb C^\times) \simeq \mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z$, because for *every* (not necessarily normalized) $3$-cochain $h : C_2^3 \to \mathbb C^\times$, direct expansion cancels all unpaired factors in
   $$
   \frac{(d^3 h)(\tau, \tau, \tau, \tau)\cdot \overline{(d^3 h)(1, \tau, 1, 1)}}{(d^3 h)(\tau, 1, \tau, \tau)\cdot (d^3 h)(\tau, \tau, \tau, 1)}
   = \left|\frac{h(\tau, \tau, \tau)\,h(1, \tau, 1)}{h(1, \tau, \tau)\,h(\tau, \tau, 1)}\right|^2 \in \mathbb R_{>0},
   $$
   whereas $c_{\mathbb R, 4}$ evaluates on that four-point ratio to $(-1) \cdot 1 / (1 \cdot 1) = -1 < 0$.
2. *Global field $K = \mathbb Q$ (deleting local completeness — `brauerInv` injectivity fails on $\overline{\mathbb Q}^\times$).* Replace $K = \mathbb Q_p$ by the incomplete global field $K = \mathbb Q$ with multiplicative Galois module $\overline{\mathbb Q}^\times$. Consider the two quadratic $2$-cocycles on $\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb Q(i)/\mathbb Q) = \{1, \rho\}$ sending $(\rho, \rho) \mapsto -1$ and $(\rho, \rho) \mapsto -3$, representing the quaternion classes $(-1, -1)_{\mathbb Q}$ and $(-1, -3)_{\mathbb Q}$ in $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q)[2]$. Neither class is trivial in $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q)$, because after restricting to $\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb C/\mathbb R)$ neither $-1$ nor $-3$ lies in $N_{\mathbb C/\mathbb R}(\mathbb C^\times) = \mathbb R_{>0}$ ($x^2 + y^2 + z^2 > 0$ and $x^2 + 3 y^2 + z^2 > 0$ on $\mathbb R^3 \setminus \{0\}$). Yet the two classes are **distinct** in $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q)$, as seen by restricting to $\mathbb Q_2$: $(-1, -1)_{\mathbb Q_2}$ is non-split because $x^2 + y^2 + z^2 \equiv 0 \pmod 8$ forces $x \equiv y \equiv z \equiv 0 \pmod 2$ (removing common powers of $2$ contradicts primitive solvability), whereas $(-1, -3)_{\mathbb Q_2}$ **splits** because $-7 \equiv 1 \pmod 8$ has a $2$-adic square root $\gamma \in \mathbb Z_2^\times$ by Hensel's lemma, giving $\gamma^2 + 3\cdot 1^2 + 2^2 = -7 + 3 + 4 = 0$ and hence $-3 = \left(\frac{2}{1}\right)^2 + \left(\frac{\gamma}{1}\right)^2 \in N_{\mathbb Q_2(i)/\mathbb Q_2}(\mathbb Q_2(i)^\times)$! Thus $|\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q)[2]| \ge 3$ (in fact $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q)[2]$ is infinite), whereas $|(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[2]| = |\{[0], [1/2]\}| = 2$; by the pigeonhole principle, **no additive homomorphism $\operatorname{Br}(\mathbb Q) \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ can be injective** (global class field theory must replace $\overline{\mathbb Q}^\times$ by the idele class group `classLimitRep`).
3. *Complete discretely valued field with non-quasi-finite residue field $K = \mathbb Q((t))$ (deleting finite/procyclic residue unramified structure — $H^3$ vanishing fails).* Keep $K$ complete under a discrete valuation $\operatorname{ord}_t$, so Hensel's lemma holds, but replace the finite residue field $\mathbb F_q$ by the global field $\kappa(K) = \mathbb Q$, setting $K := \mathbb Q((t))$. Over $K$, $L := \mathbb Q(i, \sqrt{2})((t))$ is a finite **unramified** Galois extension ($e(L/K) = 1$) whose Galois group $\Gamma := \operatorname{Gal}(L/K) \simeq \operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb Q(i, \sqrt{2})/\mathbb Q) \simeq C_2 \times C_2$ is **not cyclic** (violating `isCyclic_of_unramified`). Because $\Gamma$ acts coefficientwise on Laurent series ($\gamma(t) = t$ for all $\gamma \in \Gamma$), $L^\times = \mathcal O_L^\times \times t^{\mathbb Z}$ splits $\Gamma$-equivariantly via $m \mapsto t^m$ and $\operatorname{ord}_t : L^\times \twoheadrightarrow \mathbb Z$, making $H^3(\Gamma, \mathbb Z)$ a direct summand of $H^3(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times)$. Write elements of $\Gamma = C_2 \times C_2$ as $g_k = (a_k, b_k) \in \{0, 1\}^2$ with generators $e_1 = (1, 0), e_2 = (0, 1)$ and $c_2(x, y) = \lfloor(x + y)/2\rfloor$, and set
   $$
   z_3(g_0, g_1, g_2) := c_2(a_0, a_1)\,b_2 - a_0\,c_2(b_1, b_2) \in \{-1, 0, 1\} \subset \mathbb Z.
   $$
   Since $z_3 = d^2_{\mathbb Q}\beta_{\mathbb Q}$ for the rational $2$-cochain $\beta_{\mathbb Q}((a_1, b_1), (a_2, b_2)) = \frac{1}{2} a_1 b_2 \in \mathbb Q$, $d^3 z_3 = 0$ identically on $\Gamma^4$. Yet for **every** (even unnormalized) integer $2$-cochain $\psi : \Gamma^2 \to \mathbb Z$, the five-term shuffle functional cancels all symmetric face terms to an even integer:
   $$
   (d^2 \psi)(e_1, e_1, e_2) - (d^2 \psi)(e_1, e_2, e_1) + (d^2 \psi)(e_2, e_1, e_1) + (d^2 \psi)(0, 0, e_2) + (d^2 \psi)(e_2, 0, 0)
   = 2\bigl(\psi(e_1, e_2) - \psi(e_2, e_1)\bigr) \equiv 0 \pmod 2,
   $$
   whereas $z_3$ evaluates on those five triples to $1 - 0 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 1 \not\equiv 0 \pmod 2$! Pushing $z_3$ along $m \mapsto t^m$ gives $[t^{z_3}] \neq 0$ in $H^3(\operatorname{Gal}(L/K), L^\times)$, directly refuting finite-layer degree-$3$ vanishing (`ClassFormation.subsingleton_groupCohomology_three_quot`, `FiniteVanishing/FormationThree.lean:30–42`). Moreover, over the three-dimensional complete field $K^\sharp := \mathbb C((x))((y))((t))$ whose residue field $\kappa(K^\sharp) = \mathbb C((x))((y))$ has absolute Galois group $\Gamma^\sharp = \widehat{\mathbb Z} \times \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ (two-generator abelian rather than procyclic $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$), inertia $I = \operatorname{Gal}(K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}}/(K^\sharp)^{\mathrm{unr}}) \simeq \widehat{\mathbb Z}$ over the algebraically closed characteristic-$0$ residue field $\overline{\mathbb C((x))((y))}$ satisfies $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(I, (K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}})^\times) = H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(I, (K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}})^\times) = 0$; dimension shifting Lemma 1(2) of §11.4.3.16 once along $0 \to A \to \operatorname{discreteCoind}(A) \to Q \to 0$ makes $\operatorname{inf}_3 : H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma^\sharp, ((K^\sharp)^{\mathrm{unr}})^\times) \hookrightarrow H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}}/K^\sharp), (K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}})^\times)$ injective, and inflating $t^{z_3}$ along $\widehat{\mathbb Z} \times \widehat{\mathbb Z} \twoheadrightarrow C_2 \times C_2$ produces a nonzero class in $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(\operatorname{Gal}(K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}}/K^\sharp), (K^\sharp_{\mathrm{sep}})^\times) \neq 0$ [Expository].

**Remarks: division-algebra-free architecture, named `[Lean]` boundaries, and classical comparison.**
1. *Why the archive's local Brauer construction avoids central simple algebras.* A striking structural feature of `LocalClassFormation/` is that `brauerInv_bijective` and `brauerInv_brauerRes` never invoke Wedderburn's theorem, central simple algebras, or maximal unramified subfields of division algebras (`InvariantRes.lean:3` does import `GroundTheory/Brauer/BaseChangeRestriction.lean`, which supplies the separable-closure instance and transitively imports central-simple algebra files). Instead, `LocalClassFormation/` follows the purely cohomological route of Milne (*Class Field Theory*, v4.03, Ch. III §§1–2, Theorem 1.7, Proposition 1.8, Theorem 2.1, and Lemmas 2.2, 2.5, 2.6, pp. 97–105) [Paper]: establish $|H^2(E/F, E^\times)| \mid [E : F]$ from the cyclic local norm index (§11.4.3.10) and Galois solvability (`OrderBound.lean`), then match the size-$n$ unramified subgroup inside an auxiliary cyclotomic-unramified compositum $M = E(\zeta_{q^{fn}-1})$ (`FinrankLeNatCard...lean`, `InvariantSplit.lean`) to prove both $|H^2(E/F, E^\times)| = [E : F]$ and unramified splitting in one stroke. Similarly, in `UnramifiedRes.lean:108–117` the archive proves $\operatorname{inv}_{L'/K'} \circ \operatorname{res} = [K' : K]\,\operatorname{inv}_{L/K}$ for an arbitrary base change $K'/K$ in a single equation from $e(K'/K)$ on valuations and $f(K'/K)$ on Frobenius, matching Milne III Proposition 1.8 (pp. 100–101), whereas Serre (*Local Fields*, Ch. XIII §3 Proposition 7, pp. 193–194) splits into unramified and totally ramified substeps; Serre's Ch. XIII §3 Exercise 2 (p. 195) recovers the quasi-finite residue structure from a prescribed family of Brauer invariant isomorphisms with restriction scaling. Section 4 Theorem 1 (pp. 195–196) uses the quasi-finite residue structure to obtain the class formation [Paper].
2. *Exact named `[Lean]` boundaries.* Within this subsection, every declaration and proof body in `LocalClassFormationAlt.lean`, `LocalClassFormation/{InvariantRes,InvariantSplit,InvariantLevels,InvariantDefs,Unramified,UnramifiedRes,IntegerUnitsToUnitsHomApply,FinrankLeNatCardGroupCohomologyTwoGalUnits,OrderInfRes,OrderBound,OrderCyclic,OrderScalars,CupSymbolDefs,FiniteVanishing}.lean`, `FiniteVanishing/{FormationThree,Vanishing}.lean`, `ClassFormations/HighFormations.lean`, `GaloisCohomology/{IsZeroGroupCohomologyQuotientToInvariantsGalAdd,BrauerInflation,Brauer,BrauerInflationDefs,UnitsInflationCochainsFHomApply}.lean`, `GaloisModules/{CoeGalRootsOfUnityHomApply,Discrete,FixedField}.lean`, `CohomologyTwoIntAddEquivQZSymmApply.lean`, `TateCohomology/Characters.lean`, `Brauer/BaseChangeRestriction.lean`, and `LocalFields/{SeparableClosure,MaximalUnramified,UnramifiedGalois,Interface}.lean` has been read in full at local R3 (`[Lean, read]`). The deeper local-field and Kummer suppliers invoked across their interfaces remain **named `[Lean]` inputs** outside this subsection's proof-body reading:
   - Mathlib / `LocalFields/FiniteExtension.lean` spectral-norm extension (`spectralNorm.nontriviallyNormedField`, `spectralNorm_extends`, `spectralValue_le_one_iff`, `finiteExtensionStructure_isNonarchimedeanLocalField`); See the dedicated proof-body reading in [§11.4.3.21](#114321-why-finite-extensions-are-local-fields-in-the-same-valuation-topology).
   - Fundamental local degree and ramification-group theorems (`ramificationIndex_mul_residueDegree` $e \cdot f = [L : K]$, `ramificationIndex_tower`, `ord_algebraMap`, and local Galois solvability `LocalField.isSolvable_algEquiv` in `LocalFields/RamificationStructure.lean`);
   - Hensel cyclotomic root existence for unramified subextensions (`exists_isPrimitiveRoot_card_pow_sub_one`, `isUnramifiedSubextension_adjoin` in `LocalFields/UnramifiedExistence.lean` and `UnramifiedRootsOfUnity.lean`);
   - Open-subgroup Kummer degree-three bridge `subsingleton_cohomology_three_rootsOfUnity_open` (`FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean`), downstream local cup reciprocity and its immediate consumers (read in [§11.4.3.18](#114318-local-cup-symbols-and-evaluation-of-artin-reciprocity)), plus full local Tate duality and local Euler characteristics (`LocalTateDuality/*.lean`, `LocalEulerCharacteristic/*.lean`), and the global idele-class realization `globalClassFormation` (`GlobalClassFormation.lean:19–20`). [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, v4.03](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf) · [Source ledger and consumer contract](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-114317-arithmetic-realization-of-the-non-archimedean-local-class-formation-localclassformation)

#### 11.4.3.18 Local cup symbols and evaluation of Artin reciprocity

**Motivation.** [Section 11.4.3.17](#114317-arithmetic-realization-of-the-non-archimedean-local-class-formation-localclassformation) assigns a rational number modulo integers to a Brauer class. Here the class comes with a finite-order Galois character and an element of the base field. Its invariant can then be read from a single automorphism. For unramified characters the valuation determines the answer. A ramified character can detect a unit, so the general calculation has to change fields and take a norm. [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, III Proposition 3.6 and Lemma 3.7](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf)

**Two examples.** Write $[r]$ for the class of $r$ in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. Over $\mathbb Q_5$ choose $i^2=-1$ with $i\equiv2\bmod5$, and put $Y^4=2$ and $X^4=5$. The arithmetic Frobenius of $\mathbb Q_5(Y)$ sends $Y$ to $iY$. Its character taking this generator to $[1/4]$ gives $[1/4]$ on a uniformizer and zero on $i$. The character of the totally ramified field $\mathbb Q_5(X)$ taking $X\mapsto iX$ to $[1/4]$ gives $[3/4]$ on $i$. We will compute the cocycles and identify the corresponding Artin images by norms.

**Definition (character class and cup symbol).** Let $K$ be a non-archimedean local field and $\Omega=K^{\mathrm{sep}}$. Use the Krull topology on $G_K=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/K)$ and discrete coefficients, with the natural action on $\Omega^\times$ and the trivial action in $0\to\mathbb Z\to\mathbb Q\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\to0$. For a continuous homomorphism $\chi:G_K\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ put
$$
\operatorname{charClass}(\chi)=\delta\chi,\qquad
C_K(\chi,a)=a\cup\delta\chi\in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K,\Omega^\times)
\quad(a\in K^\times).
$$
Throughout, the invariant and its restriction law are those of §11.4.3.17. The first cup factor is an invariant in degree zero. Lean uses the additive representation `galUnitsZ`, inserts `unitsInvariant` via the degree-zero isomorphism, cups on the right, and applies the tensor right unitor to obtain `galCupClass`. Its `charClassProf` is the continuous connecting construction, and `charClass` is the finite-group version via the inverse of `h2IntAddEquiv`. [Lean, read] `CyclicCupGaloisDefs.lean:35–40,48–66`, `CyclicCupDefs.lean:24–33`.

**Lemma 1 (positive carry).** The character has finite cyclic image. If $r(g)\in[0,1)$ is the rational representative of $\chi(g)$, the cocycles for $\delta\chi$ and $C_K(\chi,a)$ are
$$
c_\chi(g,h)=r(g)+r(h)-r(gh)=\lfloor r(g)+r(h)\rfloor,\qquad
(g,h)\longmapsto a^{c_\chi(g,h)}.
$$
They pull back from the finite quotient $G_K/\ker\chi$, with a coefficient inclusion for the second cocycle. The symbol is a homomorphism in each variable and commutes with restriction.

*Proof.* Compactness and discreteness make the image finite. A common denominator embeds it in a finite cyclic subgroup of $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. The kernel is open and normal. The differential of the rational lift is $r(h)-r(gh)+r(g)$. It is integer valued by additivity of $\chi$, and $r(gh)$ is the fractional part of $r(g)+r(h)$. It is continuous and is a cocycle by $d^2d^1=0$. Changing the lift adds the integer coboundary of the difference. The invariant $a$ acts in the degree-zero cup through the equivariant coefficient map $m\mapsto a^m$, giving the second cocycle. Linearity of the connecting homomorphism and of cup gives additivity in the character. Multiplying field elements multiplies their cocycles. Pullback commutes with taking representatives, which proves restriction compatibility and the quotient description. [Lean, read] `CyclicCup.lean:30–66,76–114`, `CyclicCupProf.lean:74–122`, `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbolNorm.lean:20–71`.

**Lemma 2 (unramified evaluation).** Suppose $\psi$ kills inertia $I_K$. For any $\sigma\in G_K$ of unramified degree $1$ and any $a\in K^\times$,
$$
\operatorname{inv}_K C_K(\psi,a)=v_K(a)\psi(\sigma).
$$
The degree homomorphism has target $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$, with $1$ acting on the residue algebraic closure by arithmetic Frobenius $x\mapsto x^{|k_K|}$.

*Proof.* If the image has order $n\ge1$, the induced homomorphism on $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ kills $n\widehat{\mathbb Z}$. It descends to the unramified layer $L/K$ of degree $n$, including $L=K$ when $n=1$. Inflation takes the finite cocycle $a^{c_{\psi_L}}$ to the cocycle in Lemma 1. The inherited invariant on this layer first applies $v_L$ to the cocycle. It then applies the inverse connecting isomorphism and evaluates at arithmetic Frobenius. The valuation gives $v_L(a)c_{\psi_L}$, with $v_L(a)=v_K(a)$ because the ramification index is $1$. The inverse connecting isomorphism gives $v_K(a)\psi_L$. The restriction of $\sigma$ to $L$ is arithmetic Frobenius, giving the assertion. The archive constructs the layer with `charIndex`, descends the character using `charRestrict` and compares both inflated cochains pointwise after scalar transport. [Lean, read] `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbolUnramified.lean:19–48,56–94,104–165,178–232`.

**Lemma 3 (norm and restriction).** Let $E/K$ be finite separable inside $\Omega$. For $b\in E^\times$ and $a\in K^\times$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{inv}_K C_K(\chi,N_{E/K}b)
 &=\operatorname{inv}_E C_E(\chi|_{G_E},b),\\
\operatorname{inv}_E C_E(\chi|_{G_E},a)
 &=[E:K]\operatorname{inv}_K C_K(\chi,a).
\end{aligned}
$$

*Proof.* Put $H=G_E$. In degree zero corestriction multiplies the conjugates over the embeddings of $E$ in $\Omega$, giving $N_{E/K}$. The projection formula gives $\operatorname{cor}_H^{G_K} C_E(\chi|_H,b)=C_K(\chi,N_{E/K}b)$. To see it on cochains, choose representatives $t$ for the left cosets $G_K/H$ and write $gt=t_g u(g,t)$ with $u(g,t)\in H$. The factor of the corestriction at $(g,h)$ for the representative $t$ is
$$
t_{gh}(b)^{c_{\chi|_H}(u(g,t_h),u(h,t))}.
$$
Here $t_h$ represents $htH$ and $t_{gh}$ represents $ghtH$. Put $k(g,t)=r(u(g,t))-r(g)-r(t)+r(t_g)\in\mathbb Z$. Since $u(gh,t)=u(g,t_h)u(h,t)$, the exponent is
$$
c_\chi(g,h)+k(g,t_h)+k(h,t)-k(gh,t).
$$
Because $H$ fixes $b$, the extra factors give the coboundary of $B(g)=\prod_t t_g(b)^{k(g,t)}$. The common exponent contributes $(\prod_t t(b))^{c_\chi(g,h)}$. The coset action and the lift of $\chi$ are locally constant, so the cochains are continuous. This argument also works for nonnormal $E/K$.

For the invariant of corestriction, put $d=[E:K]$. Multiplication by $d$ on $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is surjective. By the inherited invariant bijections and restriction scaling, any degree-two class $y$ over $E$ is the restriction of some $x$ over $K$: take the invariant of $x$ to be any additive $d$-th preimage of $\operatorname{inv}_E(y)$ and use injectivity over $E$. Then $\operatorname{cor}y=dx$ by the restriction–corestriction identity. Its invariant over $K$ is $d\operatorname{inv}_K(x)=\operatorname{inv}_E(y)$. This proves the first formula. Lemma 1 and restriction scaling give the second.

In the archive, the fixed multiplicative module becomes an additive module and the product of conjugates becomes a sum. The comparison with $G_E$ uses the fixed-field group isomorphism and the same underlying element of $\Omega^\times$. The invariant `brauerInvZ` is the inherited invariant after undoing `cohomologyULiftIntAddEquiv`. [Lean, read] `ClassFormations/Corestriction.lean:31–71`, `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbolNorm.lean:75–116,126–239`, `CupSymbolDefs.lean:15–64`, `CupSymbolPrime.lean:20–48,62–91,120–215`.

**Theorem (Artin evaluation).** Use the archive's `localArtinMap`, denoted $\operatorname{Art}_K:K^\times\to\operatorname{Gal}(K^{\mathrm{ab}}/K)$. For every continuous $\chi:G_K\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, every $a\in K^\times$ and every $g\in G_K$ lifting $\operatorname{Art}_K(a)$,
$$
\boxed{\operatorname{inv}_K C_K(\chi,a)=\chi(g)=\chi(\operatorname{Art}_K(a)).}
$$
The last expression evaluates the descended character. With $M=\Omega^{\ker\chi}$ the kernel criterion is
$$
C_K(\chi,a)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad a\in N_{M/K}(M^\times).
$$
Here $M/K$ is finite cyclic and can be trivial. The statement includes the zero character and all residue characteristics and character orders. [Lean, read] `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbol.lean:60–163,173–177`.

*Proof.* The finite restriction of `localArtinMap` to $M$ is the inverse of the archive's finite reciprocity isomorphism from $\operatorname{Gal}(M/K)$ to $K^\times/N_{M/K}(M^\times)$. We use its defining rule for a Frobenius lift. [Lean, read] `LocalFields/LocalArtinMap.lean:26–48`, `LocalFields/AbstractLocalReciprocity/ReciprocityMap.lean:154–180`.

Let $U=\ker\chi$ and $I=I_K$. Choose $s$ representing $\operatorname{Art}_K(a)|_M$ with positive integral unramified degree $m$. Indeed, the degree map $d$ takes $U$ onto $f\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ for $f=f(M/K)$ by the residue-degree description. For any representative $\tau$, choose a positive $m$ congruent to $d(\tau)$ modulo $f$ and multiply $\tau$ by an element of $U$ of degree $m-d(\tau)$. Set
$$
S=\overline{\langle s,U\cap I\rangle},\qquad E=\Omega^S .
$$
The subgroup $U\cap I$ is normal. In $G_K/(U\cap I)$ the map from $\widehat{\mathbb Z}$ taking $1$ to the image of $s$ has image $S/(U\cap I)$. Its composite with the degree map induced by $d$ is multiplication by $m$, which is injective on $\widehat{\mathbb Z}=\prod_\ell\mathbb Z_\ell$. Thus $S\cap I=U\cap I$ and $d(S)=m\widehat{\mathbb Z}$. The group $S\cap U$ equals $U\cap d^{-1}(d(S\cap U))$ since $U\cap I\subseteq S$. It contains $s^{[G_K:U]}$, so its degree image, compact as the continuous image of a closed subgroup of $G_K$, is a closed subgroup containing the open subgroup $m[G_K:U]\widehat{\mathbb Z}$. Thus $S\cap U$ is open in $U$ and $S$ is open in $G_K$. The relative degree of $s$ over $E$ is $1$ and $\chi|_S$ kills inertia over $E$. These are the conditions named `field_inf_ker` and `relDeg_field`. [Lean, read] `LocalFields/AbstractLocalReciprocity/Frobenius.lean:74–81`, `Reciprocity.lean:58–92`, `ReciprocityMap.lean:19–44`, `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbolPrime.lean:141–215`.

Let $\pi_E$ be a uniformizer of $E$. The defining rule for inverse reciprocity sends the class of $s$ to the class of $N_{E/K}\pi_E$. The choice of uniformizer does not affect the norm coset: $G_{EM}=S\cap U$ contains inertia over $E$, so a quotient of uniformizers is a unit norm from the unramified extension $EM/E$. Its norm to $K$ belongs to $N_{M/K}(M^\times)$ by transitivity. We therefore have
$$
a=N_{E/K}\pi_E\cdot N_{M/K}b\qquad\text{for some }b\in M^\times.
$$
The archive writes the second factor as the norm of an inverse, which gives the same set of witnesses since inversion preserves the norm group. Lemma 3 transports the first factor to $E$, and the second to $M$ where the character restricts to zero. Since $v_E(\pi_E)=1$, Lemma 2 now gives
$$
\operatorname{inv}_K C_K(\chi,a)
 =\operatorname{inv}_E C_E(\chi|_S,\pi_E)=\chi(s).
$$
The lifts $g$ and $s$ agree on $M$, so $\chi(g)=\chi(s)$. The descended character on $\operatorname{Gal}(M/K)$ is injective, the finite Artin map has the norm group as kernel, and the inherited invariant is injective. These give the kernel criterion. [Lean, read] `LocalFields/AbstractLocalReciprocity/Reciprocity.lean:106–172`, `ReciprocityMap.lean:154–180`, `LocalFields/LocalArtinMap.lean:43–56`, `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbol.lean:90–163`.

**Remarks on the lift and sign.** The supplied `liftNormal` extends the Artin automorphism from $K^{\mathrm{ab}}$ to $\Omega$. Its choice is immaterial since $\chi$ factors through $M\subseteq K^{\mathrm{ab}}$. The explicit-$g$ declaration asks for pointwise agreement on $K^{\mathrm{ab}}$. Agreement just on $M$ suffices in the mathematical proof. `CupSymbolSep.lean:14–38` installs the canonical valuative relation and topology on a bare separable closure and forwards the theorem with the same agreement hypothesis.

The archive sends a uniformizer to arithmetic Frobenius on the unramified extension. If one defines a geometric Artin map by inverting the archive's map, the formula for the same invariant and connecting class is $-\chi(\operatorname{Art}^{\mathrm{geom}}_K(a))$. The global statement compared in the Chapter 18 table uses a geometric normalization, so its direction must be converted before using this local formula. [Lean, read] `LocalFields/LocalArtinMap.lean:79–94`, `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbol.lean:173–177`. [Serre, *Local Fields*, XI §3 Proposition 2 and Appendix, XIII §4 Propositions 10 and 13](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4757-5673-9)

**Calculation 1 (unramified degree four).** Over $K=\mathbb Q_5$, Hensel's lemma gives the unique square root $i$ of $-1$ with residue $2$. For a root $\bar Y$ of $T^4-2$ over $\mathbb F_5$, one has $\bar Y^{5^j}=2^j\bar Y$. Each root has orbit length $4$ since $2$ has order $4$, so the polynomial is separable and irreducible over $\mathbb F_5$. The lift $L=K(Y)$ is unramified of degree four. The automorphism $\sigma(Y)=iY$ reduces to fifth powers. Take $\psi(\sigma)=[1/4]$. Write $D(b,c)=\lfloor(b+c)/4\rfloor$ for $0\le b,c<4$. The cocycles for $a=5,i,5^3i$ are respectively $5^{D(b,c)},i^{D(b,c)},(5^3i)^{D(b,c)}$. Applying $v_L$ and the inverse connecting map gives $\psi,0,3\psi$, yielding $[1/4],0,[3/4]$ on $\sigma$. The Artin images are $\sigma,1,\sigma^3$ by the unramified Artin rule.

There is also an explicit norm for the unit. We have $N_{L/K}Y=-2$ and $i/(-2)^3$ has residue $1$, so it has a fourth root $w$ in $K$ by Hensel. Thus $N_{L/K}(wY^3)=i$. The coboundary of $\sigma^j\mapsto\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}\sigma^k(wY^3)$ gives the unit cocycle, since multiplying the two products joins consecutive factors and wraps once precisely when $b+c\ge4$.

**Calculation 2 (totally ramified degree four, tested on a root of unity).** Let $R=K(X)$ with $X^4=5$. Eisenstein gives degree four and $X$ is a uniformizer. All four roots $i^jX$ lie in $R$, so $R/K$ is cyclic with generator $\tau(X)=iX$. Take $\chi(\tau)=[1/4]$. Its class on $i$ has cocycle $i^{D(a,c)}$ in the exponents of $\tau$. Every unit norm has fourth-power residue because the four conjugates have the same residue. A preimage of a norm of valuation zero must be a unit since $v_K(N_{R/K}z)=v_R(z)$. Thus $i$ is outside the norm group.

Compute the invariant in $P=RL$. Total ramification and unramifiedness give $R\cap L=K$, and the two commuting generators act on $X,Y$ separately. Put
$$
\eta(\tau^a\sigma^b)=i^{ab}X^bY^a,\qquad 0\le a,b<4.
$$
On $(g,h)=(\tau^a\sigma^b,\tau^c\sigma^d)$ the phase of $g\eta(h)\eta(g)/\eta(gh)$ is $cd+ad+bc+ab-(a+c)(b+d)\equiv0\pmod4$. The remaining powers give
$$
(d^1\eta)(g,h)=5^{D(b,d)}2^{D(a,c)}.
$$
Thus the ramified cocycle for $1/2$, multiplied by $d^1\eta$, is the unramified cocycle for $5$. Its invariant is $[1/4]$. The ratio $2/i$ has residue $1$ and is a fourth power in $K$ by Hensel. A fourth power $w^4$ contributes the coboundary of $\tau^a\mapsto w^a$. It follows that the cup on $i$ is the negative of the cup on $1/2$, with invariant $[3/4]$.

We can identify the Artin image independently of the cup. The fixed field of $\langle\tau\sigma\rangle$ in $P$ is $E=K(Z)$ for $Z=X/Y$: the polynomial $T^4-5/2$ is Eisenstein, so $Z$ gives the full fixed field and is a uniformizer. Over $E$, the extension $P=EL$ is unramified of degree four and $\tau\sigma$ induces fifth powers on its residue field. A degree-one lift over $E$ therefore has image $\tau$ on $R$. By the inverse reciprocity rule its Artin class over $K$ is represented by $N_{E/K}Z=-5/2$. More explicitly, the subgroup constructed from this lift and the inertia in $G_R$ has fixed field $E$: it is contained in $G_E$, has the same inertia as $G_E$ because $P/E$ is unramified, and maps onto the degree quotient of $G_E$. Since $N_{R/K}X=-5$, dividing the two norms gives $\operatorname{Art}_K(1/2)|_R=\tau$. The ratio $2/i$ is a fourth power and its Artin image on $R$ is trivial, so $\operatorname{Art}_K(i)|_R=\tau^{-1}$. Evaluation gives $[3/4]$. This shows how a uniformizer norm can represent the Artin class of a unit.

**Strict failures.** With $\psi$ from Calculation 1 and $a=5$, replacing arithmetic Frobenius by its inverse gives $[3/4]$, distinct from the invariant $[1/4]$. To test omission of the norm, take $E=K(Y^2)$, the unramified quadratic subfield of $L$, and $b=5\in K^\times\subset E^\times$. The right side of the norm formula is $v_E(5)\psi(\sigma^2)=[1/2]$. The base value on $N_{E/K}(5)=25$ is also $[1/2]$, while the base value on $5$ itself is $[1/4]$. Deleting the unramified-character hypothesis in Lemma 2 also fails: $v_K(i)=0$ while Calculation 2 gives $[3/4]$.

**The real place.** The norm from $\mathbb C^\times$ has image $\mathbb R_{>0}$. Cyclic periodicity identifies $H^2(C_2,\mathbb C^\times)$ with $\mathbb R^\times/\mathbb R_{>0}$ and sends the carry class on $a$ to the image of $a$. Writing $s$ for conjugation, a normalized two-cocycle has only one possibly nonidentity value, at $(s,s)$. The cocycle equation on $(s,s,s)$ says this value is fixed by conjugation. If two such cocycles differ by the coboundary of $b$, evaluating at $(1,1)$ gives $b(1)=1$. The coboundary at $(s,s)$ is then $b(s)\overline{b(s)}$. Every positive real number arises this way, so the carry class corresponds to the coset of $a$ modulo the norms. The nontrivial character takes conjugation to $[1/2]$. Its cup invariant is $0$ for positive $a$ and $[1/2]$ for negative $a$, in agreement with the sign Artin map. The trivial character always gives zero. `CupSymbolReal.lean` proves a separate formula for finite Galois extensions of degree at most two. Its `quadraticInv` uses the hypothesis that the product of two nonnorms is a norm to make the rule assigning $[1/2]$ to a nonzero class additive. The hypothesis holds over $\mathbb R$ by signs. The real formula belongs to the infinite-place branch of `GlobalClassFormation/CupLocal.lean`. The finite-place branch uses `CupSymbolSep`. Inversion fixes every element of a group of order two. [Lean, read] `LocalClassFormation/CupSymbolReal.lean:143–210,268–311`.

**Where local Tate duality uses the symbol.** For an open subgroup $U$, set $F=\Omega^U$. With $n$ nonzero in $F$, `LocalTateDuality/KummerCup.lean:193–252` constructs $\chi_z$ from a $\mathbb Z/n$-valued $1$-cocycle $z$ by mapping $j\mapsto[j/n]$ on the fixed-field Galois group. After the inclusion of roots of unity into $\Omega^\times$, it compares the cup of $z$ with the Kummer connecting class of $a\in F^\times$ to the integer carry cocycle of $C_F(\chi_z,a)$ by the Bockstein identity. In degrees $(1,0)$ the connecting identity contributes $(-1)^{1+1}=1$. The Bockstein of $z$ is the differential of an integer lift divided by $n$. Dividing the lift by $n$ before taking its differential gives the rational lift used for $\chi_z$. To see the sign on cochains, use additive notation for $\Omega^\times$, choose an integer lift $\lambda$ of $z$ and a Kummer lift $A$ of $a$, so $d\lambda=nc$ and $nA=a$. The inclusion sends the Kummer cup to the cochain $\lambda\cup dA$. The Leibniz rule gives $d(\lambda\cup A)=c\cup a-\lambda\cup dA$. In degree zero, invariance of $a$ makes $c\cup a=a\cup c$ under the coefficient pairing. Thus the two representatives give the same class. The actual call to `CyclicCupBrauer.lean:199–203` supplies the norm criterion. Injectivity of the Kummer degree-two inclusion gives the equivalence before inclusion. The criterion feeds both annihilator arguments in `LocalTateDuality/EqZeroOfForallCupPairingZmodMulEqZeroLeft.lean:68–115`, using norm detection for characters on the left and norm separation for non-$n$-th powers on the right. These calls use vanishing of the symbol. The signed numerical theorem is called directly at `GlobalClassFormation/CupLocal.lean:67` in the finite-place branch.

The Kummer norm criterion and its two annihilator calls feed [§11.4.5](#1145-from-two-zero-annihilators-to-a-perfect-local-cup-pairing). That section uses the same fixed field and coefficient action to construct the finite perfect pairing.

[Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-114318-local-cup-symbols-and-evaluation-of-artin-reciprocity)

#### 11.4.3.21 Why finite extensions are local fields in the same valuation topology

**Motivation.** The local invariant in [§11.4.3.17](#114317-arithmetic-realization-of-the-non-archimedean-local-class-formation-localclassformation) is used over the fixed field of each open subgroup of the absolute Galois group. That fixed field already has a topology inherited from the separable closure. To use it as a local field, we need to know that this is the topology in which it is complete and locally compact.

The quadratic fields used in §11.4.3.17 illustrate two possibilities. In $\mathbb Q_3(i)$, where $i^2=-1$, the element $3$ is still a uniformizer and the residue field grows. In $\mathbb Q_5(\sqrt5)$, the residue field stays $\mathbb F_5$ and $5$ becomes a uniformizer squared. In both cases the base embeds isometrically, even though the normalized integer order in the second field assigns $5$ the order two.

**Definitions.** Let $K$ be complete for a nontrivial non-Archimedean multiplicative absolute value $|\cdot|_K$, with distance $|x-y|_K$. For a monic polynomial $P(T)=T^d+\sum_{j<d}a_jT^j$ of positive degree, set
$$
s(P)=\max_{0\le j<d}|a_j|_K^{1/(d-j)}.
$$
Set $s(1)=0$ for the monic constant polynomial. The *spectral norm* of an algebraic element $x$ is $s(x)=s(\operatorname{minpoly}_K(x))$. An algebra norm on an extension field is a definite $K$-vector-space norm $A$ with $A(xy)\le A(x)A(y)$. It is power-multiplicative if $A(x^n)=A(x)^n$ for every positive integer $n$. We write $K\subseteq L$ for the given field embedding.

**Theorem (the finite local extension).** For every finite field extension $L/K$, the spectral norm is the unique multiplicative absolute value extending $|\cdot|_K$. It is ultrametric and the embedding of $K$ is isometric. For any $K$-basis of $L$, the coordinate identification with $K^{[L:K]}$ and its inverse are uniformly continuous for the spectral metric and the maximum metric. In particular $L$ is complete. If $K$ is locally compact in its given valuation topology, all spectral closed balls in $L$ are compact, and $L$ is a non-Archimedean local field. If $L$ is an intermediate field of an algebraic valued extension with the original comparisons on $K$ and a compatible valuative topology, the extending valuation comparisons and topology on $L$ agree with the inherited ones. The rank-one realization of the base in [§15.4.3.13](15-two-proofs-side-by-side.md#154313-the-canonical-discrete-value-group-of-a-local-field) gives the absolute value for this application without changing its comparisons or topology.

**Lemma 1 (finite-dimensional norm comparison).** Over $K$, any vector-space norm $A$ on a finite-dimensional space is bounded above and below by positive multiples of the maximum coordinate norm in any basis.

*Proof.* The triangle inequality and scalar homogeneity give the upper bound. The reverse bound is immediate on a line and vacuous in dimension zero. For the induction step let $W$ span all the basis vectors except the last, $u$. By induction and completeness of $K$, $W$ is complete for $A$ and hence closed. Its distance $\delta$ from $u$ is positive. For $w\in W$, $A(w+au)\ge |a|_K\delta$, by rescaling when $a\ne0$. Also $A(w)\le A(w+au)+|a|_K A(u)$. These inequalities and the induction estimate on $W$ bound every coordinate by a constant times $A(w+au)$. Applying the estimates to differences proves the uniform comparison. [Pinned finite-dimensional argument][ch11-ext-dim] · [Sutherland, Proposition 10.3][ch11-ext-suth]

**Lemma 2 (the spectral algebra norm).** For an algebraic extension of any non-Archimedean field, the spectral norm is an ultrametric power-multiplicative algebra norm extending the base absolute value. This step uses neither completeness nor nontriviality.

*Proof.* For a finite extension $F/K$, choose a basis containing $1$ and let $g$ be its maximum coordinate norm. The maximum of the absolute values of the finitely many multiplication structure constants bounds $g(xy)/(g(x)g(y))$ for $x,y\ne0$, by the strong triangle inequality. Define
$$
b(x)=\sup_{z\ne0}\frac{g(xz)}{g(z)},\qquad
A(x)=\lim_{n\to\infty}b(x^n)^{1/n}.
$$
The supremum is finite and at least $g(x)$ by evaluation at $1$. The norm $b$ has the strong triangle inequality and scalar homogeneity, $b(1)=1$, and $b(xy)\le b(x)b(y)$ by composition of multiplication operators. For $x\ne0$, the roots in the definition of $A$ converge to their infimum. For fixed $m>0$, write $n=qm+r$ with $0\le r<m$. The bound $b(x^n)\le b(x^m)^qb(x^r)$ makes the upper limit at most $b(x^m)^{1/m}$. For $x=0$ the roots are zero. Taking limits gives homogeneity and submultiplicativity. Passing to multiples of a positive integer proves power multiplicativity. Definiteness follows from $A(1)=1$ and $1\le A(x)A(x^{-1})$ for $x\ne0$.

For the strong triangle inequality choose $R>\max(A(x),A(y))$, with $R>0$. Convergence gives constants $C_x,C_y\ge1$ with $b(x^j)\le C_x R^j$ and $b(y^j)\le C_yR^j$ for every $j\ge0$. Integral binomial coefficients have absolute value at most one. Expanding $(x+y)^n$ and using the strong triangle inequality and submultiplicativity of $b$ gives $b((x+y)^n)\le C_xC_y R^n$. Take roots, pass to the limit and decrease $R$ to the maximum. This gives the auxiliary norm of the finite normal argument. [Pinned basis construction][ch11-ext-aux] · [Bosch–Güntzer–Remmert, §§1.2.1 and 1.3.2][ch11-ext-bgr]

For such an $A$, a root $z$ of a monic $P$ has $A(z)\le s(P)$. Otherwise $A(z^d)=A(z)^d$ exceeds $A(a_jz^j)$ for every $j<d$, contradicting the polynomial equation. When $P$ splits, let $M$ be the maximum of $A$ on its roots, with multiplicities included. The elementary symmetric expressions for the coefficients give $|a_j|_K\le M^{d-j}$, so $s(P)=M$.

In a finite normal extension the roots of an element's minimal polynomial form its orbit under the $K$-automorphisms, by extension of embeddings and normality. Thus $s(x)=\max_{\sigma\in\operatorname{Aut}_K(F)} A(\sigma x)$. This maximum is definite, homogeneous and ultrametric. Submultiplicativity follows by bounding each product by the product of the two maxima. Power multiplicativity follows by passing the positive integer power through the maximum. For an arbitrary algebraic extension test each identity on the finite field generated by the elements involved and embed that field into a finite normal extension. The minimal polynomials stay the same under these embeddings. In particular, $\operatorname{minpoly}_K(a)=T-a$ gives $s(a)=|a|_K$ directly, under the weaker assumption that $K$ is a normed field. [Pinned spectral construction][ch11-ext-spectral] · [Bosch–Güntzer–Remmert, §§3.1.2 and 3.2.1][ch11-ext-bgr]
