### 11.4.6 From the root-of-unity base to arbitrary finite local coefficients

**Motivation.** Section 11.4.5 established a perfect cup pairing in degrees $(1,1)$ between the constant cyclic module $\mathbb Z/n$ and the $n$-th roots of unity $\mu_n(\Omega)$ over every open subgroup $U \le G_K$. Arithmetic applications — from the fundamental local cocycle in Chapter 10 (§10.4.15) to local Selmer conditions in Poitou–Tate duality — rarely pair trivial constant coefficients: their Galois modules carry unipotent Jordan blocks, ramified quadratic or cyclotomic twists, and mixed cyclic exponents. Passing from $(\mathbb Z/n, \mu_n(\Omega))$ in degree $(1,1)$ to an arbitrary finite discrete $G_K$-module $M$ with $(\lvert M \rvert : K) \neq 0$ across all complementary degrees $r + s = 2$ requires genuine *degree-two dévissage*. A one-line appeal to "the five-lemma on long exact cohomology sequences" cannot prove this passage, for two structural reasons: first, the cohomology sequence begins at $H^0$ without a left-hand $H^{-1}$ zero and ends at $H^2$ where right-exactness on the dual side requires $H^3 = 0$; second, embedding $M$ into a finite direct sum $I$ of coinduced trivial cyclic modules produces a cokernel $Q = I / i(M)$ that is a general finite discrete $n$-torsion module of *larger* cardinality than $M$, not a simpler base object. Instead of inducting downward on $\lvert M \rvert$, the archive proves injectivity and surjectivity of the curried duality maps in five ordered stages, each universally quantified over *all* finite discrete $n$-torsion modules simultaneously so that each half-step evaluated at $Q$ (or at the dual $M^\vee_D$) pays for the next half-step at $M$. [Lean, read] A2, `PoincareDuality/Devissage.lean:1–414` and `LocalTateDuality/TensorHomDualRepRootsOfUnityIsoCartierPairing.lean:74–181`. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-local-finite-coefficient-tate-devissage)

**Two examples to keep in view.** Over $K = \mathbb Q_7$ at $n = 3$ (where $\zeta_3 \in \mathbb Q_7$ trivializes $\mu_3$, while $-1 \equiv 2 \not\equiv 1 \pmod 3$ exposes every sign), let $M = (\mathbb Z/3)^2$ carry the unipotent Jordan block $\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\0&1\end{pmatrix}$ along the unramified cubic Frobenius of $\mathbb Q_7(\sqrt[3]{3})/\mathbb Q_7$. Because $\dim_{\mathbb F_3} M = 2$ is not divisible by the subgroup index $[G_K : W] = 3$, the coinduced embedding $0 \to M \xrightarrow{i} I \xrightarrow{p} Q \to 0$ ($\dim_{\mathbb F_3} I = 27$, $\dim_{\mathbb F_3} Q = 25$) does not split, the connecting class $\delta_{S,0,1}(q) \in H^1(G_K, M)$ is nonzero, and anti-commutativity of the cup product with $\delta$ forces the minus sign in $x = x' - \delta_{S,0,1}(q_{\mathrm{corr}})$ when representing ramified dual functionals. Over $K = \mathbb Q_3$ at $n = \lvert M \rvert = 4$, let $M = \mathbb Z/4(\chi_{-3})$ be twisted by the ramified quadratic character of $\mathbb Q_3(\sqrt{-3})/\mathbb Q_3$, with nontrivial dualizing module $D = \mu_4(\Omega) \simeq \mathbb Z/4(\chi_{-1})$ and Cartier dual $M^\vee_D \simeq \mathbb Z/4(\chi_3)$. Here $\gcd([G_K : W], n) = \gcd(2, 4) = 2 > 1$ again prevents splitting, every cohomology group $H^r(G_K, M)$ ($0 \le r \le 2$) is killed by $2$ rather than $4$, and the $\mathbb Z/4$-valued adjoints land bijectively onto the $2$-torsion subgroup $2\mathbb Z/4 \subset \mathbb Z/4$ before $j_4 : \mathbb Z/4 \hookrightarrow \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ identifies that image with $\operatorname{Hom}(H^{2-r}(G_K, M^\ast), \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)$.

**Definitions and the group-duality hypotheses.** Fix a compact topological group $\Gamma$, a nonzero natural number $n$, a discrete representation $D$ of $\Gamma$ over $\mathbb Z$ (`Rep (ULift ℤ) Γ` with `[IsDiscreteRep D]`), and an additive homomorphism $t : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, D) \to \mathbb Z/n$. For any discrete representation $M$ of $\Gamma$, its $D$-dual $M^\vee_D := \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(M, D)$ (`dualRep D M`) carries the contragredient action
$$
(g \cdot f)(m) := g \cdot f(g^{-1} m) \qquad (g \in \Gamma,\ f \in M^\vee_D,\ m \in M),
$$
so that the evaluation map $\operatorname{eval}_{D,M} : M \otimes_{\mathbb Z} M^\vee_D \to D$, $m \otimes f \mapsto f(m)$, is $\Gamma$-equivariant: $g \cdot f(g^{-1}(g m)) = g \cdot f(m)$. For any degrees $r, s \in \mathbb N$, composing the cohomological cup product along $\operatorname{eval}_{D,M}$ with $t$ when $r + s = 2$ defines the curried duality homomorphism
$$
\Phi_{r,s}^M := \operatorname{dualityMap}_{t,M,r,s} : H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \longrightarrow \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D),\, \mathbb Z/n\bigr), \qquad \Phi_{r,s}^M(x)(y) := t\bigl(x \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y\bigr).
$$
For any morphism $\alpha : M \to N$ of discrete $\Gamma$-modules with contragredient $\alpha^\vee : N^\vee_D \to M^\vee_D$ ($\alpha^\vee(f) = f \circ \alpha$), the identity $\operatorname{eval}_{D,N} \circ (\alpha \otimes \mathrm{id}) = \operatorname{eval}_{D,M} \circ (\mathrm{id} \otimes \alpha^\vee)$ gives naturality (`dualityMap_f`):
$$
\Phi_{r,s}^N(\alpha_*^r x)(y) = \Phi_{r,s}^M(x)(\alpha^{\vee,s}_* y) \qquad \bigl(x \in H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M),\ y \in H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, N^\vee_D)\bigr).
$$
A record `H : GroupDualityHypotheses n D t` asserts five properties on the fixed quadruple $(\Gamma, D, t, n)$:

1. `nonempty_addEquiv`: there exists an additive group isomorphism $\psi : D \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Z/n$ (ignoring the $\Gamma$-action), whence $D$ is finite of order $n$ and satisfies $n d = 0$ for all $d \in D$;
2. `finite_two`: for every finite discrete $\Gamma$-module $M$ satisfying $n M = 0$, the group $H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ is finite;
3. `subsingleton_three`: for every finite discrete $\Gamma$-module $M$ satisfying $n M = 0$, the group $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ is trivial (`Subsingleton`);
4. `bijective_cores`: for every open subgroup $W \le \Gamma$, the composite $t \circ \operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W) \to \mathbb Z/n$ is bijective;
5. `isPerfectPairing`: for every open subgroup $W \le \Gamma$, the scalar-multiplication cup pairing $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, \mathbb Z/n) \times H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W) \to H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W) \xrightarrow{t \circ \operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma} \mathbb Z/n$ is a perfect $\mathbb Z/n$-valued pairing (both curried adjoints bijective).

[Lean, read] `PoincareDuality/NsmulEqZeroOfNonemptyAddEquiv.lean:62–130, 154–158, 243–254` and `PoincareDuality/CupPairingEvalPairingCohomologyMap.lean:20–60`.

**Lemma 1 (coinduced cyclic embedding and base duality on the biproduct).** Assume `H : GroupDualityHypotheses n D t` on compact $\Gamma$. Let $M$ be a finite discrete $\Gamma$-module annihilated by $n$. Then there exist an open subgroup $W \le \Gamma$, a finite index set $J$, and a short exact sequence of finite discrete $n$-torsion $\Gamma$-modules
$$
S : \qquad 0 \longrightarrow M \xrightarrow{\;i\;} I := \bigoplus_{j \in J} \operatorname{Coind}_W^\Gamma(\mathbb Z/n) \xrightarrow{\;p\;} Q := \operatorname{coker}(i) \longrightarrow 0
$$
such that both $\Phi_{0,2}^I$ and $\Phi_{1,1}^I$ are bijective.

*Proof.* Because $M$ is discrete, each stabilizer $\operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(m)$ ($m \in M$) is open in $\Gamma$. Because $M$ is *finite*, their intersection $W := \bigcap_{m \in M} \operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(m)$ (`fixingOpenSubgroup M`) is a finite intersection of open subgroups, hence open in $\Gamma$, and compactness of $\Gamma$ makes the right-coset space $W \backslash \Gamma$ finite. On $W$, the restricted action on $M$ is trivial, so every additive character $\chi \in M^\vee_n := \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(M, \mathbb Z/n)$ defines a $W$-equivariant homomorphism $\chi : M|_W \to \mathbb Z/n$ (`charHom`). By Lemma 3 of §11.4.5, $M^\vee_n$ is finite; index it by $e : J = \operatorname{Fin}\lvert M^\vee_n \rvert \xrightarrow{\sim} M^\vee_n$. Recall Mathlib's coinduced module $\operatorname{Coind}_W^\Gamma(V) = \{f : \Gamma \to V \mid \forall w \in W,\ \forall g \in \Gamma,\ f(wg) = w \cdot f(g)\}$ with right-translation action $(g \cdot f)(x) = f(xg)$. Frobenius reciprocity (`Rep.resCoindHomEquiv`) turns each $e_j : M|_W \to \mathbb Z/n$ into $i_j : M \to \operatorname{Coind}_W^\Gamma(\mathbb Z/n)$ given explicitly by $i_j(m)(g) = e_j(g \cdot m)$ (for $w \in W$, $e_j(wg \cdot m) = e_j(g \cdot m)$ because $W$ fixes $g \cdot m \in M$). Taking $i = (i_j)_{j \in J} : M \to I$ into the finite biproduct, suppose $i(a) = i(b)$ with $m_0 := a - b \neq 0$. The cyclic subgroup $\mathbb Z m_0 \subset M$ has nonzero order $d \mid n$ because $n m_0 = 0$; sending $m_0 \mapsto [1/d] \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ defines a nonzero homomorphism $\mathbb Z m_0 \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, which extends to $\chi' : M \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ because $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is a divisible (hence Baer-injective) $\mathbb Z$-module. Since $n M = 0$, $\chi'(M) \subset (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[n] = \operatorname{im}(j_n)$, pulling back to $e_j \in M^\vee_n$ with $e_j(m_0) \neq 0$ (`zmod_exists_apply_ne_zero`). Evaluating coordinate $j$ at $1 \in \Gamma$ gives $0 = i_j(m_0)(1) = e_j(m_0) \neq 0$, a contradiction. Thus $i$ is injective. Its categorical cokernel $Q = I / i(M)$ with quotient projection $p$ completes $S$; since $W \backslash \Gamma$ and $J$ are finite and $n$ kills $\mathbb Z/n$, $I$ is finite, discrete, and killed by $n$, and surjectivity of $p$ passes finiteness, discreteness (`isDiscreteRep_X₃_of_shortExact`, via open-subgroup invariance of $i(M)$), and $n$-torsion to $Q$.

It remains to verify bijectivity of $\Phi_{r,s}^I$ for $(r,s) \in \{(0,2), (1,1)\}$. Because finite biproducts commute with $D$-duals, continuous cohomology, and Hom into $\mathbb Z/n$ (`bijective_dualityMap_biproduct`), it suffices to check a single summand $C := \operatorname{Coind}_W^\Gamma(\mathbb Z/n)$. Choose a finite set $\mathcal R \subset \Gamma$ of representatives for the left cosets $\Gamma / W$ (equivalently, $c^{-1}$ runs through right cosets $W \backslash \Gamma$). For $v \in \mathbb Z/n$, let $\delta_{W,v} \in C$ (`coindSingle`) be $v$ on $W$ and $0$ off $W$; every $f \in C$ decomposes as the finite sum $f = \sum_{c \in \mathcal R} c \cdot \delta_{W, f(c^{-1})}$ (`sum_coindSingle`), since at $x \in W c_0^{-1}$ only $c = c_0$ satisfies $x c \in W$ and then $f(c_0^{-1}) = f((x c_0) c_0^{-1}) = f(x)$. Using this basis, `coindDualIso` identifies $\operatorname{Coind}_W^\Gamma\bigl(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Z/n, D|_W)\bigr) \xrightarrow{\sim} C^\vee_D$ via
$$
\mu \longmapsto \Bigl(f \mapsto \sum_{c \in \mathcal R} c \cdot \bigl(\mu(c^{-1})(f(c^{-1}))\bigr)\Bigr), \qquad \lambda \longmapsto \bigl(x \mapsto (v \mapsto x \cdot \lambda(x^{-1} \cdot \delta_{W,v}))\bigr),
$$
independently of $\mathcal R$ because replacing $c$ by $cw$ ($w \in W$) sends $cw \cdot (\mu(w^{-1} c^{-1})(f(w^{-1} c^{-1}))) = cw \cdot (w^{-1} \cdot \mu(c^{-1})(f(c^{-1}))) = c \cdot (\mu(c^{-1})(f(c^{-1})))$. Under this isomorphism, evaluation at $1 \in \mathbb Z/n$ identifies $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(\mathbb Z/n, D|_W) \xrightarrow{\sim} D|_W$ (`zmodDualIso`, using $n D = 0$), Shapiro's isomorphism identifies $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, C) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, \mathbb Z/n)$ and $H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, C^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W)$, and the cochain projection formula $\operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma(\operatorname{res}_W^\Gamma a \cup b) = a \cup \operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma b$ (`cupPairing_evalPairing_coindDualHom`) identifies $\Phi_{r,s}^C$ with the scalar-multiplication cup pairing over $W$ composed with $t \circ \operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma$. For $(r,s) = (1,1)$, this is `(H.isPerfectPairing W).1`, which is bijective by hypothesis (5). For $(r,s) = (0,2)$, $H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, \mathbb Z/n) = \mathbb Z/n$ acts on $H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W)$ by integer multiples, and hypothesis (4) (`H.bijective_cores W`) gives a unique class $y_0 \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W)$ with $(t \circ \operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma)(y_0) = 1$; since $n H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W) = 0$, every $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(W, D|_W)$ equals $((t \circ \operatorname{cor}_W^\Gamma)(y)) \cdot y_0$, making the degree-$(0,2)$ adjoint bijective (`bijective_zmodMulPairing_zero`). [Lean, read] `PoincareDuality/Devissage.lean:99–133`, `PoincareDuality/CupPairingEvalPairingCohomologyMap.lean:89–235`, `PoincareDuality/InducedDual.lean:166–265`, `PoincareDuality/Induced.lean:59–79, 242–299`, `PoincareDuality/TensorHomZmodDualIsoZmodMulPairing.lean:199–226`, and pinned Mathlib, `RepresentationTheory/Coinduced.lean:56–106, 245–308`. $\square$

**Lemma 2 (when $n$-torsion and finiteness make the dual sequence exact and discrete).** Let $0 \to X_1 \xrightarrow{f} X_2 \xrightarrow{g} X_3 \to 0$ be a short exact sequence of discrete $\Gamma$-modules, and assume $D \simeq_+ \mathbb Z/n$ (`H.nonempty_addEquiv`).

1. (*Algebraic exactness via $n$-torsion of the middle term.*) If $n X_2 = 0$, then the contragredient sequence
   $$
   0 \longrightarrow X_{3,D}^\vee \xrightarrow{\;g^\vee\;} X_{2,D}^\vee \xrightarrow{\;f^\vee\;} X_{1,D}^\vee \longrightarrow 0
   $$
   is short exact as $\mathbb Z[\Gamma]$-modules (`dualShortComplex_shortExact`).
2. (*Topological discreteness via finiteness.*) If $X_1, X_2, X_3$ are *finite*, then $X_{1,D}^\vee, X_{2,D}^\vee, X_{3,D}^\vee$ are finite discrete $\Gamma$-modules annihilated by $n$ (`isDiscreteRep_dualRep`), so the dual short exact sequence induces a long exact sequence of continuous cohomology groups (`cochainsShortComplex_shortExact`).

*Proof.* (1) If $g^\vee(\lambda) = \lambda \circ g = 0$, surjectivity of $g$ forces $\lambda = 0$, so $g^\vee$ is injective without extra hypotheses. Clearly $f^\vee(g^\vee(\lambda)) = \lambda \circ (g \circ f) = 0$; conversely, if $\mu \in \ker(f^\vee)$ vanishes on $f(X_1) = \ker(g)$, surjectivity of $g$ descends $\mu$ uniquely to $X_3$, proving exactness at $X_{2,D}^\vee$. Surjectivity of $f^\vee$ is the step that spends $n X_2 = 0$: fix an additive isomorphism $\psi : D \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Z/n$ and let $e \in X_{1,D}^\vee$. The composite $j_n \circ \psi \circ e : X_1 \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ extends along the injection $f : X_1 \hookrightarrow X_2$ to an additive homomorphism $e_0 : X_2 \to \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ by Baer's criterion (`Module.Baer.of_divisible`, since $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ is divisible). Because $n X_2 = 0$, every value $e_0(x)$ ($x \in X_2$) satisfies $n e_0(x) = e_0(n x) = 0$, so $e_0(X_2) \subset (\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[n] = \operatorname{im}(j_n)$ (`zmod_exists_extension`). Pulling back along $\psi$ and $j_n$ yields $e' \in X_{2,D}^\vee$ with $f^\vee(e') = e' \circ f = e$.

(2) Fix a finite discrete $\Gamma$-module $X$ and $\lambda \in X^\vee_D$. For each $x \in X$, both $\operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(x)$ and $\operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(\lambda(x))$ are open because $X$ and $D$ are discrete. Their intersection over all $x \in X$,
$$
U_\lambda := \bigcap_{x \in X} \bigl(\operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(x) \cap \operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(\lambda(x))\bigr),
$$
is open *because $X$ is finite*. For $u \in U_\lambda$ and $x \in X$, we have $u^{-1} x = x$ and $(u \cdot \lambda)(x) = u \cdot \lambda(u^{-1} x) = u \cdot \lambda(x) = \lambda(x)$, so $U_\lambda \le \operatorname{Stab}_\Gamma(\lambda)$, proving `IsDiscreteRep` for $X^\vee_D$. Since $D$ is finite and killed by $n$, $X^\vee_D \subset D^X$ is also finite and killed by $n$. Applying `cochainsShortComplex_shortExact` to $S^\vee_D$ produces the continuous long exact cohomology sequence. [Lean, read] `PoincareDuality/TensorHomZmodDualIsoZmodMulPairing.lean:77–177`, `PoincareDuality/NsmulEqZeroOfNonemptyAddEquiv.lean:94–108`, `PoincareDuality/Torsion.lean:70–81`, `TorsionDualFinite.lean:65–73`, `ContinuousCochains/LongExactSequence.lean:35–116`, and pinned Mathlib, `Algebra/Module/Injective.lean:61–70, 382–414`. $\square$

**Lemma 3 (anti-commutativity of the duality pairing with connecting homomorphisms).** Let $S : 0 \to X_1 \xrightarrow{f} X_2 \xrightarrow{g} X_3 \to 0$ be a short exact sequence of finite discrete $n$-torsion $\Gamma$-modules, with dual short exact sequence $T = S^\vee_D : 0 \to X_{3,D}^\vee \xrightarrow{g^\vee} X_{2,D}^\vee \xrightarrow{f^\vee} X_{1,D}^\vee \to 0$. For any degrees $p, q \in \mathbb N$, the evaluation cup pairing satisfies
$$
(\delta_{S,p,p+1} x) \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y = (-1)^{p+1}\, x \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} (\delta_{T,q,q+1} y) \in H^{p+q+1}_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, D)
$$
for all $x \in H^p_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, X_3)$ and $y \in H^q_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, X_{1,D}^\vee)$. In particular, at $p = 0$ and $q = 1$ (`dualityMap_δ`),
$$
\Phi_{1,1}^{X_1}(\delta_{S,0,1} x)(y) = -\Phi_{0,2}^{X_3}(x)(\delta_{T,1,2} y).
$$

*Proof.* Represent $x$ by a continuous cocycle $x_3 \in Z^p(\Gamma, X_3)$ and $y$ by $y_3 \in Z^q(\Gamma, X_{1,D}^\vee)$. Because $g$ and $f^\vee$ are surjective on discrete modules, continuous cochain surjectivity (`δ_spec`) lifts $x_3 = g_*(x_2)$ for some $x_2 \in C^p(\Gamma, X_2)$ and $y_3 = f^\vee_*(y_2)$ for some $y_2 \in C^q(\Gamma, X_{2,D}^\vee)$, and pulls their coboundaries back to cocycles $x_1 \in Z^{p+1}(\Gamma, X_1)$ and $y_1 \in Z^{q+1}(\Gamma, X_{3,D}^\vee)$ satisfying $f_*(x_1) = d x_2$, $g^\vee_*(y_1) = d y_2$, $[x_1] = \delta_{S,p,p+1}(x)$, and $[y_1] = \delta_{T,q,q+1}(y)$. On inhomogeneous cochains of degrees $p$ and $q$, the graded Leibniz identity (`d_cupCochain`) reads
$$
d\bigl(x_2 \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y_2\bigr) = (d x_2) \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y_2 + (-1)^p\, x_2 \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} (d y_2) \in B^{p+q+1}(\Gamma, D).
$$
On the first term, $d x_2 = f_*(x_1)$ and $\operatorname{eval}_{D,X_2}(f(a), \mu) = \mu(f(a)) = (f^\vee \mu)(a) = \operatorname{eval}_{D,X_1}(a, f^\vee \mu)$ rewrite $(d x_2) \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y_2 = x_1 \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} f^\vee_*(y_2) = x_1 \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y_3$. On the second term, $d y_2 = g^\vee_*(y_1)$ and $\operatorname{eval}_{D,X_2}(b, g^\vee \lambda) = \lambda(g(b)) = \operatorname{eval}_{D,X_3}(g(b), \lambda)$ rewrite $x_2 \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} (d y_2) = g_*(x_2) \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y_1 = x_3 \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y_1$. Passing to cohomology classes in $H^{p+q+1}_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, D)$ gives $[x_1] \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} [y_3] + (-1)^p [x_3] \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} [y_1] = 0$, so $(\delta_S x) \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} y = -(-1)^p\, x \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} (\delta_T y) = (-1)^{p+1}\, x \cup_{\mathrm{eval}} (\delta_T y)$. Setting $p = 0$, $q = 1$ gives $(-1)^{0+1} = -1$, and applying the additive homomorphism $t$ yields `dualityMap_δ`. [Lean, read] `PoincareDuality/DeltaPairing.lean:26–104`, `PoincareDuality/Devissage.lean:47–60`, `CupProducts/ExistsCocycleMapFEqD.lean:45–199`, and `TateCohomology/CupCochains.lean:113–116`. $\square$

**Theorem 1 (degree-two dévissage for arbitrary finite $n$-torsion modules).** Let $\Gamma$ be a compact totally disconnected topological group, and assume `H : GroupDualityHypotheses n D t`. Then for every finite discrete $\Gamma$-module $M$ satisfying $n M = 0$ and every pair $(r, s) \in \mathbb N^2$ with $r + s = 2$, the curried duality map
$$
\Phi_{r,s}^M : H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \longrightarrow \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D),\, \mathbb Z/n\bigr)
$$
is bijective (`GroupDualityHypotheses.bijective_dualityMap`).

*Proof.* For each finite discrete $n$-torsion $\Gamma$-module $M$, fix its coinduced resolution $S : 0 \to M \xrightarrow{i} I \xrightarrow{p} Q \to 0$ from Lemma 1 (with $\Phi_{0,2}^I$ and $\Phi_{1,1}^I$ bijective) and its discrete contragredient short exact sequence $T : 0 \to Q^\vee_D \xrightarrow{p^\vee} I^\vee_D \xrightarrow{i^\vee} M^\vee_D \to 0$ from Lemma 2. We prove five universally quantified assertions over *all* finite discrete $n$-torsion modules $M$ in strict logical order.

*Step 1: $\Phi_{0,2}^M$ is injective for all $M$ (`injective_zero`).* Let $x \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ satisfy $\Phi_{0,2}^M(x) = 0$. For every $z \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D)$, naturality gives $\Phi_{0,2}^I(i^0 x)(z) = \Phi_{0,2}^M(x)(i^{\vee,2} z) = 0$. Hence $\Phi_{0,2}^I(i^0 x) = 0$, and injectivity of $\Phi_{0,2}^I$ forces $i^0 x = 0$. Under the canonical identification $H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, -) \simeq (-)^\Gamma$ (`cohomologyZeroEquivInvariants`), $i^0$ is the restriction of the injection $i : M \hookrightarrow I$ to $\Gamma$-invariants, which is injective (`injective_cohomologyMap_zero`). Therefore $x = 0$.

*Step 2: $\Phi_{0,2}^M$ is surjective for all $M$ (`surjective_zero`, completing `bijective_zero`).* Let $\ell \in \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D), \mathbb Z/n\bigr)$. In the long exact cohomology sequence of $T$, the tail reads
$$
H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{\;p^{\vee,2}\;} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{\;i^{\vee,2}\;} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{\;\delta_{T,2,3}\;} H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D).
$$
Because $Q^\vee_D$ is finite, discrete, and killed by $n$ (Lemma 2(2)), hypothesis (3) (`H.subsingleton_three (dualRep D Q)`) gives $H^3_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) = 0$, so exactness at $H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D)$ makes $i^{\vee,2}$ *surjective*. Pull $\ell$ back to $\ell \circ i^{\vee,2} : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D) \to \mathbb Z/n$. By surjectivity of $\Phi_{0,2}^I$, there exists $v \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I)$ with $\Phi_{0,2}^I(v) = \ell \circ i^{\vee,2}$. Push $v$ forward to $p^0 v \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q)$: for every $w \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D)$, naturality and $i^\vee \circ p^\vee = 0$ give
$$
\Phi_{0,2}^Q(p^0 v)(w) = \Phi_{0,2}^I(v)(p^{\vee,2} w) = \ell\bigl(i^{\vee,2}(p^{\vee,2} w)\bigr) = \ell(0) = 0.
$$
Thus $\Phi_{0,2}^Q(p^0 v) = 0$. Applying **Step 1 to the cokernel module $Q$** forces $p^0 v = 0$! Exactness of $0 \to H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \xrightarrow{i^0} H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I) \xrightarrow{p^0} H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q)$ then supplies $m \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ with $i^0 m = v$. For any $y \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D)$, surjectivity of $i^{\vee,2}$ chooses $z \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D)$ with $i^{\vee,2} z = y$, and
$$
\Phi_{0,2}^M(m)(y) = \Phi_{0,2}^M(m)(i^{\vee,2} z) = \Phi_{0,2}^I(i^0 m)(z) = \Phi_{0,2}^I(v)(z) = \ell(i^{\vee,2} z) = \ell(y).
$$
Combined with Step 1, $\Phi_{0,2}^N$ is now *bijective* for every finite discrete $n$-torsion $\Gamma$-module $N$.

*Step 3: $\Phi_{1,1}^M$ is injective for all $M$ (`injective_one`).* Let $x \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ satisfy $\Phi_{1,1}^M(x) = 0$. By naturality, $\Phi_{1,1}^I(i^1 x) = \Phi_{1,1}^M(x) \circ i^{\vee,1} = 0$, so injectivity of $\Phi_{1,1}^I$ gives $i^1 x = 0$. Exactness of $H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I) \xrightarrow{p^0} H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q) \xrightarrow{\delta_{S,0,1}} H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \xrightarrow{i^1} H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I)$ produces $q \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q)$ with $\delta_{S,0,1}(q) = x$. Consider the functional $\Phi_{0,2}^Q(q) : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) \to \mathbb Z/n$ on $\ker\bigl(p^{\vee,2} : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) \to H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D)\bigr)$. By exactness of $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{\delta_{T,1,2}} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{p^{\vee,2}} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D)$, any $z \in \ker(p^{\vee,2})$ has the form $z = \delta_{T,1,2}(y)$ for some $y \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D)$. **Lemma 3** (`dualityMap_δ`) then gives
$$
\Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)(z) = \Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)(\delta_{T,1,2} y) = -\Phi_{1,1}^M(\delta_{S,0,1} q)(y) = -\Phi_{1,1}^M(x)(y) = 0.
$$
Thus $\ker(p^{\vee,2}) \subset \ker\bigl(\Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)\bigr)$, so $\Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)$ descends to a homomorphism $\overline{\psi} : \operatorname{im}(p^{\vee,2}) \to \mathbb Z/n$. Because $n I^\vee_D = 0$ implies $n H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D) = 0$ (`cohomology_nsmul_eq_zero`), Baer extension into $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ followed by pullback to $(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[n] = \operatorname{im}(j_n)$ (exactly as in Lemma 2(1), `zmod_exists_comp_eq_of_ker_le`) extends $\overline{\psi}$ across $\operatorname{im}(p^{\vee,2}) \hookrightarrow H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D)$ to an additive map $\psi : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I^\vee_D) \to \mathbb Z/n$ satisfying $\psi \circ p^{\vee,2} = \Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)$. Surjectivity of $\Phi_{0,2}^I$ represents $\psi = \Phi_{0,2}^I(v)$ for some $v \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I)$, whence $\Phi_{0,2}^Q(p^0 v) = \Phi_{0,2}^I(v) \circ p^{\vee,2} = \psi \circ p^{\vee,2} = \Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)$. Applying **Step 1 to $Q$** yields $p^0 v = q$, and therefore $x = \delta_{S,0,1}(p^0 v) = 0$ (`comp_δ`).

*Step 4: $\Phi_{1,1}^M$ is surjective for all $M$ (`surjective_one`, completing `bijective_one`).* Let $\ell \in \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D), \mathbb Z/n\bigr)$. Surjectivity of $\Phi_{1,1}^I$ represents $\ell \circ i^{\vee,1} = \Phi_{1,1}^I(v)$ for some $v \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I)$. For every $w \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D)$, $\Phi_{1,1}^Q(p^1 v)(w) = \Phi_{1,1}^I(v)(p^{\vee,1} w) = \ell\bigl(i^{\vee,1}(p^{\vee,1} w)\bigr) = \ell(0) = 0$, so $\Phi_{1,1}^Q(p^1 v) = 0$. Applying **Step 3 to $Q$** (`H.injective_one Q hnQ`) forces $p^1 v = 0$! Exactness of $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \xrightarrow{i^1} H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, I) \xrightarrow{p^1} H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q)$ then supplies a preliminary class $x' \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ with $i^1 x' = v$. Form the residual functional
$$
\nu := \ell - \Phi_{1,1}^M(x') \in \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D),\, \mathbb Z/n\bigr).
$$
On $\ker(\delta_{T,1,2}) = \operatorname{im}(i^{\vee,1})$, we have $\nu(i^{\vee,1} z) = \ell(i^{\vee,1} z) - \Phi_{1,1}^I(i^1 x')(z) = \ell(i^{\vee,1} z) - \Phi_{1,1}^I(v)(z) = 0$. Hence $\ker(\delta_{T,1,2}) \subset \ker(\nu)$. Since $n Q^\vee_D = 0$ implies $n H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) = 0$, `zmod_exists_comp_eq_of_ker_le` produces $\psi : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q^\vee_D) \to \mathbb Z/n$ satisfying $\psi \circ \delta_{T,1,2} = \nu$. Applying **Step 2 to $Q$** (`(H.bijective_zero Q hnQ).2`) represents $\psi = \Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)$ for some $q \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, Q)$. Now set
$$
x := x' - \delta_{S,0,1}(q) \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M).
$$
For every $y \in H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D)$, **Lemma 3** (`dualityMap_δ`) cancels the minus sign:
$$
\Phi_{1,1}^M\bigl(x' - \delta_{S,0,1}(q)\bigr)(y) = \Phi_{1,1}^M(x')(y) - \bigl(-\Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)(\delta_{T,1,2} y)\bigr) = \Phi_{1,1}^M(x')(y) + \nu(y) = \ell(y).
$$

*Step 5: $\Phi_{2,0}^M$ is bijective for all $M$ (`bijective_two`).* Consider the canonical evaluation morphism $\operatorname{ev}_M : M \to (M^\vee_D)^\vee_D$ (`toDualDual`), $\operatorname{ev}_M(m)(f) = f(m)$. If $m \neq 0$, character separation into $\mathbb Z/n \simeq_+ D$ (`exists_dualRep_apply_ne_zero`) produces $f \in M^\vee_D$ with $f(m) \neq 0$, so $\operatorname{ev}_M$ is injective; since $\psi : D \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathbb Z/n$ identifies additive maps into $D$ with additive maps into $\mathbb Z/n$, Lemma 3 of §11.4.5 gives $\lvert (M^\vee_D)^\vee_D \rvert \le \lvert M^\vee_D \rvert \le \lvert M \rvert$, forcing $\operatorname{ev}_M$ to be a $\Gamma$-module isomorphism (`bijective_toDualDual`). Next, because $\Gamma$ is compact *and totally disconnected*, every continuous cocycle factors through a finite discrete quotient where graded cup-product braiding holds (`cohomologyMap_braiding_cup`); in complementary degrees $(2,0)$, the graded sign is $(-1)^{2 \cdot 0} = +1$, and $\operatorname{eval}_{D, M^\vee_D}(f, \operatorname{ev}_M(m)) = \operatorname{ev}_M(m)(f) = f(m) = \operatorname{eval}_{D,M}(m, f)$ yields (`dualityMap_two_eq`)
$$
\Phi_{2,0}^M(x)(y) = \Phi_{0,2}^{M^\vee_D}(y)\bigl(\operatorname{ev}_{M,*}^2 x\bigr) \qquad \bigl(x \in H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M),\ y \in H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D)\bigr).
$$
Applying **Steps 1–2 (`bijective_zero`) to $M^\vee_D$** and precomposing with the isomorphism $\operatorname{ev}_{M,*}^2 : H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M) \xrightarrow{\sim} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, (M^\vee_D)^\vee_D)$ shows that the *flipped* adjoint $y \mapsto \bigl(x \mapsto \Phi_{2,0}^M(x)(y)\bigr)$ is an isomorphism $H^0_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M^\vee_D) \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M), \mathbb Z/n\bigr)$. Finally, hypothesis (2) (`H.finite_two M`) guarantees that $A := H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma, M)$ is *finite*, while $n M = 0$ gives $n A = 0$. For any bilinear map $B : A \times B_0 \to \mathbb Z/n$ whose right adjoint $R : B_0 \xrightarrow{\sim} A^\vee_n$ is bijective on finite $n$-torsion $A$, the left adjoint $L : A \to B_{0,n}^\vee$ is injective because any $0 \neq a \in A$ is detected by some $\chi \in A^\vee_n = \operatorname{im}(R)$, and $\lvert A \rvert = \lvert B_0 \rvert \ge \lvert B_{0,n}^\vee \rvert \ge \lvert A \rvert$ forces $L$ to be surjective (`bijective_of_bijective_flip`). Thus $\Phi_{2,0}^M$ is bijective. Since $r + s = 2$ in $\mathbb N$ forces $(r,s) \in \{(0,2), (1,1), (2,0)\}$, all cases are proved. [Lean, read] `PoincareDuality/Devissage.lean:150–411`, `PoincareDuality/TensorHomZmodDualIsoZmodMulPairing.lean:25–70`, `PoincareDuality/Torsion.lean:102–133`, and `CupProducts/Graded.lean:54–74`. $\square$

**Corollary 1 (local Tate duality for arbitrary finite Galois modules).** Let $K$ be a non-archimedean local field, $\Omega / K$ a separable closure with Krull Galois group $G_K = \operatorname{Gal}(\Omega / K)$, and $M$ a finite discrete $G_K$-module whose cardinality satisfies $(\lvert M \rvert : K) \neq 0$. Write $M^\ast := \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(M, \Omega^\times)$ (`cartierDual K Ω M`) with the contragredient $G_K$-action, and write
$$
\langle - , - \rangle_{r,s} : H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M) \times H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\ast) \xrightarrow{\;\cup\;} H^2_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, \Omega^\times) \xrightarrow{\;\operatorname{inv}_K\;} \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z
$$
for the local cup pairing (`localDualityPairing`). Then `tate_local_duality` holds:

1. $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M)$ is finite for $r \le 2$ and trivial (`Subsingleton`) for $r \ge 3$;
2. for every $r + s = 2$, both curried adjoints $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M) \to \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\ast), \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\bigr)$ and $H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\ast) \to \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}\bigl(H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M), \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\bigr)$ are bijective.

*Proof.* Set $n := \lvert M \rvert = \operatorname{Nat.card}\,M$. The assumption $(n : K) \neq 0$ implies $n \ge 1$ (`NeZero n`), while Lagrange's theorem in the finite additive group $M$ gives $n m = 0$ for every $m \in M$. At this exponent $n$, `localDualityHypotheses` (`LocalTateDuality/MainDefs.lean:88–95`) bundles the §11.4.5 theorem `isPerfectPairing_localInvRootsOfUnity` with the named local class-formation supplies (`nonempty_addEquiv_galRootsOfUnity`, `finite_cohomology_galois_le_two`, `subsingleton_cohomology_three_galois` from `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing.lean`, and `localInvRootsOfUnity_cores`) into `DualityHypotheses n (galRootsOfUnity K Ω n)`. Restricting to the top open subgroup $\top \le G_K$ via the topological group isomorphism $\top \simeq G_K$ (`DualityHypotheses.groupDualityHypotheses`, `tTop`, `bijective_dualityMap_of_group` in `PoincareDuality.lean:233–269, 326–348`) produces `GroupDualityHypotheses n (galRootsOfUnity K Ω n) tTop` on $G_K$ itself, yielding item (1) (`finite_cohomology_localField`, `subsingleton_cohomology_localField_of_ge_three`) and bijectivity of $\Phi_{r,s}^M$ into $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\vee_{\mu_n}), \mathbb Z/n)$ by Theorem 1.

Next, for any $f \in M^\ast = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(M, \Omega^\times)$ and $m \in M$, the relation $n m = 0$ in $(M, +)$ forces $f(m)^n = f(n m) = 1 \in \Omega^\times$, so every homomorphism $M \to \Omega^\times$ automatically lands in $\mu_n(\Omega)$, giving a canonical $G_K$-module isomorphism $\iota_M : M^\vee_{\mu_n} \xrightarrow{\sim} M^\ast$ (`dualRepRootsOfUnityIso`, `CartierDual.lean:85–126`) compatible with evaluation into $\mu_n(\Omega) \hookrightarrow \Omega^\times$. By the defining normalization of $t_{\mathrm{top}} = \operatorname{localInvRootsOfUnity}_{K,\Omega,n,\top}$ (§11.4.5), postcomposing $t_{\mathrm{top}}$ with $j_n : \mathbb Z/n \hookrightarrow \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ equals $\operatorname{inv}_K$ on the Kummer image of $\mu_n(\Omega)$ (`brauerInvOfIsSepClosure_rootsOfUnity`, `:74–86`), so for $x \in H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M)$ and $y \in H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\vee_{\mu_n})$ (`localDualityPairing_cohomologyMap`, `:98–110`),
$$
\langle x,\, \iota_{M,*}^s y \rangle_{r,s} = j_n\bigl(\Phi_{r,s}^M(x)(y)\bigr) \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z.
$$
Because $n M^\ast = 0$ kills $B := H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\ast)$, postcomposition with $j_n$ is an isomorphism $\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(B, \mathbb Z/n) \xrightarrow{\sim} \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(B, \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)$ (`bijective_of_zmodToQZ`, `Torsion.lean:102–113`), giving bijectivity of the left $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$-adjoint (`bijective_localDualityPairing`, `:130–144`). Finally, because $H^r_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M)$ and $H^s_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\ast)$ are finite $n$-torsion groups, `bijective_of_bijective_flip` flips the bijection to the right adjoint (`bijective_localDualityPairing_flip`, `:146–173`). [Lean, read] `PoincareDuality.lean:233–269, 326–348`, `CartierDual.lean:85–126`, and `LocalTateDuality/TensorHomDualRepRootsOfUnityIsoCartierPairing.lean:26–181`. $\square$

**Example 1 (nontrivial unipotent Jordan block over $K = \mathbb Q_7$ at $n = 3$: non-split dévissage and the minus sign in `surjective_one`).** Take $K = \mathbb Q_7$ and $n = 3$. Since $7 \equiv 1 \pmod 3$, Hensel's lemma lifts $2 \in \mathbb F_7^\times$ ($2^2 + 2 + 1 = 7 \equiv 0 \pmod 7$) to a primitive cube root $\zeta_3 \in \mathbb Z_7^\times$ with $\zeta_3 \equiv 2 \pmod 7$, so $D = \mu_3(\Omega)$ carries the *trivial* $G_{\mathbb Q_7}$-action ($\zeta_3^j \leftrightarrow j \bmod 3$). Let $L = \mathbb Q_7(\sqrt[3]{3})$: because $T^3 - 3$ has no root in $\mathbb F_7$ ($x^3 \equiv 0, \pm 1 \pmod 7$), $L / \mathbb Q_7$ is unramified cubic with open subgroup $W = G_L \trianglelefteq G_{\mathbb Q_7}$ of index $3$, and arithmetic Frobenius $\phi \in G_{\mathbb Q_7}$ satisfies $\phi(\sqrt[3]{3}) / \sqrt[3]{3} \equiv 3^{(7-1)/3} = 9 \equiv 2 \equiv \zeta_3 \pmod 7$. Let $\chi_{\mathrm{unr}} : G_{\mathbb Q_7} \twoheadrightarrow \mathbb Z/3$ send $\phi \mapsto 1$ (with $\ker \chi_{\mathrm{unr}} = W$), and let $M = (\mathbb Z/3)^2$ carry the unipotent representation $\rho_M(g)(a, b) = \bigl(a + \chi_{\mathrm{unr}}(g) b,\, b\bigr)$, i.e.
$$
F_M := \rho_M(\phi) = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 1 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad F_{M^\vee} := \rho_{M^\vee_D}(\phi) = (F_M^{-1})^{\mathsf T} = \begin{pmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 2 & 1 \end{pmatrix} \pmod 3,
$$
both trivial on inertia. Here $\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{\mathbb Q_7}}(0, 1) = W$, so `fixingOpenSubgroup M` is $W$. Indexing $M^\vee_3 = \{\chi_{r,s}(a,b) = ra + sb \mid (r,s) \in (\mathbb Z/3)^2\}$ ($9$ characters), Lemma 1 builds the $27$-dimensional $\mathbb F_3$-space $I = \bigoplus_{(r,s) \in (\mathbb Z/3)^2} \operatorname{Coind}_W^{G_{\mathbb Q_7}}(\mathbb Z/3)$, where each $3$-dimensional summand $(u_0, u_1, u_2)$ ($u_k = f(\phi^k)$) has cyclic shift $F_C(u_0, u_1, u_2) = (u_1, u_2, u_0)$, and embeds $M \hookrightarrow I$ via $i(a,b)_{(r,s)} = (ra + sb,\ ra + (r+s)b,\ ra + (2r+s)b) \bmod 3$, with $25$-dimensional cokernel $Q = I / i(M) \simeq J_3^{\oplus 8} \oplus J_1$.

Every $3$-primary continuous cocycle of $G_{\mathbb Q_7}$ factors through the maximal pro-$3$ quotient $\Gamma_3 = \langle \phi, \tau \mid \phi \tau = \tau^7 \phi \rangle$ (tame since $3 \nmid 7$). On any $\mathbb F_3[\Gamma_3]$-module $A$ where $\tau$ acts trivially and $F_A^3 = \mathrm{id}$, the exact length-$2$ Demushkin resolution $A \xrightarrow{d_0} A \oplus A \xrightarrow{d_1} A \to 0$ has $d_0(a) = \bigl((F_A - I)a, 0\bigr)$ and $d_1(u, v) = (I - \tau^7)u + \bigl(F_A - \sum_{k=0}^6 \tau^k\bigr)v = (F_A - 7I)v = (F_A - I)v \pmod 3$, giving $H^0 = \ker(F_A - I)$, $H^1 = \bigl(A / (F_A - I)A\bigr) \oplus \ker(F_A - I)$ (unramified $\phi$-value $u$ plus ramified $\tau$-value $v$), $H^2 = A / (F_A - I)A$, and $H^3 = 0$. Thus $M$ and $M^\vee_D$ have $\mathbb F_3$-cohomology dimensions $(1, 2, 1, 0)$, whereas $I, Q, I^\vee_D, Q^\vee_D$ all have dimensions $(9, 18, 9, 0)$ (in particular $i^2 : H^2(M) \to H^2(I)$ and $i^{\vee,0} : H^0(I^\vee_D) \to H^0(M^\vee_D)$ are zero!). On the finite metacyclic quotient $G_{81} = \langle \phi, \tau \mid \phi^9 = \tau^9 = 1,\ \phi \tau = \tau^7 \phi \rangle$, expanding any coboundary $dh$ ($h : G_{81} \to \mathbb Z/3$) along $\phi \tau = \tau^7 \phi$ cancels $h(\phi)$ and $7h(\tau) - h(\tau) = 6h(\tau) \equiv 0 \pmod 3$, yielding the exact cochain-level Brauer invariant
$$
L_{\mathrm{rel}}(c_2) := c_2(\phi, \tau) - c_2(\tau^7, \phi) - \sum_{j=1}^6 c_2(\tau^j, \tau) \pmod 3, \qquad L_{\mathrm{rel}}(\chi_{\mathrm{unr}} \cup \chi_{\mathrm{ram}}) = +1, \quad L_{\mathrm{rel}}(\chi_{\mathrm{ram}} \cup \chi_{\mathrm{unr}}) = 2 \equiv -1,
$$
where $\chi_{\mathrm{ram}}(\phi^a \tau^b) = b \bmod 3$ corresponds under §11.4.5's Artin formula $B(\chi, \kappa_3(a)) = \chi(\operatorname{Art}_{\mathbb Q_7}(a))$ to $-\kappa_3(3)$ (since $3^{-(7-1)/3} \equiv 4 \equiv \zeta_3^{-1} \pmod 7$). Choose ordered bases $e_1 = z_{\mathrm{unr},M}$ ($z(\phi) = (2,1), z(\tau) = (0,0)$), $e_2 = z_{\mathrm{ram},M}$ ($z(\phi) = (0,0), z(\tau) = (1,0)$) of $H^1(G_{\mathbb Q_7}, M)$, and $f_1 = z_{\mathrm{unr},M^\vee}$ ($y(\phi) = (1,0), y(\tau) = (0,0)$), $f_2 = z_{\mathrm{ram},M^\vee}$ ($y(\phi) = (0,0), y(\tau) = (0,1)$) of $H^1(G_{\mathbb Q_7}, M^\vee_D)$. Evaluating $L_{\mathrm{rel}}$ on inhomogeneous $G_{81}$-cochains gives the invertible pairing matrices
$$
P_{0,2} = \begin{pmatrix} 1 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad P_{1,1} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ 2 & 0 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad P_{2,0} = \begin{pmatrix} 1 \end{pmatrix} \pmod 3,
$$
with $H^0(M) = \operatorname{span}\{(1,0)\}$, $H^2(M^\vee_D) = M^\vee_D / \operatorname{span}\{(0,1)\} = \operatorname{span}\{[(1,0)]\}$, $H^2(M) = M / \operatorname{span}\{(1,0)\} = \operatorname{span}\{[(0,1)]\}$, and $H^0(M^\vee_D) = \operatorname{span}\{(0,1)\}$.

Now trace Steps 3 and 4 of Theorem 1 explicitly on $e_1 = z_{\mathrm{unr},M}$. In $I$, define $x_{2,I} := \bigl(0,\, 2r+s,\, 2r+2s\bigr)_{(r,s) \in (\mathbb Z/3)^2}$. Applying $\phi - 1$ gives $(2r+s,\ s,\ r+s)_{(r,s)} = i(2, 1) = i(e_1(\phi))$, while $x_{2,I} \notin i(M)$ (at $(r,s) = (0,1)$, $(0,1,2)$ does not have the form $(b, b, b)$). Thus $q := p(x_{2,I})$ is a *nonzero* class in $H^0(G_{\mathbb Q_7}, Q)$ satisfying $\delta_{S,0,1}(q) = e_1$ (forcing $i^1(e_1) = 0$). Dually, $\operatorname{im}\bigl(i^{\vee,1} : H^1(I^\vee_D) \to H^1(M^\vee_D)\bigr) = \operatorname{span}\{f_1\}$ misses the ramified class $f_2$: lifting $(0,1) \in M^\vee_D$ along $i^\vee$ to $v_{I^\vee} \in I^\vee_D$ produces $y_2(\phi^a \tau^b) = F_{I^\vee}^a(b v_{I^\vee})$ whose coboundary $d y_2 = p^\vee(y_1)$ represents $\delta_{T,1,2}(f_2) \neq 0$ in $\ker(p^{\vee,2}) \subset H^2(G_{\mathbb Q_7}, Q^\vee_D)$. Evaluating both sides of Lemma 3 on $G_{81}$ yields
$$
\Phi_{1,1}^M(\delta_{S,0,1} q)(f_2) = P_{1,1}(e_1, f_2) = 1 \pmod 3, \qquad \Phi_{0,2}^Q(q)\bigl(\delta_{T,1,2}(f_2)\bigr) = 2 \pmod 3,
$$
confirming $1 \equiv -2 \pmod 3$ while the unnegated value $2 \not\equiv 1 \pmod 3$ fails! Consequently, for an arbitrary functional $\ell : H^1(M^\vee_D) \to \mathbb Z/3$ with values $(l_1, l_2)$ on $(f_1, f_2)$, Step 4 first lifts $\ell \circ i^{\vee,1}$ (which sees only $l_1$) to $x' = (2 l_1) e_2 \in H^1(M)$ (giving $\Phi_{1,1}^M(x') = (4l_1, 0) \equiv (l_1, 0)$ and residual $\nu = (0, l_2)$), represents $\psi(\delta_{T,1,2} f_2) = l_2$ via $q_{\mathrm{corr}} = (2 l_2) q \in H^0(Q)$ (since $2 l_2 \cdot 2 \equiv l_2 \pmod 3$), and forms
$$
x = x' - \delta_{S,0,1}(q_{\mathrm{corr}}) = (-2 l_2) e_1 + (2 l_1) e_2 = l_2 e_1 + (2 l_1) e_2,
$$
whose pairing row $l_2(0, 1) + 2l_1(2, 0) = (l_1, l_2) \bmod 3$ matches $\ell$ identically — whereas omitting the minus sign in $x' + \delta_{S,0,1}(q_{\mathrm{corr}}) = (2 l_2) e_1 + (2 l_1) e_2$ gives $(l_1, 2 l_2) \neq (l_1, l_2)$ whenever $l_2 \not\equiv 0 \pmod 3$. Finally, in Corollary 1 the archive calls `localDualityHypotheses` at $n_{\mathrm{call}} := \lvert M \rvert = 9$ rather than $3$: embedding $\mu_3 \hookrightarrow \mu_9$ via $\zeta_3 = \zeta_9^3$ multiplies $\mathbb Z/9$-representatives by $3$, and $j_9(3j) = [3j / 9] = [j / 3] = j_3(j) \in \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ reconciles both normalizations identically.

**Example 2 (ramified quadratic twist $M = \mathbb Z/4(\chi_{-3})$ over $K = \mathbb Q_3$ at $n = \lvert M \rvert = 4$ with nontrivial $D = \mu_4(\Omega)$).** Take $K = \mathbb Q_3$ and $M = \mathbb Z/4(\chi_{-3})$ of order $n = 4$ (so $(4 : \mathbb Q_3) \neq 0$), where $\chi_{-3}$ is the ramified quadratic character of $L = \mathbb Q_3(\sqrt{-3}) / \mathbb Q_3$ ($T^2 + 3$ Eisenstein at $3$). Because $3 \equiv -1 \pmod 4$, $\mu_4(\Omega) = \{\pm 1, \pm i\} \simeq \mathbb Z/4(\chi_{-1})$ carries the *nontrivial* unramified quadratic action $\sigma(i) = -i = i^3$, so for $f \in M^\vee_D = \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb Z}(M, \mu_4(\Omega))$ and $g \in G_{\mathbb Q_3}$ with mod-$2$ coordinates $(s, t) = (\chi_{-1}(g), \chi_{-3}(g)) \in \{0, 1\}^2$,
$$
(g \cdot f)(m) = 3^s f(3^{-t} m) \equiv 3^{s \oplus t} f(m) \pmod 4 \quad \Longrightarrow \quad M^\vee_D \simeq \mathbb Z/4(\chi_3),
$$
twisted by the *third* quadratic field $\mathbb Q_3(\sqrt{3})$! Here $W = G_L$ has index $2$, $M^\vee_4$ has $4$ characters $\chi_k(m) = km \bmod 4$, $I = \bigoplus_{k=0}^3 \operatorname{Coind}_W^{G_{\mathbb Q_3}}(\mathbb Z/4)$ has cardinality $4^8 = 65{,}536$, $i(m) = (km,\, 3km)_{k=0}^3 \bmod 4$, and $Q = I / i(M)$ has cardinality $16{,}384$; the element $x_2 = (0, k)_{k=0}^3 \in I$ satisfies $(\tau - 1) x_2 = (k, 3k)_{k=0}^3 = i(1)$, witnessing non-splitting of $0 \to M \to I \to Q \to 0$ (as $\gcd([G_K : W], n) = \gcd(2, 4) = 2 > 1$).

Because $\gcd(4, 3) = 1$, every $2$-primary extension of $\mathbb Q_3$ is tamely ramified, factoring through $\Gamma_2 = \langle \sigma, \tau \mid \sigma \tau = \tau^3 \sigma \rangle$ and its order-$16$ quotient $G_{16} = \Gamma_2 / \langle \sigma^4, \tau^4 \rangle$. Crucially, because $\sigma$ acts on $D = \mu_4$ by $3 \equiv q \pmod 4$ while $\sigma \tau = \tau^3 \sigma$, expanding any $1$-cochain $h : G_{16} \to \mu_4$ gives $h(\sigma \tau) \equiv 3 h(\tau) + h(\sigma) - dh(\sigma, \tau)$ and $h(\tau^3 \sigma) \equiv h(\sigma) + 3 h(\tau) - dh(\tau, \tau) - dh(\tau^2, \tau) - dh(\tau^3, \sigma) \pmod 4$; both $h(\sigma)$ and $3 h(\tau)$ cancel identically modulo $4$ *despite the nontrivial action on $D$*, yielding the exact Brauer invariant functional
$$
t_{\mathrm{rel},\mathbb Q_3}(c_2) := c_2(\tau^3, \sigma) + c_2(\tau, \tau) + c_2(\tau^2, \tau) - c_2(\sigma, \tau) \pmod 4,
$$
normalized by $t_{\mathrm{rel},\mathbb Q_3}(x_{\mathrm{unr}} \cup y_{\mathrm{ram}}) = 1 \pmod 4$. Direct cocycle enumeration gives $H^0(G_{\mathbb Q_3}, M) = \{0, 2\} \simeq \mathbb Z/2$, $H^0(G_{\mathbb Q_3}, M^\vee_D) = \{0, 2\} \simeq \mathbb Z/2$, $H^2(G_{\mathbb Q_3}, M) \simeq H^2(G_{\mathbb Q_3}, M^\vee_D) \simeq \mathbb Z/2$, and $H^1(G_{\mathbb Q_3}, M) \simeq H^1(G_{\mathbb Q_3}, M^\vee_D) \simeq (\mathbb Z/2)^2$, with $H^1(M)$ generated by $e_1(s,t) = 2s \bmod 4$ and $e_2(s,t) = t \bmod 4$ ($= \delta_{S,0,1}(p(x_2))$) and $H^1(M^\vee_D)$ generated by $f_1(s,t) = 2s \bmod 4$ and $f_2(s,t) = (s \oplus t) \bmod 4$. Notice why $G_4 = \operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb Q_3(i, \sqrt{-3})/\mathbb Q_3) \simeq C_2 \times C_2$ alone cannot evaluate $e_2 \cup f_2$: modulo $2$, $e_2 \cup f_2$ projects to $\chi_{-3} \cup \chi_3$, which is nontrivial on $C_2 \times C_2$ but vanishes upon inflation to $G_{16}$ because $3 = N_{L/\mathbb Q_3}(\sqrt{-3})$ (§11.4.5, Example 1), placing $[e_2 \cup f_2]$ into $\ker\bigl(H^2(\mathbb Q_3, \mu_4) \to H^2(\mathbb Q_3, \mu_2)\bigr) = 2 H^2(\mathbb Q_3, \mu_4) = \{0, 2\}$. Evaluating $t_{\mathrm{rel},\mathbb Q_3}$ on $G_{16}$ yields
$$
P_{0,2} = \begin{pmatrix} 2 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad P_{1,1} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 2 \\ 2 & 2 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad P_{2,0} = \begin{pmatrix} 2 \end{pmatrix} \pmod 4.
$$
Every cohomology group has exponent $2 < n = 4$, so $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z/2, \mathbb Z/4) = \{0, 2\}$ and $\operatorname{Hom}((\mathbb Z/2)^2, \mathbb Z/4) = \{0, 2\}^2$; the matrices $(2)$ and $\begin{pmatrix}0&2\\2&2\end{pmatrix}$ map $\{0, 1\}$ and $\{0, 1\}^2$ bijectively onto $\{0, 2\}$ and $\{0, 2\}^2$, and composing with $j_4 : \{0, 2\} \xrightarrow{\sim} \{0, \frac12\} \subset \mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ gives Corollary 1's Cartier pairings.

**What the hypotheses control (four exact deletion tests).**

1. *Deleting $n$-torsion (`hnI` in Lemma 2(1) or `hM` in Lemma 1 and Step 5).* Take $n = 2$, $\Gamma = \{1\}$, $D = \mathbb Z/2$, and the non-split extension $0 \to \mathbb Z/2 \xrightarrow{\cdot 2} \mathbb Z/4 \to \mathbb Z/2 \to 0$ whose middle module fails $2 X_2 = 0$. Every $\lambda \in \operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z/4, \mathbb Z/2)$ satisfies $\lambda(2x) = 2\lambda(x) = 0$, so $f^\vee : \operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z/4, \mathbb Z/2) \to \operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z/2, \mathbb Z/2) \simeq \mathbb Z/2$ is *identically zero*, breaking `dualShortComplex_surjective_g`. Likewise, feeding $M = \mathbb Z/4$ directly at $n = 2$ breaks both `exists_injective_biproduct_coind` ($\ker i = 2\mathbb Z/4 \neq 0$) and `bijective_toDualDual` ($\lvert (M^\vee_2)^\vee_2 \rvert = 2 < 4 = \lvert M \rvert$).
2. *Deleting finiteness `[Finite M]` while keeping discreteness and $n M = 0$.* Let $\Gamma = \mathbb Z_3$, $n = 3$, $D = \mathbb Z/3$, and $M = \bigoplus_{k \ge 1} \mathbb Z/3$ with $k$-th summand inflated from $\mathbb Z_3 / 3^k \mathbb Z_3$ (or trivial action). Each element is fixed by an open subgroup $3^k \mathbb Z_3$, so $M$ is discrete and $3$-torsion, yet $\bigcap_{k \ge 1} 3^k \mathbb Z_3 = \{0\}$ is *not open* (`fixingOpenSubgroup` fails), $M^\vee_D = \prod_{k \ge 1} \mathbb Z/3$ fails `isDiscreteRep_dualRep` under nontrivial actions, and even for trivial action $\lvert (M^\vee_3)^\vee_3 \rvert = 2^{2^{\aleph_0}} > \aleph_0 = \lvert M \rvert$ breaks both `bijective_toDualDual` and `bijective_of_bijective_flip` in Step 5.
3. *Deleting degree-three vanishing `subsingleton_three` (and `bijective_cores`).* Let $\Gamma = C_2 = \operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb C / \mathbb R)$ (compact totally disconnected of order $2$, corresponding to an archimedean local field), $n = 2$, and $D = \mathbb Z/2$. For $M = \mathbb Z/2$, Lemma 1 produces the exact sequence $S : 0 \to \mathbb Z/2 \xrightarrow{\mathrm{diag}} \mathbb F_2[C_2] \to \mathbb Z/2 \to 0$, which is self-dual ($T \cong S$). By Shapiro's lemma $H^r(C_2, \mathbb F_2[C_2]) = 0$ for all $r \ge 1$, whereas $\lvert H^r(C_2, \mathbb Z/2) \rvert = 2$ for *every* $r \ge 0$. In Step 2 (`surjective_zero`), the map $i^{\vee,2} : H^2(C_2, I^\vee) = 0 \to H^2(C_2, M^\vee) = \mathbb Z/2$ is zero because its cokernel injects into $H^3(C_2, Q^\vee) \simeq \mathbb Z/2 \neq 0$, blocking the lift of the nontrivial functional on $H^2(C_2, M^\vee)$; simultaneously, $\operatorname{cor}_{\{1\}}^{C_2} : H^2(\{1\}, \mathbb Z/2) = 0 \to H^2(C_2, \mathbb Z/2) = \mathbb Z/2$ is zero, violating `bijective_cores`.
4. *Deleting $(\lvert M \rvert : K) \neq 0$ in Corollary 1.* Over $K = \mathbb F_p((T))$ with $M = \mathbb Z/p$, $(\lvert M \rvert : K) = 0$. In characteristic $p$, $X^p - 1 = (X - 1)^p$ collapses $\mu_p(K^{\mathrm{sep}}) = \{1\}$, so $M^\ast = \operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb Z/p, K^{\mathrm{sep},\times}) = 0$ and $H^{2-r}_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, M^\ast) = 0$, whereas Artin–Schreier classes $[T^{-m}] \in K / \wp(K)$ ($\gcd(m, p) = 1$, $\wp(x) = x^p - x$) have distinct negative pole valuations not divisible by $p$, making $H^1_{\mathrm{cts}}(G_K, \mathbb Z/p)$ infinite and breaking both finiteness and duality (which in characteristic $p$ requires flat/Milne–Kato differential cohomology rather than Galois $\mu_p$).

**Remark on precedence and actual consumers.** Theorem 1 and Corollary 1 are the classical local duality theorem of John Tate for arbitrary finite Galois modules of order prime to $\operatorname{char}(K)$ (including mixed-characteristic $p$-torsion), proved by abstract profinite dévissage as in Milne's *Arithmetic Duality Theorems*. Where Milne embeds $M \hookrightarrow \operatorname{Ind}_W^\Gamma(M|_W)$ and reduces $M|_W$ to cyclic prime-power summands, the archive embeds $M \hookrightarrow \bigoplus_{\chi \in M^\vee_n} \operatorname{Coind}_W^\Gamma(\mathbb Z/n)$ at the single uniform exponent $n = \lvert M \rvert$ so every summand reduces directly to §11.4.5's constant-coefficient pairing, and executes dimension shifting through the five explicit universally quantified steps above. [Paper] [Tate, *Duality theorems in Galois cohomology over number fields*, Proc. ICM Stockholm 1962, §1 and Theorem 2.1, pp. 288–289](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/BSDseminar/refs/TateICM.pdf) · [Milne, *Arithmetic Duality Theorems*, 2nd ed., 2006, I §1 Theorems 1.8 and 1.13, pp. 21–25, and I §2 Theorem 2.1, Corollary 2.3 and Remark 2.5, pp. 26–29](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf)
