The open-subgroup Kummer input to the degree-three vanishing is proved in [§11.4.7](#1147-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three).

The low-degree finiteness input to the same bundle is proved in [§11.4.8](#1148-low-degree-finiteness-on-an-open-subgroup).

### 11.4.7 The open-subgroup Kummer bridge in degree three

A Brauer class of invariant $[1/3]$ over a local field is three times another class. Take the class of invariant $[1/9]$. This is the divisibility needed in the Kummer sequence. It is a statement about degree two, while Kummer exactness on coefficients comes from solving a power equation in the separable closure. Together with the degree-three vanishing for multiplicative coefficients supplied by local class formation, divisibility gives the root-of-unity vanishing used on the way to §11.4.6. We will see this over $\mathbb Q_3(\zeta_3)$ and $\mathbb Q_2(\sqrt{2})$, where the same calculation works with two different actions on $\mu_3$. The exact sequence with its natural root-of-unity action is classical. [Paper] [Milne, *Arithmetic Duality Theorems*, I §2, p. 27](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf)

**Coefficients and restriction.** Fix a non-archimedean local field $K$, a separable closure $\Omega=K_{\mathrm{sep}}$, a natural number $n$ whose image in $K$ is nonzero, and an arbitrary open subgroup $V$ of $G_K=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/K)$. Put $E=\Omega^V$. The open-subgroup dictionary identifies $V$ with $G_E=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/E)$ with its Krull topology. The field $E/K$ is finite separable and is local for the extended valuation. Write $B=\Omega^\times$ and $A=\mu_n(\Omega)=\{u\in B:u^n=1\}$ with discrete topology and the restricted Galois actions. In Lean, `galUnits` is all of $B$ written additively, and `galRootsOfUnity` is the additive version of $A$. Each element is fixed by the open subgroup fixing the finite field it generates. In particular, the topology used for these cochains is discrete on the coefficients and comes from the Krull topology on $V$. [Lean, read] A2, `GaloisModules/Discrete.lean:31–40,57–80` and `CohomologyFunctoriality.lean:16–32`. [Pinned sources](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three)

**Lemma 11.4.7a (the restricted sequence and its connecting map).** There is a short exact sequence of discrete $V$-modules
$$
1\longrightarrow A\xrightarrow{\iota}B\xrightarrow{g}B\longrightarrow 1,
\qquad g(u)=u^n .
$$
On continuous cohomology, $g_*$ is multiplication by $n$ in every degree. The connecting map $\delta_2:H^2(V,B)\to H^3(V,A)$ sends a cocycle $z$ to the class of $d^2\widetilde z$, viewed in $A$, where $\widetilde z$ is any locally constant cochain with $\widetilde z^n=z$. Here and below $H^i$ denotes continuous cohomology.

*Proof.* The inclusion $\iota$ and the power map are equivariant for the actual field action. The kernel of $g$ consists of the $n$-th roots of unity. For every $b\in B$, the polynomial $X^n-b$ has positive degree and is separable: its derivative is $nX^{n-1}$ and $0$ is no root of $X^n-b$. Hence it has a root in $\Omega$, which is nonzero. This proves surjectivity. The restriction functor keeps the underlying modules and linear maps and precomposes the actions with $V\hookrightarrow G_K$. Thus it carries the Kummer sequence to the same sequence of abelian groups, now equivariant for $V$. This is the construction of `kummerSequenceRes` and `kummerSequenceRes_shortExact`. These two declarations work for a subgroup, with openness used at the local class-formation step below. [Lean, read] A2, `GaloisCohomology/UnitsInflationCochainsFHomApply.lean:36–56`, `GaloisCohomology/Kummer.lean:196–248`, and `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean:20–49`. [Pinned sources](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three)

The continuous inhomogeneous cochains in degree $i$ are the locally constant maps $V^i\to B$, or $V^i\to A$ for the first term. A set-theoretic section of $g$ lifts a locally constant cochain by composition, which preserves local constancy. The section need only choose one lift of each value. The kernel of the pointwise power map is the set of cochains valued in $A$. The maps commute with the differential by equivariance. In multiplicative notation the degree-two differential is
$$
(d^2 u)(s,t,r)=s(u(t,r))\,u(st,r)^{-1}\,u(s,tr)\,u(s,t)^{-1}.
$$
In particular $d^2\widetilde z$ takes values in $A$ because its $n$-th power is $d^2z=1$. The identity $d^3d^2=1$ on cochains makes it a cocycle. Changing the lift multiplies it by an $A$-valued coboundary. Changing $z$ by a coboundary allows the corresponding lift to be changed by the coboundary of a lifted one-cochain, which leaves the connecting class unchanged. This is the lift-and-differentiate formula for the connecting map of the degreewise short exact cochain complexes. In Lean the coefficient map is $n$ times the identity, so its cochain map acts pointwise as repeated addition. Passing to cocycles modulo coboundaries gives `cohomologyMap_kummerSequenceRes_g_apply`. [Lean, read] A2, `ContinuousCochains.lean:13–20,34–108,127–128`, `ContinuousCochains/Functoriality.lean:17–91`, `ContinuousCochains/LongExactSequence.lean:28–89`, and pinned Mathlib, `Algebra/Homology/HomologySequence.lean:292–352`. [Pinned sources](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three)

**Theorem 11.4.7b (degree-three vanishing on the same open subgroup).** With the above data, assume the local class-formation input $H^3(V,B)=0$. Then $H^3(V,A)=0$. More generally, the exact sequence identifies the two possible sources of a class in $H^3(V,A)$ by
$$
0\longrightarrow H^2(V,B)/nH^2(V,B)
\xrightarrow{\bar\delta_2} H^3(V,A)
\xrightarrow{\iota_*} H^3(V,B)[n]
\longrightarrow 0,
$$
where $[n]$ on a group denotes the kernel of multiplication by $n$. [Lean, read] A2, `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean:156–177` for the vanishing, with the short exact description obtained from its long exact sequence. [Pinned sources](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three)

*Proof.* The relevant five terms are
$$
H^2(V,B)\xrightarrow{n}H^2(V,B)\xrightarrow{\delta_2}H^3(V,A)
 \xrightarrow{\iota_*}H^3(V,B)\xrightarrow{n}H^3(V,B).
$$
At the second term, exactness gives $\ker\delta_2=nH^2(V,B)$. At the third, $\operatorname{im}\delta_2=\ker\iota_*$. At the fourth, $\operatorname{im}\iota_*=H^3(V,B)[n]$. These identities give the asserted short exact sequence.

The class formation `localClassFormationOfIsSepClosure K Ksep` supplies one additive bijection
$$
\operatorname{inv}_V:H^2(V,B)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z .
$$
It is the local invariant of $E=\Omega^V$ transported through the open-subgroup identification. **Both** degree-two terms use this same map: each is the cohomology of the same $V$-module $B$, and the arrow between them is induced by $g$ on coefficients. Under $\operatorname{inv}_V$ this arrow is multiplication by $n$, with no subgroup index or change of normalization. Given $z$ in its target, choose $r\in\mathbb Q$ representing $\operatorname{inv}_V(z)$. Let $w$ have invariant $[r/n]$. Then $\operatorname{inv}_V(nw)=\operatorname{inv}_V(z)$, so $nw=z$. Thus $\delta_2(z)=0$ for every $z$. The hypothesis $H^3(V,B)=0$ makes $\iota_*$ the zero map, hence $\delta_2$ is onto. The zero map can be onto only if $H^3(V,A)=0$.

In the proof term, `h3.elim` gives $\iota_*(x)=0$ for the chosen $x\in H^3(V,A)$. `cohomology_exact₁` then produces $z$ with $\delta_2(z)=x$. `QZ.exists_nsmul_eq` chooses $[r/n]$, `F.inv_bijective` lifts that value to $w$ and detects the equality $g_*(w)=z$, and `comp_δ` kills $\delta_2(g_*(w))$. The proof concerns the twisted module $A$ throughout. All it uses about the action is discreteness and equivariance of the two coefficient maps, so $\mu_n(\Omega)\subset E$ is unnecessary. The class-formation input and the Brauer invariant precede this step. Invoking the final Tate duality theorem here would use a conclusion whose construction consumes this vanishing. [Lean, read] A2, `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean:158–177`. [Pinned sources and inherited inputs](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three)

**Two calculations in absolute Galois cohomology.** For each field $E$ below, take the degree-three and degree-nine unramified extensions $E_3\subset E_9$ inside the chosen $\Omega$, and write $\pi$ for the given uniformizer of $E$. For $m=3,9$ let $a_m(s)\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}$ be the exponent with which $s\in V$ acts on $E_m$ as a power of arithmetic Frobenius. Use the carry $c_m(i,j)=\lfloor(i+j)/m\rfloor$ and put
$$
z(s,t)=\pi^{c_3(a_3(s),a_3(t))},
\qquad
w(s,t)=\pi^{c_9(a_9(s),a_9(t))}.
$$
These cochains already live on $V$. They are the inflations of specific unramified cocycles with values in $E^\times$. Indeed $c_m$ is the rational coboundary of the lift $i\mapsto i/m$ of the character $i\mapsto[i/m]$, so $d^2 c_m=0$. The action on the values $\pi^j$ is trivial. Under valuation on the unramified layer, the cocycle $\pi^{c_m}$ becomes $c_m$, since $v_{E_m}(\pi)=1$. The inverse of the connecting map for $0\to\mathbb Z\to\mathbb Q\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z\to0$ sends $[c_m]$ to the character $i\mapsto[i/m]$. Evaluating at arithmetic Frobenius gives $[1/m]$. By compatibility of the normalized invariant with unramified inflation, these are the invariants of the actual continuous classes $[z]$ and $[w]$ in $H^2(V,B)$, so each class is nonzero. [Paper] [Milne, *Class Field Theory*, III §1, pp. 98–100 and Theorems 1.7 and 2.1](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf) · [Milne, *Algebraic Number Theory*, Example 7.54](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf)

The equality $3[w]=[z]$ has an explicit witness. Write $a_9(s)=3k(s)+a_3(s)$, with $k(s)\in\{0,1,2\}$. The identity $a_m(s)+a_m(t)-a_m(st)=m c_m(a_m(s),a_m(t))$ gives $c_3=3c_9-d^1k$ on $V$. Thus $z=w^3 d^1(\pi^{-k})$. Choose $\gamma\in\Omega$ with $\gamma^3=\pi$ and put $v(s)=\gamma^{-k(s)}$. The cochain $\widetilde z=w\,d^1v$ satisfies $\widetilde z^3=z$ since the cube map commutes with $d^1$ even if $V$ acts on $\gamma$. Its differential is $d^2w\cdot d^2d^1v=1$, so this lift gives the trivial connecting cocycle. For a sign check write $s(\gamma)=\zeta_3^{b(s)}\gamma$ and $s(\zeta_3)=\zeta_3^{\epsilon(s)}$. If $u(s,t)=\gamma^{c_3(a_3(s),a_3(t))}$ is the pointwise carry lift, then
$$
\widetilde z(s,t)/u(s,t)=\zeta_3^{-b(s)k(t)},
\qquad
(d^2u)(s,t,r)=\zeta_3^{b(s)c_3(a_3(t),a_3(r))}.
$$
The relation $b(st)=b(s)+\epsilon(s)b(t)$ modulo $3$ and $d^1 k=3c_9-c_3$ give $d^2(\widetilde z/u)=(d^2u)^{-1}$. The exponent identities hold on all of $V$. For these particular cochains one can also check them on a joint finite quotient. In the first example, $E_9(\gamma)/E$ has group $C_9\times C_3$. In the second, $E_{18}(\gamma)/E$ has group $C_3\rtimes C_{18}$, with Frobenius acting on $C_3$ by inversion. Indeed $X^3-\pi$ is Eisenstein over the unramified layer in each case, and Frobenius lifts by fixing $\gamma$ in the power basis. The layer contains $\mu_3$ in each case, so its relative automorphisms send $\gamma$ to $\zeta_3^b\gamma$. These are finite quotients through which the displayed representatives and lift factor.

1. **$K=\mathbb Q_3$, $n=3$.** Take $E=\mathbb Q_3(\zeta_3)$ and $V=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/E)$. The element $\pi=\zeta_3-1$ satisfies $\pi^2+3\pi+3=0$. This polynomial is Eisenstein at $3$, so $E/K$ is totally ramified quadratic, $\pi$ has valuation one in $E$, and the residue field is $\mathbb F_3$. The full group $G_K$ acts on $\mu_3$ through the quadratic quotient that interchanges $\zeta_3$ and $\zeta_3^{-1}$. The action of this $V$ is trivial. On a pair of exponents $(2,2)$ in the unramified ninth-degree layer, $z=\pi$, $w=1$, and $k(s)+k(t)-k(st)=-1$, so $d^1(\pi^{-k})=\pi$. On $(8,8)$ the two cocycles take the value $\pi$, and the correction is $\pi^{-2}$, giving $\pi^3\pi^{-2}=\pi$. Their invariants are $[1/3]$ and $[1/9]$, with $3[1/9]=[1/3]$ and $\delta_2([z])=0$ by the displayed lift. Taking $V=G_K$ instead gives the same cochain identities with uniformizer $3$, while the quadratic action remains. Here $3$ is nonzero in $K$ even though it vanishes in its residue field. [Paper] [Milne, *Algebraic Number Theory*, Proposition 7.55](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf)
2. **$K=\mathbb Q_2$, $n=3$.** Take $E=\mathbb Q_2(\sqrt 2)$ and $V=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/E)$, with $\pi=\sqrt2$. The polynomial $X^2-2$ is Eisenstein, giving a totally ramified quadratic extension with residue field $\mathbb F_2$. The polynomial $X^2+X+1$ reduces to an irreducible separable polynomial over $\mathbb F_2$. Its roots give the unramified quadratic extension of $E$ and lift the two primitive cube roots of unity of $\mathbb F_4$. Arithmetic Frobenius squares those reductions, so on $\mu_3$ it sends $\zeta_3$ to $\zeta_3^2=\zeta_3^{-1}$, and inertia fixes $\mu_3$. Thus the action remains nontrivial on this proper open subgroup. With $\pi=\sqrt2$, exponents $(2,2)$ give $z=\sqrt2$, $w=1$ and correction $\sqrt2$. Exponents $(8,8)$ give $z=w=\sqrt2$ and correction $1/2$, with $(\sqrt2)^3/2=\sqrt2$. Valuation and Frobenius give $[1/3]$ and $[1/9]$ in $\operatorname{Br}(E)$, and the same $\widetilde z=w\,d^1v$ gives $\delta_2([z])=0$ in $H^3(V,\mu_3)$. For $V=G_K$ the uniformizer is $2$ and the action is again unramified quadratic. For the subgroup fixing the unramified quadratic extension of $\mathbb Q_2$, the action becomes trivial. [Paper] [Milne, *Algebraic Number Theory*, Propositions 7.31, 7.50 and 7.55 and Example 7.54](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf)

**What the two vanishing inputs do.** The short exact description in the theorem says that the exact necessary and sufficient conditions are surjectivity of $n$ on $H^2(V,B)$ and $H^3(V,B)[n]=0$. The hypothesis $H^3(V,B)=0$ implies the second condition.

- **Delete surjectivity.** An abstract complex $D$ has $D^2=\mathbb Q$, $D^3=\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, with differential the quotient and all other terms zero. Multiplication by $3$ is degreewise surjective, and its kernel complex $J$ has only $J^3=(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[3]$. Here $H^2(D)=\mathbb Z$, $H^3(D)=0$. The connecting map of $0\to J\to D\xrightarrow{3}D\to0$ takes $1$ to $[1/3]$ by lifting to $1/3$ and applying the quotient. Its kernel is $3\mathbb Z$ and it accounts for all of $H^3(J)$. This is an abstract cochain-complex model.
- **Delete degree-three unit vanishing.** Take an abstract complex with $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ in degrees two and three and zero differentials. Multiplication by $3$ is again degreewise surjective. The kernel complex has $H^3(J)=(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[3]$, and inclusion identifies it with $H^3(D)[3]$. The degree-two arrow remains surjective and the connecting map is zero. A finite group-cohomology version uses $C_3$ acting trivially on $B_0=\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ and $A_0=B_0[3]$. In the cyclic periodic cochain complex the maps alternate between $s-1=0$ and the norm $3$ starting in degree zero with $s-1$. Hence $H^2(C_3,B_0)=0$, $H^3(C_3,B_0)=B_0[3]$, and $H^3(C_3,A_0)=A_0$ maps isomorphically to $H^3(C_3,B_0)$. This uses the finite cyclic resolution as a check of the maps on the right of the sequence.

These are controls for the exact-sequence argument. The $C_3$ module example is a finite-group calculation. The unramified quotients in the local examples only supply specific cochains on the absolute Galois group, with the separate invariant comparison identifying their classes. [Expository] [Pinned cyclic cohomology formulas](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RepresentationTheory/Homological/GroupCohomology/FiniteCyclic.lean#L64-L140)

**Where the result is used.** In `Vanishing.lean:44–52`, `subsingleton_cohomology_three_rootsOfUnity` passes the class-formation unit vanishing to `subsingleton_cohomology_three_rootsOfUnity_open`. For a prime $\ell$ with nonzero image in $K$, `cdp_galois_le_two` then calls this result at $n=\ell$ for the open subgroup that supplies the class on a closed pro-$\ell$ Sylow subgroup $P$ (`:132–147`). Only at the next step does it use triviality of the $P$-action on $\mu_\ell$, to compare with the constant $\mathbb Z/\ell$ module (`:149–177`). The resulting $\operatorname{cd}_\ell(G_K)\le2$ enters `subsingleton_cohomology_of_three_le` (`:179–195`), and the finite-module interface `subsingleton_cohomology_three_galois` supplies the degree-three hypothesis consumed in §11.4.6. [Lean] A2, `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing.lean:16–19,45–48` and `LocalTateDuality/MainDefs.lean:88–95`. [Pinned sources](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three)

Milne gives the same local Kummer vanishing directly in the proof of I.2.1. His I.1.6(b) records the local multiplicative class formation and I.1.12 records the strict dimension bound furnishing the multiplicative vanishing. The unramified normalization and the full invariant are classical, as in Milne's *Class Field Theory* III and Serre's *Local Fields* XIII §3. The work here is a specialization and an explanation of the archived arrows, including explicit continuous representatives and controls. Source reading covers the degree-three Kummer proof and the direct suppliers listed in the ledger. Lean kernel replay, declaration-level reachability from the exported main theorem, and peer review have separate evidence requirements. [Paper] [Milne ADT](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf) · [Milne CFT](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/CFT.pdf) · [Serre](https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/Serre-Local.pdf)

### 11.4.8 Low-degree finiteness on an open subgroup

**Motivation.** The passage from roots of unity to general finite local coefficients in §11.4.6 requires finite cohomology in degrees zero, one and two. The restricted Kummer sequence of [§11.4.7](#1147-the-open-subgroup-kummer-bridge-in-degree-three) gives two different arrows. In degree one, elements of the fixed field give classes by taking a root in the separable closure. In degree two, a class has a local invariant through its image with multiplicative coefficients. The first arrow gives a finite list covering the classes. The second gives each class a distinct label in a finite group. [Lean, read] [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

**Examples to keep in view.** Use the two proper open subgroups of §11.4.7 and $n=3$. Over $E=\mathbb Q_2(\sqrt 2)$ every valuation-ring unit is a cube. The power classes record only valuation modulo three. Over $E=\mathbb Q_3(\zeta_3)$, following the principal units through depth four gives 27 unit classes and 81 field power classes. In both examples degree two has the same finite target $(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[3]$. [Expository]

**Definitions.** Let $K$ be a non-archimedean local field, $\Omega=K_{\mathrm{sep}}$ a chosen separable closure, and $V$ any open subgroup of $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/K)$ with its Krull topology. Set $E=\Omega^V$. Then $E/K$ is finite separable, $E$ is local for the extended valuation, and $V$ acts on $\Omega$ as its absolute Galois group. Take $n\in\mathbb N$ with $(n:K)\ne0$. The field embedding gives $(n:E)\ne0$, and $n>0$. Use the discrete modules $A=\mu_n(\Omega)$ and $B=\Omega^\times$ with their restricted actions. Cohomology is continuous and written additively. For an additive group $C$ write $C[n]=\ker(n:C\to C)$. With $\mathcal O_E$ the valuation ring and $\mathfrak m$ its maximal ideal, use $U_0=\mathcal O_E^\times$ and $U_j=1+\mathfrak m^j$ for $j\ge1$. [Lean, read] Inherited from §§11.4.5 and 11.4.7. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

**Lemma 11.4.8a (the finite source).** Normalize $v_E(\pi)=1$ at a uniformizer $\pi$, put $d=v_E(n)$ and $N=2d+1$, and write $q=|\mathcal O_E/\mathfrak m|$. The finite set $\{0,\ldots,n-1\}\times U_0/U_N$ maps onto $E^\times/(E^\times)^n$ by $(r,\bar u)\mapsto[\pi^r u]$.

*Proof.* Apply the finite-power-quotient lemma of [§11.4.5](#1145-from-two-zero-annihilators-to-a-perfect-local-cup-pairing) over $E$. Its Hensel test for $X^n-w$ at $1$ is $v_E(1-w)\ge2d+1>2v_E(n)$, giving a unit root for every $w\in U_N$. The factor at depth zero is $U_0/U_1\simeq k^\times$ and the subsequent factors are $U_j/U_{j+1}\simeq (k,+)$ for $j\ge1$, where $k=\mathcal O_E/\mathfrak m$. Hence $[U_0:U_N]=(q-1)q^{N-1}$ and changing $u$ by $U_N$ preserves the power class. Write $x=\pi^a u$ and divide $a=r+nb$ in $\mathbb Z$ with $0\le r<n$. The discarded factor is the $n$-th power of $\pi^b$, also for negative $a$. This is the representative used by `finite_quotient_range_pow`. [Lean, read] Inherited from §11.4.5, A2 `GroundTheory/LocalFields/UnitsPowers.lean:156–182,195–235` and `UnitsBasic.lean:275–410`. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

**Lemma 11.4.8b (the finite target).** The elements of $(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[n]$ are $[a/n]$ for $0\le a<n$, each occurring once.

*Proof.* If $n[r]=0$, then $nr=m\in\mathbb Z$. Write $m=a+nb$ with $0\le a<n$. Subtracting $b$ from $r$ gives $a/n$. All listed classes are killed by $n$, and two listed representatives differ by an integer only when equal. A2 defines `QZ` as the rational additive circle of period one. The pinned Mathlib proof chooses the residue in `Fin n` and uses regularity of multiplication by $n$ in $\mathbb Q$ to show that a residue determines at most one torsion class. A2 consumes finiteness of this subtype. [Lean, read] A2 `TorsionDualDefs.lean:8–24`, `TorsionDualFinite.lean:11–12` and pinned `AddCircle/Defs.lean:239–265,285–286`. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

**Theorem 11.4.8c (the low-degree input).** For the data above and $0\le r\le2$, $H^r(V,A)$ is finite. Its degree-zero group identifies with $\mu_n(E)$. The finite maps used in degrees one and two are
$$
E^\times/(E^\times)^n\twoheadrightarrow H^1(V,A),
\qquad H^2(V,A)\hookrightarrow(\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z)[n].
$$

*Proof.* Inherit $1\to A\xrightarrow{\iota}B\xrightarrow{g}B\to1$ with $g(b)=b^n$ and $H^r(g)=n$ from §11.4.7. Use the inherited Hilbert 90 assertion $H^1(V,B)=0$ and the additive local invariant $\operatorname{inv}_V:H^2(V,B)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$ from the class formation on this very subgroup. [Lean] [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

A degree-zero cochain is determined by its value, since $V^0$ has one point. The differential at $s$ is $s(a)/a$ in multiplicative notation. There are no incoming boundaries. Thus `H0Iso` identifies degree zero with invariants. For $A$ these are the roots in $E$, a subset of the finite root set of $X^n-1$. For either $B$, inclusion takes $E^\times$ to the invariant units. Conversely the field value of a fixed unit belongs to $E$ and is nonzero there. Constructing the unit in $E$ from that nonzero value gives a preimage with exactly the original value in $\Omega$. [Lean, read] A2 `ContinuousCochains/Functoriality.lean:164–212` and `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean:83–111,181–190`. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

The exact segment in degree one is
$$
H^0(V,B)\xrightarrow{n}H^0(V,B)\xrightarrow{\delta_0}
H^1(V,A)\xrightarrow{\iota_*}H^1(V,B).
$$
The group on the right vanishes, so every class comes from an invariant unit via the connecting map. For $a\in E^\times$, choose $b^n=a$ in $\Omega^\times$. The representative of $\delta_0(a)$ is $s\mapsto s(b)/b$. Its $n$-th power is $s(a)/a=1$, and expanding $st(b)/b$ gives the cocycle identity. The cocycle is locally constant because $b$ has open stabilizer. Changing $b$ by a root of unity changes the representative by an $A$-valued coboundary. Multiplying chosen roots for two elements gives additivity on classes. A power $a^n$ maps to zero because one can take the $V$-fixed root $a$ itself. The source gets this zero using naturality of `H0Iso` for the power map and the zero composite with $\delta_0$. The homomorphism therefore descends to the power quotient, and the same preimage in $E^\times$ provides a preimage in that quotient. Lemma 11.4.8a gives finiteness. [Lean, read] A2 `GaloisCohomology/Kummer.lean:35–110` and `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean:67–134`. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

In degree two the exact segment is
$$
H^1(V,B)\xrightarrow{\delta_1}H^2(V,A)\xrightarrow{\iota_*}
H^2(V,B)\xrightarrow{n}H^2(V,B).
$$
Hilbert 90 gives zero kernel for $\iota_*$. The composite on its right is zero, so $n\,\iota_*c=0$ and $n\operatorname{inv}_V(\iota_*c)=0$. If the labels of two classes agree, injectivity of $\operatorname{inv}_V$ gives equality of the unit-coefficient classes, and injectivity of $\iota_*$ gives equality of the original classes. This is the injection into Lemma 11.4.8b used by `Finite.of_injective`. Both degree-two copies of $B$ have the same coefficient module and subgroup, so the calculation uses one invariant. The wrapper installs finiteness of the restricted root module and dispatches $r=0,1,2$ to the three results. [Lean, read] A2 `GaloisCohomology/Kummer.lean:114–120,128–130`, `LocalClassFormation/FiniteVanishing/KummerOpen.lean:138–154,181–190` and `FiniteVanishing/Devissage.lean:93–95`. [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

**Example 1 (residue characteristic two).** Take $K=\mathbb Q_2$, $E=K(\sqrt 2)$, $V=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/E)$ and $n=3$. The Eisenstein polynomial $X^2-2$ gives $\pi=\sqrt 2$ and residue field $\mathbb F_2$. The polynomial $X^2+X+1$ reduces to an irreducible quadratic there, so only $1$ among the cube roots of unity belongs to $E$. The primitive roots are in the unramified quadratic extension, where arithmetic Frobenius squares them. The restricted action on $\mu_3(\Omega)$ is thus nontrivial, as in §11.4.7.

Here $d=0$ and $N=1$. The residue-unit quotient is trivial, so $U_0=U_1$. For every $w\in U_1$, $X^3-w$ has the simple root $1$ modulo $\pi$ since its derivative there is $3$. Hensel lifting gives a root in $U_1$, hence $U_1^3=U_1$. The valuation distinguishes the three power classes $1,\pi,\pi^2$, and removing the valuation coordinate modulo three leaves a unit cube. Consequently $H^0(V,A)$ has one element, the connecting classes of $1,\pi,\pi^2$ cover $H^1(V,A)$, and $H^2(V,A)$ injects into the three rational classes $[0],[1/3],[2/3]$. In particular $\delta_0(\pi)$ is nonzero. A coboundary representative would allow one to multiply the chosen root of $\pi$ by an inverse root of unity to obtain a root fixed by $V$. A cube root in $E$ would have valuation $1/3$, impossible for the normalized integer valuation. [Expository] [Milne, *Algebraic Number Theory*, Propositions 7.31, 7.50 and 7.55](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf)

**Example 2 (residue characteristic three, field characteristic zero).** Take $K=\mathbb Q_3$, $E=K(\zeta_3)$, $V=\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/E)$ and $n=3$. The relation $\pi^2+3\pi+3=0$ for $\pi=\zeta_3-1$ is Eisenstein. The residue field is $\mathbb F_3$, $v_E(3)=2$, and $\epsilon=3/\pi^2=-1/(1+\pi)$ is a unit. All three roots of unity lie in $E$. Their images in $U_1/U_2$ are all three residues, since $(1+\pi)^a\equiv1+a\pi\bmod\pi^2$ for $a=0,1,2$. Thus every principal unit is a unique product of a cube root of unity and an element of $U_2$, and its cube is the cube of that second factor.

The general bound gives $U_5\subset U_0^3$. To get the whole principal-unit image use
$$
(1+\pi^2 x)^3
 =1+\pi^4\bigl(\epsilon x+\epsilon\pi^2x^2+\pi^2x^3\bigr),
 \qquad x\in\mathcal O_E .
$$
This puts $U_2^3$ in $U_4$. For $1+\pi^4 y\in U_4$, the polynomial in parentheses minus $y$ reduces to $\bar\epsilon x-\bar y$ and has a simple root. Hensel lifting gives an exact solution in $\mathcal O_E$ for each $y$, so $U_1^3=U_2^3=U_4$. The residue units are represented by $\{1,-1\}$ and cubing fixes those representatives, hence $U_0^3=\{1,-1\}U_4$. The quotient $U_1/U_4$ has three successive factors of order three. A complete list of 27 representatives is
$$
\zeta_3^a(1+\pi^2)^b(1+\pi^3)^c,\qquad 0\le a,b,c<3 .
$$
Remove the factor at each of depths $1,2,3$ in order by matching its leading residue. This leaves a unit in $U_4$. The first unequal exponent distinguishes two representatives in the corresponding graded quotient. Combining these representatives with $\pi^r$ for $0\le r<3$ gives the 81 power classes. Here $H^0(V,A)$ has three elements, the 81 connecting classes cover $H^1(V,A)$, and the invariant gives a degree-two injection into $[0],[1/3],[2/3]$. The valuation again gives $\delta_0(\pi)\ne0$. [Expository] [Milne, *Algebraic Number Theory*, Propositions 7.31 and 7.55](https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/ANT.pdf)

**Checking the finite residue calculations.** In each quadratic field the terms of $a+b\pi$ for $a,b\in\mathbb Q_p^\times$ have valuations $2v_p(a)$ and $2v_p(b)+1$. These are distinct, so the smaller one is the valuation of the sum. Therefore $(1,\pi)$ is an integral basis over $\mathbb Z_p$. Reducing coefficients modulo $p^{\lceil N/2\rceil}$ gives a finite ring that maps onto $\mathcal O_E/(\pi^N)$, because $p$ is a unit times $\pi^2$. The quadratic relation gives exact multiplication in this ring. The filtration and Hensel arguments above identify which finite cosets give classes in $E^\times$.

**Deleting the field-cast hypothesis.** Specialize the characteristic-$p$ control in §11.4.5 to $K=E=\mathbb F_p((T))$ and $V=G_K$. For $n=p$, valuation excludes a $p$-th root of $T$ in $K$, and $X^p-T$ has a unique root in an algebraic closure, purely inseparable over $K$. It is absent from $\Omega$. For any $p\mid n$, an $n$-th root of $T$ would give a $p$-th root by raising to $n/p$. The last coefficient arrow of the original Kummer sequence thus fails surjectivity. Already for $n=p$ the power subgroup is $\mathbb F_p((T^p))^\times$. For positive $a<b$ prime to $p$, the ratio $(1+T^a)/(1+T^b)$ has first nonconstant term $T^a$, precluding a $p$-th power. This gives infinitely many power classes. The Galois module $\mu_p(\Omega)$ itself is trivial because $X^p-1=(X-1)^p$. [Expository]

**Where the finite groups enter.** In `FiniteVanishing/Trivial.lean:107–145,180–183` the ambient $n$ kills a trivial finite module, and a chosen composition factor has prime order $p\mid n$. Thus $(p:K)\ne0$. The subgroup there fixes the $n$-th roots of unity and hence the $p$-th roots. The injection into the module has source the resulting $\mu_p(\Omega)$, and line 181 supplies finiteness of its degree-$r$ cohomology. The smaller-cardinality induction gives finite cohomology of the quotient. Exactness yields finiteness for the middle group. `FiniteVanishing.lean:23–41` uses this through `finite_cohomology_of_forall_trivial` in its low-degree branch. `LocalTateDuality/MainDefs.lean:91` installs the resulting universal finite-cohomology field of `localDualityHypotheses`. Its degree-three field and pairing field come from the paths in §§11.4.7 and 11.4.5, respectively. General local duality and the cardinal argument in Chapter 10 §10.4.15 enter at their existing downstream interfaces. [Lean] [Source ledger](../sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-low-degree-kummer-finiteness)

**Classical scope.** Milne's proof of I.2.1 gives the Kummer cohomology table for $n$ prime to the field characteristic of a non-archimedean local field. Taking that field to be $E$ with the same $\Omega$ and action covers the low-degree result, including mixed characteristic with residue characteristic dividing $n$. Tate's Theorem 2.1 gives the general finite-coefficient statement under invertibility of the module order in the field. The proof here uses the Kummer arrows and the local class-formation inputs. The book contributes this reading of the archived production and the two fixed-field power-quotient calculations. [Paper] [Milne, *Arithmetic Duality Theorems*, I §2, pp. 26–27](https://www.jmilne.org/math/Books/ADTnot.pdf) · [Tate, Stockholm ICM 1962, §§1–2, pp. 288–289](https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/BSDseminar/refs/TateICM.pdf)

Three community observations (`[Community]`) help orient specialists reading into the 157,603-module cone:

1. **Where the genuine mathematical novelty of P12 sits (`PropGK` and `PfromAut`):** As reported in
   LUMI's `docs/06-window-bound.md` [Community], `FerreroWashington`, the minus-part Euler-system bound,
   `LemmaK`, and Kato's Euler system all follow recognizable classical blueprints (`[FerreroWashington1979]`,
   `[Sinnott1984]`, `[MazurWiles1984]`, `[Soule1979]`, `[Kato2004]`). By contrast, `PropGK` (bounding
   Vandiver-parity `ShaPlus` lengths by an explicit $U(3)$ Frobenius-trace integer `NN`) and the
   residually reducible ordinary modularity-lifting step `PfromAut` (stated for general dimension $n$
   and residual pseudo-representations over CM fields, cf. `[Thorne2015]`) sit at or beyond the published
   literature frontier; human expert verification of `PatchingModules/` and `UnitaryThreeGroups/` is
   therefore one of the highest-value open digestion tasks (`[Open]`, Chapter 16).
2. **Why `KatoTwoW` needs no non-vanishing $L$-value hypothesis (`Zulip #642442900`):** On Lean Zulip,
   Ishaan Dixit (`#642435200`) asked how the archive's `KatoTwoW` could deduce Bloch–Kato Selmer vanishing
   without assuming $L(f \otimes \varphi, \cdot) \neq 0$; David Loeffler (`#642442900`) pointed out that
   in `KatoUse` (`src/Development/AutomorphicArithmetic/AutomorphicVarieties/UnitaryThreeGroups/KatoUse.lean:15–17`), twisting a weight-$(2e + 1)$ newform ($e \ge 1$) by $\chi_{\mathrm{cyc}}^{-e}$
   evaluates Kato's Euler system at $s = 2e + 1 - e = e + 1$ in classical normalization (analytic
   $s = 1$, strictly to the right of the critical center $s = 1/2$ and at the edge of Euler-product
   non-vanishing!), where $L$-values of $\mathrm{GL}_2$ cusp forms never vanish `[Kato2004]` [Community]
   (see **Chapter 18**, Lemma 18.2(1), for the line-by-line `R3` verification of `Published.Kato` and `2 * r ≠ k` `[Lean, read]`)!
3. **What happened to Behrend's set (`remark.pdf` p. 4, item 19 vs. `P12` and `HostOfField`):** In `remark.pdf`
   (p. 4, item 19 and fn. 7), Alpöge listed an October step tuning the choice of the Iwasawa tower via
   Behrend sets while noting *"This is the sort of trick that intuitively to me is
   obviously going to be unnecessary"* [Paper; remark p. 4]. Indeed, on the **P12 `VWin`** branch (`LUMI doc 06`
   [Community]), the Iwasawa root-avoidance window uses the elementary pigeonhole count `BConst.J`

   `Behrend.exists_large_sphere` (`Mathlib/Combinatorics/Additive/AP/Three/Behrend.lean:245`) IS
   load-bearing on the `src/Main.lean` spine** inside host-field thick-frame assembly
   (`src/Formalism/FrobeniusOrbits/FrobeniusOrbitsProofs/ThickExists.lean:181` $\to$

### 11.5.2 Masullo's counterpart

Masullo's draft *The Music of the Primes* has **no counterpart** to `VWin`, Iwasawa $\mathbb Z_\ell$-towers,
Ferrero–Washington, generalized Bernoulli numbers, Kato's or Kolyvagin–Rubin's Euler systems, $U(3)$
deformation rings, or Taylor–Wiles patching [Expository]. The only conceptual point of contact is
indirect: Alpöge motivated his Iwasawa $\mathbb Z_2$-tower (`remark.pdf` p. 4, item 11 [Paper; remark p. 4])
as a characteristic-zero analogue of passing to constant-field extensions $\mathbb F_{q^n} / \mathbb F_q$
in Stepanov's curve proof (Chapter 6, Step 2–3 of Theorem 6.3), whereas Masullo works directly on the
fixed number field's idele class group $C_F = \mathrm{GL}_1(\mathbb A_F)/F^\times$ without auxiliary
field towers (and explains in §14.2, pp. 534–536, why his Hardy–Sobolev trace formula fails over
$\mathbb F_q(X)$ [Paper; Masullo pp. 534–536]).

**Summary of the chapter (and of Part II).** For every cyclotomic CM host field $E$ cut out by
`IsChiField ψ (e x) q d E` ($q \equiv 3 \pmod 8$, $d \equiv 7 \pmod{16}$), `LengthEqAddEigen.lean`
(Proposition 11.1) decomposes the $\log \ell$-weighted Tate–Shafarevich sum across $1 \le e < \lfloor x^{401} \rfloor$
into $\ell = 2$, odd-$\ell$ `ShaMinus`, and odd-$\ell$ `ShaPlus` [Lean, read]. The archive bounds
$\ell = 2$ and finitely many bad primes by $\exp(x^{4\varepsilon/5})$ on a pigeonholed window $[Y, Y^2]$
using its Sinnott-route proof of Ferrero–Washington (`FW.ferreroWashington`, Proposition 11.3)
`[FerreroWashington1979]`, `[Sinnott1984]` and Soulé-type $K$-theory bounds (`LemmaK` `[Soule1979]`),
bounds `ShaMinus` polynomially via cyclotomic Euler systems (`ThmBcFree` `[MazurWiles1984]`, `[Rubin2000]`),
and bounds `ShaPlus` via a nonzero integer annihilator `NN` built from $U(3)$ / $\mathrm{GSp}_4$ Frobenius
trace congruences (`PropGK`), Kato's Euler system (`KatoUse` `[Kato2004]`), and Taylor–Wiles patching
(`PfromAut` `[TaylorWiles1995]`, `[Thorne2015]`). Because $\frac{\varepsilon}{4} < \frac{4\varepsilon}{5} < \varepsilon$,
`error_terms_le_sqrt_exp_rpow` and `vwin_of_counts` (Proposition 11.2) squeeze the combined bound below
$\sqrt{\exp(x^\varepsilon)} = \exp(x^\varepsilon / 2)$, discharging `HypV` in Chapter 10 and **completing
Part II**! Only the three foundational packages passed into `MainConclusion.lean` — automorphic Galois
representations `Row0b` and partial local Langlands `RecTExists` (Chapter 12), together with Green's
theorem and the literature layer `OpenLiteratureStatements` (Chapter 13) — remain to be opened in **Part III**.
